# FastShaders

Bi-directional TSL (Three.js Shading Language) visual shader editor. Users author and execute their own shader code inside the app, and `.fastshader` files can be shared between users — treat any loaded `.fastshader` or pasted shader source as adversarial input.

> **Before changing node visuals, the glyph system, `ShaderNode`, `NodePreviewCard`, or the Node Designer (`node-designer.html`), read `private/NODE_DESIGN_REQUIREMENTS.md` — the authoritative spec for node appearance/layout.** It is internal working material: `private/` is gitignored, so the file is not in a fresh clone and the path is deliberately not a link (it would 404 on GitHub). Elsewhere in this file and in source comments the spec is cited by bare name — that is the file.

## Stack

- React 18 + TypeScript + Vite (ES modules, base path `/FastShaders/`)
- `@xyflow/react` v12 — node graph editor
- `@monaco-editor/react` — code editor
- `zustand` v5 — state management
- `@babel/parser` + `@babel/traverse` — code→graph parsing
- `@dagrejs/dagre` — auto-layout (LR direction)
- `three` (WebGPU build) — shader runtime; FastShaders uses only `three/tsl`
  built-in functions including the MaterialX noise family (`mx_noise_*`,
  `mx_fractal_noise_*`, `mx_worley_noise_*`, `mx_cell_noise_float`)
- Path alias: `@/*` → `./src/*`

## Commands

- `npm run dev` — start dev server (port 5173)
- `npm run build` — typecheck + build (`tsc -b && vite build`)
- `npx tsc --noEmit` — typecheck only
- `npm test` — run the vitest suite once (CI-friendly)
- `npm run test:watch` — run vitest in watch mode
- `npm run tauri dev` / `npm run tauri build` — desktop (Tauri v2) shell; needs a local Rust toolchain. Releases are built in CI (no local Rust required)
- `cargo fmt --check && cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings && cargo test`, run inside `src-tauri/` — the Rust gate for any change to the desktop shell. The crate is fmt- and clippy-clean, so a failure is always the change under test. CI runs only `cargo test` (release.yml's desktop job, after the build), never clippy, so a new toolchain's lint cannot block a release. Two traps: `generate_context!` needs `../dist` and `../package.json` (a placeholder `dist/index.html` does), and tauri-build writes `src-tauri/gen/`, so a throwaway check is best run in a copy of `src-tauri/`

## Deployment

**When the user says "deploy" (or "deploy to all"), it ALWAYS means: commit the pending work, push, and ship to ALL THREE targets** — never stop after the release pipeline. They serve different audiences and drift apart silently, because each one is deployed by its own command and only the first is triggered by the tag: GitHub Pages is the public app, **alvismisjuns.lv/fastshaders/** is the author's site, and **fs.sferas.lv** is the STUDY host the eval arms are handed out from. Nothing reconciles them — a release that stops after step 1 leaves two hosts on an older build with no warning anywhere, which is how fs.sferas.lv sat two versions behind (measured 2026-09-09: Pages 0.3.31, sferas 0.3.28).

0. **Commit + push first**: commit whatever is pending in the working tree (and push the `a-frame-shaderloader` submodule first if it changed, then purge jsdelivr for every loader file that is new or changed — see the push+purge convention) — "deploy" is standing authorization to commit and push; don't ask again.
1. **Release pipeline** (web on GitHub Pages + desktop binaries, in lockstep): `npm version patch && git push --follow-tags`. The `v*` tag triggers `.github/workflows/release.yml` → tests, macOS `.dmg` + Windows installers, gh-pages deploy.
2. **alvismisjuns.lv**: `npm run deploy:alvismisjuns` — builds with `FS_BASE=/fastshaders/` + the alvismisjuns CSP origins and uploads `dist-alvismisjuns/` via psftp to `https://alvismisjuns.lv/fastshaders/` (credentials in the gitignored `.vscode/sftp.json`; needs `brew install putty`). `-- --no-build` re-uploads the existing snapshot. Run it from the SAME committed/tagged state as step 1 so both hosts serve the same version — it deploys the working tree, not the last tag. The script curl-verifies the deployed version meta at the end.
3. **fs.sferas.lv** (the STUDY host — `…/eval`, `…/evalpro`, `…/evalp` are handed to participants from here, so it is the one host where being behind changes what a study measures): `npm run deploy:sferas` — same shape as step 2 and the same gitignored `.vscode/sftp.json` credentials, with three differences the script's own header spells out: base is `/` (this host serves the app at its ROOT, not under a subpath), `FS_PREVIEW_ORIGIN` names this host so the sandboxed preview's opaque origin may fetch the built-in models, and the target is **`/var/www/fs/src`** — the docroot of the dedicated `fs` container, NEVER `/var/www/sferas/src`, which is a shared directory of a dozen unrelated projects. `-- --no-build` re-uploads the existing snapshot. It curl-verifies the served version meta AND that **`/evalp/` returns 200**, which is the check worth having: the study entries are DIRECTORY urls served by a static host, so a path or rewrite mistake shows up there while `index.html` still looks perfect.

## Testing

- Framework: **vitest** (configured in `vite.config.ts` so `@/*` alias + TS setup are inherited from the build config). Test files match `src/**/*.test.ts`; environment is `node` (no jsdom — tests target pure logic only).
- **The suite runs with `isolate: false`** (vite.config.ts), so every file in a worker SHARES that worker's module registry and globals. That halves the wall clock — a per-file worker re-imports the heavy engine graph (store → builtin textures/presets → codeToGraph → Babel) at ~350 ms a file — and it is what the costTable import-cycle flake below was really about. The contract a new test must honour is therefore NOT "no global mocking" (vite.config.ts's comment still says that, and it was never true of the whole suite): **every file that calls `vi.stubGlobal` (`grep -rl 'vi.stubGlobal(' src`) must restore it** (`src/stubGlobalRestore.test.ts` fails on one that does not) — `vi.unstubAllGlobals()` in an `afterEach`/`afterAll`, since a leaked stub lands on whatever file the worker runs next, and `unstubGlobals: true` in the vitest config would make that enforceable rather than aspirational. The second shared thing is the module registry: `registry/editorVisibility.test.ts` is the ONE file that calls `vi.doMock`/`vi.resetModules` (it has to — the shipped visibility JSON is EMPTY again since the Image node was unhidden on 2026-09-18, so every direct assertion would be vacuous), and a `resetModules` clears the registry the setting exists to share. Keep store-mutating suites self-resetting in `beforeEach`.
- Shared test factories live in `src/test-utils.ts` (`makeNode`, `makeEdge`). Always import these instead of redefining stub builders in each test file — the helpers cast through `unknown as AppNode/AppEdge` to skip React Flow's full Node generic constraints. It also holds the KTX2 harness: `installInProcessWorker()` runs three's blob Workers (KTX2Loader, DRACOLoader) in `node:vm` — it stubs `Worker`, so its caller must `vi.unstubAllGlobals()`, and `stubGlobalRestore.test.ts` holds callers to that — plus `fakeRenderer()` (r184's `hasFeature` throws until `init()` resolves) and `makeKtx2Glb()`. It holds the ONE glTF/GLB fixture set, too (Phase 5): `makeGlb(doc | { jsonText }, bin?)` (the research doc's section 11 recipe: JSON padded 0x20, BIN padded 0x00; `{ jsonText }` goes in verbatim for fuzzing), `TRIANGLE_POSITIONS` + `gltfPrimitiveDoc(extra)` (asset, buffer 0, view 0, accessor 0; `extra` keys REPLACE the base's), the header-only image fixtures `pngHeaderBytes`/`jpegHeaderBytes` (an `app1Bytes` run can push the SOF past a scan window)/`webpHeaderBytes` (`VP8 `/`VP8L`/`VP8X`)/`ktx2HeaderBytes`, and `makeRealPng(w, h, rgba)`, a real decodable PNG (zlib `deflateSync` + `crc32`). `makeKtx2Glb` and `gltfTestFixtures.packGlb` build their containers through `makeGlb`, so the tree has one GLB layout; `glbFixtures.test.ts` proves every builder writes what it claims. Use these rather than a local `glb()` helper (the two in previewMesh.test.ts and projectImportZipLimits.test.ts predate them). Three r184's KTX2 test textures are committed under `src/engine/fixtures/ktx2/` (MIT, never edit) beside two GLBs from `scripts/gen-ktx2-fixture-glbs.mjs`; `ktx2Transcode.test.ts` runs the real basis transcoder over them, not a stub.
- **Every shaderloader suite gets its loader from `src/shaderloaderHarness.ts`** (a non-test module, imported only by tests). `ACTIVE_LOADERS` is `['0.6', '0.8']`: 0.6 is read from the SUBMODULE (the frozen file exports fetch; `loaderAvailable` skips it on a non-recursive checkout), 0.8 from its served `public/js` copy (vendorSync pins it to the submodule). The weld, per-mesh dispatch, schema, mesh-edge and transform suites run the SAME assertions against both, through `evalLoader` (the vm sandbox 0.6's suites always built, plus options for `AFRAME.components` / `AFRAME.THREE` stubs; by default the AFRAME stub carries a FRESH inert `gltf-model` from `inertGltfModelEntry`, because 0.8 warns when it cannot hook one and its agent-wide `Symbol.for` flag would leave a stub shared between sandboxes hookable only once) and `transformsOf` (0.6's top-level functions, or 0.8's `FastShaders.transforms`). `PINS` holds the few source anchors that necessarily moved between 0.6's object-literal component and 0.8's core functions (the dispatch slice, syncWeld→unbary, weld-before-bary); every other pinned literal is spelled identically in both files by construction, so do not add a row for one — make the 0.8 spelling match instead. Never read a loader at a suite's module top level: a copy that stops being vendored makes that read THROW, not skip. `shaderloader08Core.test.ts` covers what only 0.8 has — the core without A-Frame, against real three/webgpu inside vm, including the first end-to-end run of the component through the injectable `FastShaders.fetch`/`.importSource` (the default Blob-URL import cannot run in vm and is only source-pinned; it needs a browser).
- Current coverage: utilities (`colorUtils`, `idGenerator`, `nameUtils`, `graphTraversal`, `csvParser`, `binaryCodec`, `dataNode`, `dataViz`, `imageNode`, `exposedPorts`, `uniformDefaults` — Set-as-default plan/apply — `zipReader` — a typed `ZipLimitError` per cap, the largest-legal-bundle round trip (`zipLargestBundle.test.ts`) and podest's cap mirror — `zipWriter`, `previewMesh` — custom-mesh detect/caps/sanitize — `previewMeshCache` — IndexedDB record codec + re-validation of cached bytes — `exportBundle` — Download-Shader js-vs-zip assembly — and `exportPreflight` — the N1 too-large-to-reopen decision) + engine (`topologicalSort`, `cpuEvaluator`, `graphToCode`, `codeToGraph`, `scriptToTSL`, `tslToShaderModule`, `tslToPreviewHTML`, `tslCodeProcessor`, `projectImportMesh` — zip model restore + stale-mesh clearing, `dataStripes`, `dataViz.node`, `imageNode`, `imageAssets` — placeholder/inline round-trip + comment sanitizing — `variadicArithmetic`, `outputPartSettings` — per-part Transparent/Side/Alpha clip/Depth write, `layoutEngine`, `propertyColor`, `parseCorpus`, `registryDrift`, `imageGraphByteStability` (image-graph emission pinned byte for byte, beside the built-ins'), `imageTextureShare` (one texture per shared payload, the double-march-scope regression), `reviewFixes` regression suite, plus a `graphToCode ↔ codeToGraph` round-trip invariant suite) + registry (`citations`, `descriptionSplice`, `designerEntry` — the Node Designer is a Vite entry, not a snapshot page — plus `glyphCoverage`) + node-designer data (`ndData` — the designable set derives from the live registry) + design helpers (`nodeArt` — draft-overrides-saved contract + the movable-art transform) + i18n (`i18n`) + node-editor interaction (`dragConnect`, `connectionReveal`, `overlapCascade`, `recentNodes`) + a shaderloader-transform guard (`shaderloaderTransform.test.ts`) + the trusted-side glTF reader (`glbContainer` container and strict base64 plus magic, `gltfReader` with every network and decoder global stubbed to throw, `gltfNaming` with a PARITY suite against the real GLTFLoader, `gltfStrip` — the texture-stripping writer, its output re-parsed by the real loader, plus the proof that a 70 MiB scan the model gate refuses becomes a preview mesh once stripped — `gltfSignatureParity`, the reader's signature and material indices against loader 0.8's parse record, and a seeded mutation fuzz `gltfReaderFuzz` over the reader and the writer) + edge geometry (`bezierGeometry` — control-point math, drop-on-edge distance, Catmull-Rom waypoint splines) + window seams (`seamLensGeometry` — the hover lens and junction ring path data — and `splitPane`, the source pins for the draggable-seam model that also fail if a retired grip name returns) + store (`historyCoalescing` — one undo entry per scrub — plus `graphPersistence`, `organizeSelection`, `deviceTextureCap` — headset `maxTextureDim` caps — and `drawings.store`) + drawings utils (`drawings` — stroke model + opacity quantization) + preview-link geometry (`previewLinkGeometry` — the symbolic Output→preview link curve math) + a vendor-sync drift guard (`vendorSync.test.ts`) + adversarial-input guards for the two payloads the ENGINE never reads and therefore nobody validated (`edgeExtras` — edge `data`/waypoints on both restore paths — and `safeJson` — the shared JSON.parse deny-list reviver, which also fails on a second declaration of itself anywhere in `src/`) + the GLB-restore guards (`projectImportGlb` — the real-store restore — `glbRestoreFlow`, `podestGlbReader` — podest's twin run against the editor reader — and `embeddedModuleNeverRuns` — no preview or XR document can run a model's module). **This paragraph is a highlight list, not an inventory** — `find src -name '*.test.ts'` is the source of truth, and it is the ONLY figure worth trusting: at the time of writing it returns 344 files against the ~60 suites named above, i.e. over four fifths of the suite is not mentioned here at all. Any count written into this paragraph is stale within a week — the previous pair (97 files, ~20 unnamed) survived until the suite had nearly doubled past it. The zustand store imports cleanly under the `node` env, so store logic is unit-testable without jsdom.

## Project Structure

```
src/
  App.tsx             — entry component, initial demo graph, SyncController; `seedFromStored` is the boot seed, which the desktop build runs AFTER its asynchronous autosave-file read, behind a boot overlay (see the desktop-autosave bullet under src-tauri)
  main.tsx            — React 18 root, CSS imports
  nodeEditor.tsx      — React root for node-editor.html (renders GraphsPage — node/texture overview + description/citation editor + the "In editor" visibility checkboxes)
  nodeEditorBootstrap.ts — MUST be nodeEditor.tsx's FIRST import: disables the store's graph autosave so this shared-origin page can't overwrite the user's real fs:graph
  vite-env.d.ts       — Vite env type declarations
  styles/
    tokens.css        — CSS design tokens (colors, spacing, typography, z-index)
    controls.css      — shared chrome primitives: the `.fs-seam` draggable-seam rules (cursor, the `--fs-seam-hit` hit zone, hover colour, the `fs-seam--dragging` capture state and the `.fs-seam-lens` hover bulge — shared by both SplitPane seams and the asset bar's top edge, each of which renders `<SeamLens>` as its last child; see the divider bullet) + the `.fs-dragging` app-wide selection-suppression state (set on <html> by utils/dragChrome.ts); loaded from main.tsx only
    reset.css         — CSS reset
  components/
    CodeEditor/       — Monaco editor panel (TSL / A-Frame / Three.js tabs, Apply [code→graph], Import). The A-Frame tab is ONE read-only file: a copy-ready, comment-free VR page that loads the exported module from beside it (`engine/tslToAFrameHTML.ts`). The Three.js tab is its plain-three sibling (`engine/tslToThreeHTML.ts`, three pinned by `THREE_VERSION`), which hands the module to loader 0.8's plain-three core (`FastShaders.use`/`load`/`apply`) instead of wiring it by hand — see the plain-Three.js convention
      tslLanguage.ts  — Monaco TSL type declarations + hex color picker
    Graphs/           — GraphsPage (node/texture overview for node-editor.html; the row's leading **In editor** checkbox is the unfinished-node switch — see the hiding convention) + GraphModal + DesignerModal
    Layout/           — Toolbar (**deliberately has NO `z-index`** — it is `position: relative` for its popovers' sake and nothing more. The app's top-plate shadow is drawn by `.app-layout__left::before` instead, i.e. by the pane that RECEIVES it; the toolbar briefly cast it itself, with `z-index: 1` to beat the later-sibling layout and the 3D preview opting out at `z-index: 2`, and that pair of z-indexes was a trap: a positioned element with a z-index is a STACKING CONTEXT, which sealed the toolbar's own popovers inside it. Both then broke in opposite directions — the About/Contact panel (z 100) fell BEHIND the canvas's NEW button (10), and the "Download app" dropdown vanished behind the preview entirely (measured: `elementFromPoint` at its centre returned the preview iframe). Scoping the shadow to the left pane needs no z-index, no opt-out to keep in sync, and cannot reach the preview by construction — the preview is the TOP pane of the OTHER column, so "the left column only" and "everywhere but the 3D view" are the same region. brand→contact popover, version, "Name:" input + EXPORT button [shared engine/exportShader download path, through the export pre-flight (N1, see that convention) — in a study session it opens EvalFinishModal BEFORE the pre-flight, so Submit never shows it; right-click/long-press opens the export-settings popover — include-the-mesh checkbox backed by the SESSION-ONLY store flag `exportIncludeMesh`; and the FORMAT choice — Shader file (.js/.zip) vs One .glb — backed by the SESSION-ONLY `exportAsGlb`. It is never read raw: `effectiveExportFormat` (utils/glbExportAvailability.ts) falls back to the bundle while the loaded model cannot be packed (a built-in shape, an OBJ, an external-data .gltf, an unreadable file — the reason is VISIBLE text under the disabled row, since WebKit drops a disabled control's title) and in a study session, whose popover renders no Format row — the popover IS reachable there by right-click, so the engine wrapper gates it again] + desktop-only WorkFolder control [see the src-tauri bullet], right cluster ↻refresh [right-click/long-press opens the Reload / Hard reload menu — see the hard-reload convention]/P(odest) [a new TAB on web; on desktop a button invoking `podest_open` for a real second app window — see the src-tauri bullet]/SC/LV/"Download app" + desktop-only "VR" LAN-bench popover + the app-wide sun/moon theme toggle. **That right cluster COLLAPSES into a ☰ menu on a narrow window** — measured, not a media query: what it needs differs between the web and desktop builds (SC + "Download app" vs VR) and between languages, so a breakpoint would be wrong for three of the four combinations. `toolbarOverflow.ts` owns the decision because the naive form cannot work — collapsing REMOVES the overflow that triggered it, so "collapse while it overflows" is an infinite flicker at ResizeObserver rate; the expand threshold is therefore captured at the instant of collapsing, while the expanded layout is still measurable, and only that width plus a slop re-expands. MEASURED: collapses at 1200px, exactly where the bar would otherwise overflow, and one collapse + one expansion across a slow drag through the threshold. The EVAL badge and the feedback "!" are deliberately NOT collapsible — one is visible by ethics posture, the other is the loud control someone reaches for when stuck (and the study's FINISH button). Below ~875px the bar still overflows, but on the CENTRE cluster (name field + EXPORT + Work folder + Palettes), which the menu cannot help with: the name input's `min-width: 200px` makes that cluster incompressible, and both obvious relaxations were tried and REVERTED — a `flex-basis` bought 25px (an `<input>`'s min-content contribution comes from its `size` attribute, so an explicit `min-width` never binds), and `min-width: 0` on the centre let its BOX collapse to 25px while its children spilled out and overlapped the right cluster, which as a side effect stopped the ☰ ever triggering), CostBar, SplitPane (**every window divider is a draggable SEAM, and the seam is the whole control** — 2026-09-16, owner request; the grip tabs with their diamonds are gone. Both SplitPane seams and the asset bar's top edge carry the shared `.fs-seam` rules (styles/controls.css): the line drags anywhere along its length through an invisible `--fs-seam-hit` zone (10px desktop / 28px coarse, centred on the 2px / 6px line — the sash convention every thin-splitter editor uses; on touch it is the only target a seam has), it is the keyboard target itself (role separator, tab stop, arrow keys), and on hover the SEAM LENS opens around the pointer — `components/Layout/SeamLens.tsx` over the pure, node-tested `seamLensGeometry.ts` (named so because a `seamLens.ts` beside `SeamLens.tsx` is the titleSplit/NodeTitle case-only filename trap): the line splits into two strokes that bulge to ±6px, enclose a `--bg-panel` gap and rejoin the single line TANGENTIALLY 43px either side (two cubics per side, `S` reflecting the control point, so the seam reads as having opened rather than as a shape dropped on it), with a 28px grip line down the middle of the gap — the friction mark (5/36/12 at first, ×1.2 the same day: owner, "make it +0.2 bigger"). It follows the pointer along the seam through ONE custom property (`--fs-lens-at`, written on the seam element only, per pointermove, drag or not — during a drag the seam moves under the pointer), opens as a scale ACROSS the seam from 0, is `pointer-events: none` so it cannot steal the press it invites, and is gated on `hover: hover` so a mobile engine's emulated `:hover` cannot strand it after a tap-drag. A captured drag sets `fs-seam--dragging` on the host, so the highlight and the lens never depend on how an engine reports `:hover` during capture. **A seam drag also takes every IFRAME out of hit-testing** (`:root.fs-dragging iframe { pointer-events: none }`, controls.css — the `fs-canvas-busy` fix for the same boundary): MEASURED, a rightward column-seam drag logged its pointerdown and then nothing, because the first move after the press was already over the preview iframe (2px past the seam), that document took it, and the parent's PENDING pointer capture never activated; the old 28px tab hid this because its first move stayed on the tab. `beginDragChrome` stamps the class synchronously in the press handler, which is what makes the rule land before that move. **Where two seams MEET is a JUNCTION, and a junction drags both** (owner, same day: "when on cross points make a circle with the stripe in middle and allow to drag both"). The column seam has two — the preview/code seam entering from the RIGHT and the asset bar's top edge entering from the LEFT (`JUNCTION_ZONE_PX` 12 along the seam; the nearer wins). On one, the lens becomes a RING: a 16px-radius circle (12 at first, ×1.3 by owner request) on the crossing with the same 18px stripe through it (the `fs-seam-lens__ring` group cross-fades in over the bulge under the host's `fs-seam--xy` — `transform-box: view-box` + a 50% origin, or an SVG group scales about the viewBox's corner), SNAPPED onto the crossing rather than under the pointer and riding a drag by the pointer's along-seam travel; the cursor is `move`; `data-fs-junction="row"|"bar"` lights the neighbour that will move (the row seam by a sibling selector in SplitPane.css, the bar's border-top through `:has()` in ContentBrowser.css, since the bar sits in the pane BEFORE the divider); and the press drags the column split PLUS that neighbour — the row ratio through `clampPreviewSplit`, the bar's height through `components/Layout/assetBarDrag.ts`, the ONE registered handle (`edgeY`/`height`/`push`/`commit`; ContentBrowser paints through its own `paintHeight`, bounds measured once per gesture, and commits at pointerup — SplitPane's pointerup, pointercancel and unmount all call `commit`, which is a no-op when nothing was pushed). Shift locks to an axis, touch starts axis-locked; everywhere else on the line moves that seam alone. The column seam is raised ONE z-index step (`calc(var(--z-controls) + 1)`) so it WINS the hit test at both crossings — the row seam comes later in DOM order and would otherwise own the very point the ring sits on. The preview/code seam drags on its own now, and both hands on it use `clampPreviewSplit`, whose floor is the store's `RIGHT_SPLIT_MIN` (one definition; `splitPane.test.ts` pins it). **With the tab gone, the push-the-other-divider subsystem went with it** (`utils/splitClearance.ts`, `utils/assetBarHeight.ts`, `--fs-asset-bar-h`, `--fs-grip-*`, ContentBrowser's `pushRowSeam`/`discreteStartCross`): it existed only because the tab hung ~24px into the asset bar's pane, and nothing reaches across a pane any more — the hit zone stops 4px in. The bar's wheel and arrow paths are plain `setBarHeight` updaters again (no store write inside them, so StrictMode's double call is harmless). The bar-junction drag is NOT that coupling returning: it moves the bar only while the user holds the crossing, never as a side effect of moving something else. `splitPane.test.ts` source-pins the model and fails on any of the retired names returning. **Several Output nodes wear a `_01` SUFFIX** (`outputOrdinals` in outputNodePlans.ts): one node is one material and the Output's header is the registry LABEL — not a ShaderNode's generated var name — so a GLB import was a column of cards all headed "Output". Numbered in EMIT order (the module's own; the nodes array is spliced by a drag-into-a-group and reordered by every Apply), silent on a single-Output document, two digits, and DISPLAY ONLY — it never reaches `data.label`, the code or anything saved, because the number describes a node's place in the CURRENT document. **The 3D preview is the TOP pane of the right column, the code editor the bottom one** — and the full-bleed 3D viewport must never touch a seam directly: `--edge-color` is a single mid grey (#999) shared by both seams AND the lens strokes so they read as one continuous line, while the scene background is user-pickable and defaults to #808080, so a seam laid straight against the render merges into it and the 3D view appears to spill over the editor. `.shader-preview__body` therefore carries a 1px `--border-subtle` hairline — a light line beside the dark seam, so at most ONE of the two can vanish into whatever colour the user picked, in either theme, incl. an exact #999999 (dropped in fullscreen, where there is no seam to separate from). It stays a HAIRLINE and not a wider chrome buffer because the seam has to read as the 3D viewport's own bottom edge: the breathing room belongs on the FAR side, as `.code-editor__tabbar`'s 4px `padding-top`, which is what keeps the Apply/Import buttons and the tab tops off the divider. `CROSS_MIN_PANE_PX` is measured against the resulting 37px bar (the Apply/Import buttons are the app-wide `--ctl-size` button height) — move them together. **Every window divider is ONE thickness and casts NO shadow.** The line reads `--fs-seam` (2px desktop, 6px coarse) — both SplitPane seams AND the asset bar's top edge, which carried its own hardcoded thickness until 2026-08-29, so the app drew two different dividers depending which you looked at; the token also gives the asset bar the coarse-pointer bump it never had, and the lens's split strokes are drawn at the same width so the opened line is the weight of the closed one. Dividers are drawn lines, not raised strips, and at a 2px seam a 2px shadow reads as a second, softer seam beside the real one; if one is ever wanted back it must be `filter: drop-shadow` on the DIVIDER, which casts the lens with it as one silhouette. NB `--fs-seam` is inside the bar's BORDER-BOX height, so `zoomForHeight`/`measureBounds` subtract it — measured as `rect.height − clientHeight`, not read from the token; leaving it in clipped the tallest tile by the seam width), AppLayout, PreviewLink + previewLinkGeometry + previewLinkHit (the decorative Output→preview wire, DOM-tracked per rAF, mounted inside ReactFlow at z-index -1; it draws **ONE WIRE PER CONTRIBUTING OUTPUT NODE** — `previewWireTargets` over `contributingOutputs`, the same set emission reads, so the canvas can neither draw a wire from a node the module ignores (a PARKED untargeted Output) nor miss one it emits; a DORMANT node is skipped, since it renders header-plus-chip and mounts no socket for a wire to anchor on, and a driving Raymarch Output collapses the set to itself. Each wire leaves that node's own decorative output SOCKET and ends on its own socket of the **RAIL** (`previewRailGeometry.ts` + `PreviewRail.tsx`, 2026-09-18): one per contributing Output down the canvas's RIGHT edge, placed by a FRACTION of the pane's height (the canvas is resizable from the seam and the window, so a fixed offset would hold at one size only) with half the disc clipped by `.node-editor__canvas` so it reads as meeting the seam. They used to converge on the preview's CENTRE, which looked like a connection and was not: the SVG lives inside `.react-flow` and that pane CLIPS it, so the curve was cut at the seam and never reached what it pointed at. Hover names the material with the wire's OWN wording (`linkLabelText`) and click glides to the Output, through a CustomEvent (`previewRailEvents.ts`) because the rail is chrome outside <ReactFlow> while `fitView` lives in NodeEditor. With NOTHING contributing it draws one HOLLOW socket that ADDS the first Output — through the store's own `addNode`, so one history entry and the reviewed node-add chokepoint, guarded against minting a second. The container is `pointer-events: none` with only the discs opting back in, or a full-height box down that edge would eat presses aimed at the graph. **CANVAS ONLY, deliberately**: a mirroring rail was built on the 3D preview's left edge and REMOVED the same day — the panes are different boxes (the canvas runs the column's full height; the preview body starts under its own control bar and is only the TOP pane of the right split), so the same fraction of each landed at visibly different heights, and holding them in step needs cross-pane measurement surviving split drags, a wrapping control bar, collapse and fullscreen. `previewWires.ts` is the ONE derivation the wire and the rail share, memoized on the store slices it reads. The anchor ELEMENTS are cached across frames, keyed on the contributing-id LIST and never a COUNT — two nodes swapping identity keeps the count equal while every cached element is the wrong node's — while the per-frame `getBoundingClientRect` reads ARE the tracking (pan, zoom, drag and resize all land there) and are never cached; the hot wire is tracked by ID for the same reason, since the live list is rebuilt every frame and an index would survive a change of wire set while naming something else. **Hovering a wire names what it shades and clicking it glides to its Output**, and neither can be done through the DOM — the load-bearing part, checked rather than assumed: `.preview-link` is a z-index -1 sibling of `.react-flow__renderer`, itself a stacking context (z-index 4) wrapping the hit-testable pane, so NO `pointer-events` value lets a decorative wire receive a pointer event without lifting it above every node card, where it would swallow presses aimed at the graph — and `isPointInStroke` is defeated by the wire's own `stroke-dasharray`, whose gaps the pointer falls straight through (`previewLinkWire.test.ts` pins that the stylesheet spells no `pointer-events` value but `none`, because a future reader's first instinct is to flip it). So the hit test is ARITHMETIC on the same control points the path is drawn from — `distanceToLink` over `linkControlPoints`, which `linkPath` also draws through, the `bezierGeometry` rule that what highlights is what answers — run from a CAPTURE-phase `pointermove` on the React Flow root (capture because any descendant's `stopPropagation`, which React Flow's own gesture code does, would otherwise hide the move that says the pointer LEFT a wire) and gated to `.react-flow__pane` targets, which is exactly the condition under which a press reaches `onPaneClick`, so hover and click can never disagree about what is hittable. `pickLinkAt` takes the NEAREST wire rather than the first within `LINK_HIT_RADIUS` (12 SCREEN px, undivided by zoom — the wire is drawn OUTSIDE the transformed viewport at a constant 4.5px), because every wire converges on one point and crossings are the norm. The label is its OWN element positioned imperatively, never a `title` on the `<path>`: TooltipLayer's `findHost` needs an HTMLElement, so a title on an SVG element silently never opens one. The click routes through React Flow's own `onPaneClick`, the one signal that already knows a marquee did not just end, no connection is in progress and the press landed on the pane rather than on a card — so a wire running under a node is correctly not clickable there. `previewLinkHit.ts` is the module singleton carrying the frame's endpoints to that handler: deliberately not store state, because which wire the pointer is near is not a fact about the graph and must never reach history, the autosave or a shared file)
    NodeEditor/       — React Flow graph editor (top-right chrome = the cost pill, whose TOTAL is a button that glides the canvas to the node where the points are SPENT — the DRIVING Raymarch Output when one drives, else the active sink, else the first CONTRIBUTING Output (`costFocusId` in outputFocus.ts; that last term matters because `activeSink` is NULL on a document with no UNTARGETED Output at all, which a GLB import does not produce (it keeps an untargeted default) but targeting every Output by hand does — aiming at the Output alone framed an ignored node, or a dead span on a graph like the black hole that has no plain Output) — so it carries the same "take me to it" glide as the Output's palette tile and Add-node row, through the same `focusNode` so all three land identically; it renders as an inert `<span>` when the graph has neither, the "never offer a control that silently does nothing" rule. **With SEVERAL sinks it CYCLES** (owner decision D4): the first press still lands on `costFocusId`, and every press after that walks `outputCycleOrder` — a stable TOTAL order over plain AND Raymarch Outputs (centre-y banded to 24px, then x, then id; absolute centres, so a grouped node sorts where it looks), never the nodes array, which `liftChildrenAfterParents` splices on an ordinary drag-into-a-group and `useSyncEngine` reorders on every Apply, so an array-order cycle would visit a different sequence after a gesture that changed nothing visible and could revisit one node while skipping another. A Raymarch Output is IN the order because `costFocusId` can resolve to one, and a cycle that cannot contain its own starting point is not a cycle. With exactly ONE sink it keeps the plain glide, since a second press that re-glides to where the view already is reads as a dead control. The cursor is a `useRef` holding the last-focused ID: `useState` would give the handler a new identity per press and re-render the memo'd CostBar for a value it does not display, and an INDEX into a list rebuilt at click time (nodes are added, deleted and dragged between presses) names an arbitrary node. The title says which it will do. The NEW button is parked at the top LEFT — `store.newGraph()`: one Output node, no edges, no ink, ONE undo entry; it snapshots inline rather than via pushHistory so a not-yet-cleared `isUndoRedo` can't swallow the entry and make the clear unrecoverable. It also resets `shaderName` to `DEFAULT_SHADER_NAME` and persists it, so NEW is indistinguishable from a fresh boot: the name is what every export path turns into a FILE name, and the desktop Work folder now adopts the opened file's name on load, so keeping it would aim the next Save at the shader just opened. That reset is deliberately NOT undoable — `shaderName` is absent from `HistoryEntry`, which is acceptable for a visible text box. **NEW also removes the imported preview MODEL** (2026-09-10, owner request) through `setPreviewMesh(null)` — store, mesh inventory AND the IndexedDB copy, so a reload does not bring it back, and ShaderPreview drops a `custom` geometry to the sphere. Not undoable either: the mesh is session-only by design (never in history — it can be tens of MB), so Cmd+Z after NEW restores the graph without the model, which is why `NewShaderModal` adds a line saying so whenever a model is loaded. It also dispatches **`fs:graph-new`**, which the Work folder needs and the reset cannot replace: a document already at the default name kebabs to the same file, so the tracked target would survive the rename test and the next Save would replace that shader with the blank document, silently. Its own event rather than `fs:graph-imported` — that one arms the import auto-fit, and `startNewShader` already schedules its own) + ContentBrowser (node palette + Presets + Textures + Saved Groups tabs; the ready-made-asset tabs Presets/Textures/Noise/DataViz lead the strip, building-block categories follow; the Textures and Distance fields tabs are OPTIONAL categories — off by default, switched on from the toolbar's right-click list, see the optional-categories convention) + DrawingLayer/DrawToolbar (freehand board-ink annotations)
      edges/          — TypedEdge (multi-channel, drag-to-disconnect, routing waypoints), EdgeInfoCard, bezierGeometry (shared render↔hit-test curve math)
      handles/        — TypedHandle with color-coded data types
      inputs/         — DragNumberInput (drag/click-to-edit number widget)
      menus/          — ContextMenu (dispatcher), AddNodeMenu, NodeSettingsMenu, ShaderSettingsMenu, GroupSettingsMenu, EdgeContextMenu, NoteSettingsMenu, StripesSettingsMenu, DataVizSettingsMenu, ConnectionStub (screen-space wire from the source handle to the AddNodeMenu on wire-drop-on-empty-canvas), menuShared (row styles + NumberRow + NodeActions), recentNodes, plus the four per-node menus the generic NodeSettingsMenu delegates to: ColormapSettingsMenu, NoiseNodeSettings (the signed/0–1 range flag), MicNodeSettings (the device picker), DataColumnStats (the wired Data column's statistics, shared by the Data Viz + Data Range menus), and ImageNodeSettings (the Image node's section) with its TexturePicker (the "Texture" row: an inline thumbnail grid of every image the project already holds, plus "From file…"; see the Image node bullet) and its ImageMappingSettings (the collapsed "glTF mapping" block: orientation, UV set, the normal-map green flip and the texture transform, read and written only through `utils/imageUvMapping.ts`)
      nodes/          — ShaderNode, OutputNode, RaymarchOutputNode (the marching output in the Output's chrome, driven by MARCH_NODE_CONFIG), ColorNode, PreviewNode, MathPreviewNode, ClockNode, SoundNode, GroupNode, NoteNode, NodeBase.css, RevealSockets + connectionReveal (drag-to-reveal hidden sockets), glyphs/ (NodeGlyph, customGlyphs); the Sound node splits its chrome out so the canvas node and its inert palette-tile twin cannot drift — SoundNodeButton (the arm light); SoundSourceSelect (the source `<select>`) lives in the settings menu; activeSinkSelector.ts is the store selector both output nodes ask "am I the active sink?" (single-entry memo on `(nodes, edges)` identity, over `getUnwrappedEdges` — every card asks, and the active-FLAG shortcut only fires once someone has clicked a preview socket, so an import-built document ran ~17 whole-node scans AND 17 edge-array allocations per store notify, i.e. per drag frame); **outputNodePlans.ts is THE cross-node plans, computed ONCE for the canvas** — `outputBindingsKey` (the cheap string, keyed on the nodes array then on each Output's `data`, so a drag frame and a colour scrub are both free), `outputPlansFor` (keyed on that string plus the `LoadedModel` IDENTITY, returning `contributing`/`named`/`index`/`coverage`/`defaultId`/`firstNamedId`/`moduleSignature`), `indexClaimLabels` (keyed on the plans plus the language, which must NOT enter the plans key), plus `defaultContributesOf` and `meshNamesOf`. They were per-instance `useMemo`s over the node SET, i.e. O(N²): ~17 cards each re-running the same six plans over the same 17 nodes, MEASURED at ≈0.8 ms per binding change and per mount on a 256-mesh model. It is a MODULE and not module scope inside the `.tsx` because the vitest env is `node`: a source pin is all a component can have, and a source pin is exactly what missed this — the per-instance form reads perfectly correct in isolation, so `outputNodePlans.test.ts` COUNTS the shared walk instead. Every memo is SINGLE-entry (`isolate: false` shares module state between test files) and nothing may read `values`/`exposedPorts`/`materialSettings` off `plans.contributing`, which holds the generation that last moved the key; sectionLabelText.ts is the one formatter of what an Output node shades (`formatSectionLabel` over `sectionLabel`, plus `indexChipTitle` and `linkLabelText`, the preview wire's hover chip — so the wire, the node's own picker and the settings menu's scope line all name a material the same way); IndexSectionChip.tsx is the read-only label an import-built INDEX-bound Output shows instead of a MeshTargetPicker
      nodes/NodeVisual.tsx — THE static ShaderNode replica, shared by every preview surface: asset cards (via NodePreviewCard), the node-editor overview (same cards), and the Node Designer's stage (via src/nodeDesigner/bridge). Props cover the designer's needs — draft-design override (replaces the saved CUSTOM_GLYPHS entry outright), per-socket preview states (live/range + channels → edge-value labels + the multi-channel stack), interactive (real widgets + data-port/data-nd-* hooks for the designer's delegated gestures). Mirrors ShaderNode's markup per NODE_DESIGN_REQUIREMENTS — change them together
      NodePreviewCard.tsx — content browser card renderer, ALSO the node-editor.html overview's Graph column. Dispatches on `getFlowNodeType`: the ShaderNode-rendered types (incl. `slider`) delegate to NodeVisual; `color`/`preview`/`mathPreview`/`clock`/`mic`/`output`/`raymarchOutput` have per-type content that must MIRROR its canvas component (see the one-node-one-look convention)
    PreviewRail.tsx + previewRailGeometry + previewRailEvents + previewWires — the wires' far END made visible:
      one socket per contributing Output down the canvas's right edge (see the PreviewLink bullet; a mirroring
      rail on the 3D preview was built and removed — the panes are different boxes and the same fraction of each
      does not line up). The geometry is pure and node-tested; `previewWires` is the ONE memoized derivation the
      wire and the rail share; the events carry the rail's focus/add requests to NodeEditor, which holds `fitView`
    AssetCostBadge.tsx  — the shared cost badge for asset tiles (TextureCard/PresetCard). Reuses the node cards' `.node-base__cost-badge`, so a bundle's price reads the same way a node's does — a coloured number above the card, not a caption inside it. Keeps the user's cost-gradient colour (NodeBase.css's contrast override is scoped to `.react-flow`; the asset bar is outside the canvas)
      SavedGroupCard.tsx  — draggable tile for a user-saved group (Saved Groups tab)
      TextureCard.tsx     — draggable tile for a built-in texture (Textures tab, CPU canvas preview)
      PresetCard.tsx      — draggable tile for a built-in preset (Presets tab; per-preset CPU shade functions render the thumbnails)
      tilePreview.ts      — shared tile-thumbnail plumbing (renderPixels loop + clamp01/lerp3/smoothstep) used by TextureCard + PresetCard
      AssetTooltip.tsx    — hover tooltip for content-browser tiles (portal, dwell delay, viewport-clamped)
      tileDrag.ts         — single placement path for palette tiles (tileActivationProps → fs-tile-drop → placeTilePayload, drop-on-edge splice)
      dragConnect.ts      — pure drag-node-onto-node connection decision logic (see the Drag-connect convention)
      overlapCascade.ts   — post-snap make-room BFS that pushes overlapped nodes off the connected pair
      keyboardNav.ts / useKeyboardNav.ts — canvas keyboard navigation: the PURE decision half (tab order, graph traversal, arrow direction, pane cycling) and the effect that binds it. Mostly TAMING React Flow v12's own a11y layer rather than building one — see the file header
      flowTypes.ts        — the ONE React Flow `nodeTypes`/`edgeTypes` registry, shared by the canvas and node-editor.html's GraphModal. MODULE SCOPE is load-bearing (React Flow memoizes on its identity), and a second copy is a second chance to forget a node type — which already happened once, to `mic`
      PreviewRoute.tsx    — Preview mode's straight route line (previewed socket → the Output's Color), DOM-tracked per rAF like PreviewLink but ABOVE the node layer; see the Preview-mode convention
    Modals/           — CsvImportModal (over-wide CSV decision), LimitModal (image/storage limit notices + ignore-limits opt-out; the COPY lives in the pure `limitNoticeCopy.ts`, which is where a new notice kind adds its `case` — `limitNoticeCopy.test.ts` renders every kind in EN and LV and fails `tsc` on a kind it does not list — and so does `utils/desktopAppNoteRules.ts`'s `LIFTS` table, where the kind decides whether the desktop app lifts it), FeedbackModal (the toolbar's red "!" — composes a plain-text report and hands it off; see the Feedback convention), NewShaderModal (the canvas NEW button's save-first confirmation → Export / Don't save / Cancel), ExportPreflightModal (+ `useExportPreflight`, one host per surface: the N1 "This export is too large to open again" pre-flight before EXPORT / NEW's save-first / the Work-folder Save → Export without the 3D model / Export anyway / Cancel), DesktopAppNote (the WEB build's pointer to the desktop app, mounted by LimitModal and ExportPreflightModal only when `desktopLiftsLimit` / `exportLiftedByDesktop` say the desktop room would have let the user through; it renders nothing inside the desktop app, nothing in a study session, and nothing on a platform with no build — iPad/iPadOS desktop mode, phones, Quest, Linux, ChromeOS — and its macOS first-launch sentence stays off until `MAC_FIRST_LAUNCH_ROUTE_VERIFIED`, which only a quarantined Apple Silicon download may flip; `desktopAppNote.test.ts` renders it under a stubbed `navigator`), GlbImportModal (the model-drop REPLACE gate — “Build a shader from this model's materials (replaces the current shader)” / Model only / **Restore the stored shader** (only for a FastShaders single-GLB export — the GLB-restore convention) / Cancel — carrying the N9 budget pre-check, the N11 material gate and the N12 memory estimate; never a “don't ask again”; see the GLB-import convention)
    Preview/          — ShaderPreview (iframe-based 3D preview via blob URL; top bar = labeled Light/Model/Subd controls + a right-hand toggle GROUP; the bar SCROLLS horizontally rather than wrapping or squeezing, since the pane is user-resizable well below what those controls need — same arrows and same wheel-to-horizontal mapping as the asset browser's two rows, through the same implementation (`components/Layout/ScrollArrows.tsx` owns the overflow detection and the markup, `.fs-scroll-arrow` in styles/controls.css owns the visual, and a surface supplies exactly one thing: `--fs-arrow-bg`, the colour of the strip the solid plate has to match). **This bar's arrows take the INVERTED variant** (`<ScrollArrow invert>` → `.fs-scroll-arrow--invert`: dark plate, light glyph, and the reverse in dark mode, expressed as the same two tokens swapped so it survives the theme toggle — the `.toolbar__refresh` precedent). The default plate-matches-the-strip look is right against the asset browser's busy tile strip and wrong here: this bar is a near-white chrome strip, so a plate in its own colour reads as part of the bar and the arrow stops looking like a button at all. MEASURED after the swap: 16.7:1 plate-against-bar in light, 9:1 in dark. Its HOVER glyph is a `#fff` LITERAL, not `--bg-panel` — `--border-focus` is a fixed blue in both themes so the thing on it must be fixed too, and `--bg-panel` is a dark grey in dark mode, measured at 1.66:1 against that blue (`.toolbar__refresh:hover` still has that bug). `scrollArrows.test.ts` pins which surface inverts, since that is a judgement invisible in either stylesheet alone. **The detection must watch the CHILDREN, not just the scroller** — overflow is `scrollWidth - clientWidth` and the two move independently: a pane resize changes the scroller's own box, a CONTENT change leaves it exactly as it was. Observing only the element (which is what this did, in the asset browser, from before the extraction) misses every content change and strands the arrow: MEASURED, filtering the asset browser to a single tile left › drawn over a strip with nothing to scroll, and switching the preview to an OBJ geometry — which removes the Subd slider — did the same to its control bar. Clicking either did nothing, which reads as a broken control rather than a stale one. So the ResizeObserver takes the children too (a relabelled language switch, a grown `<select>`, a filtered strip) and a MutationObserver on `childList` picks up ones mounted later, since the effect runs once; nothing unobserves a REMOVED child, because ResizeObserver holds its targets weakly. `scrollArrows.test.ts` source-pins all of it — the vitest env is `node`, so there is no DOM to drive the hook against (`.shader-preview__ctl-right`) holding WGSL/GLSL + FPS + Uniforms — the auto margin lives on the group, since two `margin-left:auto` siblings divide the slack instead of stacking and the pair would drift apart. **WGSL/GLSL** forces the sandboxed preview onto the WebGL2 backend (what the immersive popup and Safari always run), so backend-dependent shader behavior is checkable without a headset: the SESSION-ONLY store flag `previewForceWebGL2` (the `exportIncludeMesh` rule — a persisted force would leave the preview silently degraded weeks later) feeds `PreviewOptions.forceWebGL2`, and since the backend is decided at document boot the flag joins the `previewHtml` memo deps — toggling is an iframe rebuild, not a hot-update. The emitted force is a SEPARATE branch ahead of `__fsForceWebGL2`, never a clause inside it: `feedbackReport.test.ts` extracts that function's source verbatim and evaluates it against `previewBackend()`, so a flag reference inside would break the drift guard (`previewBackend` instead takes the flag as its own parameter and reports `forced — preview GLSL toggle` BELOW the platform reasons, which force regardless). The button's label is the backend the CURRENT document reported — `boot()` posts `fs:backend` (sandbox-only; the XR popup is a top-level page whose parent is itself), last-known-wins across rebuilds so the label doesn't flicker per edit on Safari — and active styling marks only the FORCED state, so auto-GLSL reads as a fact, not a setting. **The report drives the LABEL only, never the lock**: the sandboxed document runs the loaded shader (adversarial), so a forged `fs:backend` could otherwise pin the toggle inert with a false "WebGPU is not available" tooltip — and even the honest report mislocks, since a FORCED document accurately reports `webgl2` and that stale value would lock the button for the whole rebuild window after unforcing. The locked state (`AUTO_IS_WEBGL2`, module-scope) therefore derives from PARENT-observable facts — no `navigator.gpu`, or `platformWebGL2Reason` exported from `utils/feedbackReport` (the SAME statement of the platform rule the drift test covers, not a third copy). When auto mode already yields WebGL2 the button LOCKS instead of hiding — `aria-disabled` + explanatory tooltip (the WorkFolder rule: a truly `disabled` control drops its native `title` in WebKit), because a control that vanishes per-browser reads as a regression — and it carries a STABLE `aria-label` ("Force the WebGL2 (GLSL) backend"): the visible WGSL/GLSL text flips with state, and a flipping accessible name beside `aria-pressed` reads as a contradiction in a screen reader. **FPS** draws `.shader-preview__stats` over the viewport's top-RIGHT corner: the stage measures (the `fs-stats` A-Frame component, `fs:stats-on` down / `fs:stats {fps,ms}` up — the SAME contract podest uses between its own stage and panel) and the parent only paints, imperatively through a ref because reports land ~4x/s and React would re-render this whole panel at that rate. Silent until asked, so a preview with it off carries no traffic; re-armed on `fs:preview-ready` because every shader edit swaps srcDoc and the fresh component boots OFF (without the replay it dies on the first edit, which reads as a broken toggle). The number is the presented frame PERIOD — it includes vsync, so it reads the display's refresh rate until the shader genuinely misses frames. It shares the corner with the Uniforms overlay, which drops to `top: 36px` under `.shader-preview__body--stats` — the fixed-height chip yields to the taller interactive panel rather than the two fighting over z-index. `previewStats.test.ts` executes the component against a stub AFRAME (gating, the fps↔ms agreement, the 2 s hidden-tab gap drop, and that the XR popup gets the head-locked panel INSTEAD — a top-level document has no parent to report to); the bottom row is ONE flex bar (`.shader-preview__bottom-bar`, `pointer-events: none` with its children opting back in, so an orbit drag aimed at a gap still reaches the iframe) holding — LEFT = play/pause, bg swatch, red ✕ Reset; then the model-animation pill + its scrubber, both present only while the loaded model carries a clip (see the model-animation convention); RIGHT = VR popup + ⛶ fullscreen [exit glyph ⤡, never ✕], the "escape this pane" pair kept away from the everyday controls; Uniforms overlay incl. "Set as default" → utils/uniformDefaults)
    — **the Uniforms overlay is the ONE place uniforms are tuned live.** The panel is height-capped to the 3D pane and SCROLLS internally (`max-height: calc(100% - 65px)` + `overflow-y: auto` — 100% is `.shader-preview__body`, the 65 = its 6px top offset + 59px reserved for the bottom bar's row [10px inset + a MEASURED 43px bar], which matters because the panel is z-index 5 over the bar's 2, so an overlap occludes the pills; the stats variant re-derives 95 for its 36px top), because a long property list otherwise grew straight past the pane. It lists only CONNECTED properties (`utils/connectedUniforms`, pure + tested), i.e. those that REACH the Output: a property feeding a dead-end branch still emits, but scrubbing it would do nothing. **A property whose output feeds NOTHING no longer emits at all** — `graphToCode`'s `isOrphanedProperty` skips both its `uniform(…)` line and its import, so it reaches no schema key, no `<a-entity shader="…">` attribute and no `params.x` on the exported module. That gate is the WEAKER "has an outgoing edge" test rather than this module's "reaches the Output", because codegen also runs on graphs with no Output node at all (every built-in preset and texture, and every saved group), where the stronger rule would strip every property they own — `orphanedUniforms.test.ts` pins both halves. Because such a property is then absent from the generated code, `codeToGraph` cannot rebuild it, so **`useSyncEngine` carries orphaned property nodes across a resync** the way it already carries groups and waypoints (precise about WHY it is missing: no consumer on the UNWRAPPED edges, and absent from the parse) — without that, an Apply silently deleted the property node you had just dropped and not yet wired. Names are matched by the EMITTED var name (`nodeVarNames`), never the stored one — two properties may share a name (same preset dropped twice) and codegen uniquifies the later one (`colorA` → `colorA2`), which the stored-name match silently dropped from the overlay; the stored name is the fallback that covers a code-panel Apply, where fresh node ids miss every `nodeVarNames` lookup. Live scrubbing stays overlay-local (writing back would tear down the iframe); **"Set as default" is the write-back**, baking values into the property nodes → the graph→code sync then updates the code and the export. The Output node's settings menu deliberately has NO uniforms list — it duplicated the node's own editor, and showed float properties but not colour ones. Tuning still persists, but it no longer wins SILENTLY: a row whose stored value differs from the authored default shows a `graph <n> ↺` chip carrying the GRAPH's number beside the row's own value (the PREVIEW's), so each surface displays the one the other is hiding, and clicking it adopts the graph value; the collapsed Uniforms button carries a dot so a shut overlay can't hide an override, and "Set as default" highlights with a count. All three are drawn in `--text-primary` rather than a fixed literal — the uniform panel flips to a dark surface in the dark theme, where black type and a black border would be invisible; the token is #1A1A1A in light and #eceef0 in dark, so the controls read as black where the user sees black AND sit a full step darker than the `--text-secondary` rows around them. The override bookkeeping runs on the CONNECTION-AGNOSTIC uniform list (`nonMicUniforms`) even though the rows don't: a value tuned on a property that is wired but filtered out of the visible rows still counts as an override rather than lurking unflagged. Its scope narrowed when orphaned properties stopped emitting — a property feeding NOTHING is absent from the code and so cannot be seen here at all — and that is self-correcting rather than a hole: an unemitted uniform is also never pushed at `fs:preview-ready`, so its stale value is inert for exactly as long as it is invisible, and the badge appears the moment the wire lands and the uniform returns. That list also backs the `fs:preview-ready` push's kind guard, which was built from connected rows only and therefore short-circuited for every other name (a leftover `'#rrggbb'` could be posted at a float uniform). Slider fallback bounds are kept only while they still CONTAIN the value the row displays (`fallbackBounds`) — the ↺ revert, Reset and a node edit all move that value without touching the authored default, and a stale seed would print the true number against a track that cannot reach it, so the first touch would clamp the number away and store the clamp. That is containment, NOT "re-seed when the authored default moves": a value the slider itself emitted is inside its own range by construction, so a drag can never re-seed and the exponential compounding `seedBounds` warns about stays impossible. Explicit user bounds are never auto-widened. "Set as default" deliberately does NOT clear anything — the bake moves the authored default, so the redundant entry stops being an override by derivation; clearing in the handler would sit below `if (plan.size === 0) return`, paint an intermediate render at a third value, and make undo of the bake lose the tuning it just baked.
    — the Sound node’s live capture is driven from here: `useSoundPump` (rAF loop → `fs:uniform`, targets read from the generated code) + `SoundControl` (arm/disarm + level meter, rendered ONLY while the shader reads a `sound<n>_<channel>` uniform; it carries NO source picker — that lives in the node's settings menu). See the Sound-node convention.
    Modals/GlbExportModal.tsx — the single-GLB export's dialog + `useGlbExportUi` (building after 300 ms with Cancel, N1-GLB too-large, failed, and the fresh-click "ready" step); `useExportPreflight` composes it, so each surface still mounts ONE `modal`
    Preview/useGlbImport.tsx — the GLB import flow `loadMeshFile` hands an offerable .glb/.gltf to (read on the trusted side → facts → plan → dialog → build → `commitGlbImport` → one import-note report); it holds the reader's transient report for exactly as long as the dialog is open
    Preview/subdivisionSteps.ts — the subdivision slider's POWER-OF-TWO stops: the slider's value is the INDEX of a stop, so every notch is one doubling and one notch of travel can never cost a 25 ms re-tessellation
    Tooltip/          — TooltipLayer (app-wide tooltip portal, mounted in AppLayout)
  engine/
    graphToCode.ts    — graph → TSL code generation (topological sort, import collection)
    codeToGraph.ts    — TSL code → graph parsing (Babel, node matching, pattern detection)
    tslCodeProcessor.ts — shared TSL processing (import extraction, TDZ fix, body parsing — `parseBody` counts brace depth on the MASKED body and only a depth-0 `return` is the shader's, see the imperative-code convention; `extractFnBody` looks for `const shader = Fn(() => {` FIRST and only falls back to the first `Fn(() => {` — a zero-parameter helper Fn declared above the shader (`rayDirection`) was otherwise taken for the shader, and the whole module collapsed to `colorNode: normalize(sub(positionWorld, cameraPosition))`: the ray direction painted as colour, measured 2026-09-03)
    partKeyLiteral.ts — a leaf (its one import, the JSON reviver, is itself a leaf) shared by graphToCode, tslCodeProcessor and the LAZY scriptToTSL chunk: `moduleStringLiteral`, THE encoder of every string key and signature name written into generated code (JSON.stringify + the star-slash, `<` and U+2028/U+2029 escapes, the last two because the module parse matches the return object one LINE at a time — graphToCode aliases it as `partKeyLiteral`), `partEntryColon`, `partEntryKey`, and `stripMirrorParts` (rule R6)
    moduleHelpers.ts  — the ONE table of module-scope helper `Fn`s that are not `three/tsl` exports (`hsl`, `toHsl`, `rayDirection`, the distance-field family), keyed by helper NAME: an entry may be a VARIANT of a def (`alias: { type, ports?, values? }` — a mode's helper, or a width-dispatched one), `HELPER_ALIASES` maps every variant AND the legacy names of folded nodes back to their def, `helperNameFor`/`modeOf`/`helperCallPorts` are what graphToCode and the settings menu ask; graphToCode emits the helpers actually CALLED (by callee name, table order) + force-imports their `imports`, codeToGraph skips the declarators by `MODULE_HELPER_NAMES`; `moduleHelpers.test.ts` Babel-parses every body so a forgotten import fails
    tslToPreviewHTML.ts — TSL → standalone HTML preview (A-Frame + shaderloader 0.8)
    teapotData.ts       — the Utah Teapot's 28 bicubic Bézier patches (1976 Blinn version) + the official page's bijective-atlas rectangle per patch, RECOVERED by least squares from the official generator's own output (provenance, method, residuals and the Utah terms in the file header). Newell's rationals to 5 dp — not float noise
    teapotGeometry.ts   — `TEAPOT_SCRIPT`: the runtime tessellator + the `teapot-mesh` A-Frame component, one script string evaluated by the preview iframe, by node tests (`loadTeapot()`) and by scripts/gen-teapot-obj.mjs; `SUBDIVISION_CAP`-bound resolution; fixtures/utahTeapot1976Res4.json is the official generator's res-4 mesh the test proves equality against
    tslToShaderModule.ts — TSL → shaderloader-compatible ES module (property schema)
    topologicalSort.ts — Kahn's algorithm (warns on cycles)
    layoutEngine.ts   — Dagre auto-layout (+ the unmeasured-node footprint estimate; its imageNode branch counts the thumbnail or empty slot plus one row per output, the opt-in params sharing those rows — only a sixth exposed param, `dir`, adds one). Its OPTIONAL 5th argument `rankOrder` (id → key) overrides the order within each rank and switches the spine float OFF, for a caller that knows what the vertical order MEANS — dagre does not, since every wire into a node is the same wire to it; absent, the layout is exactly what it always was. Today's only caller is the GLB section builder
    cpuEvaluator.ts   — CPU-side recursive graph evaluation (live previews, cost)
    evaluateTSLScript.ts — detects `model.material.*Node = ...` direct-assignment style
    scriptToTSL.ts    — reverse of tslToShaderModule (converts .js script back to Fn-wrapped TSL)
    projectImport.ts  — shared import path for every surface (Load Script, code-panel drop, canvas drop, preview drop, desktop Work folder): applyProjectToStore + importShaderText + importShaderZip + importShaderGlb (the shader stored inside a FastShaders single-GLB export, restored only from the GLB import dialog; see the GLB-restore convention). Emits TWO events: `fs:project-imported` (prefs re-read — ShaderPreview + usePersistedState) and `fs:graph-imported` (the graph was REPLACED → NodeEditor frames it, see the auto-fit convention; the desktop WorkFolder is the SECOND listener and uses it for the opposite purpose — dropping the work-folder file it was tracking, since the document on screen is no longer that file). `importShaderZip` rethrows the reader's `ZipLimitError` (plus its own pre-read gate) and throws `ZipModelSkippedError` for a model-only zip whose model is refused; a corrupt archive still resolves null. `reportZipImportError(e, fileName)` is the ONE catch-side mapping, and all five surfaces call it before their own error text. `commitGlbImport(project, mesh)` (GLB Phase 5) is the ONE commit path for an import-built shader: the texture-stripped mesh FIRST (importShaderZip's order; the geometry no longer DEPENDS on it — `shownGeometry` derives the fallback from the live mesh — but a commit that sets the model before the project keeps the prefs re-read describing the document it is about), then the module-private `applyProjectToStore` with `preview.geometry` forced to 'custom' — one undo entry, the same restore-path sanitizers as any shared file, `fs:project-imported` then `fs:graph-imported`, each ONCE (applyProjectToStore already announces; a second announce would arm the auto-fit twice)
    gltfImport.ts     — THE GLB IMPORT COMPOSER (GLB Phase 5 Step 8; no UI entry until Step 9's dialog): `buildGlbImport(report, fileName, opts)` runs `planBuiltMaterials` → `encodeRequests` + `encodeGltfImages` (serial) → `buildGltfSectionGraph` → `planTextureStrip` + `stripGltfTextures` → `createPreviewMesh` over the stripped copy, which MUST pass before anything is assembled (a 'kept'-mode copy may still be over 64 MiB) → the project (the model's sanitised stem as its name, `preview.geometry` forced to 'custom' at commit) and ONE `GlbImportReport`. It never writes the store, and every failure is a RESULT (`aborted` / `mesh-refused` / `failed`). It takes the dropped BYTES beside the reader's transient report, because a null strip plan (nothing to strip, or the writer's walk budget exceeded) keeps them as the mesh
    gltfSectionBuilder.ts — PURE, node-tested: the reader's report + the encoded images → ONE Output NODE per built material, each bound to its glTF material INDEX (ids `gi_output_m<gltfIndex>`, with an untargeted empty DEFAULT at `emitOrder: 0` beside them; glTF material 0 is a node like the rest), ONE Texture node per fan-out key (`gltfTextureNodeKey`: image | sampler | colour space | `gltfTextureValues` incl. the green flip | `gltfSamplerValues` — the dialog's texture facts use the same function), a factor beside a texture as a Color/Float × node and a factor alone as a STORED channel value only when it differs from the Output's own default (roughness 1, metalness 0, white), COLOR_0 × Vertex Color (and its alpha, a Split's `w`, × a non-opaque material's opacity), BLEND → `transparent` + `depthWrite: false`, MASK → `alphaTest` ≤ 0.99, doubleSided → `side: 'double'`, emissive strength a Float ×, `modelMeshes` from the certain single-material names; deterministic ids (the snapshots rest on it), then `autoLayout` under `feederOrder` — every feeder takes the key of the EARLIEST Output socket it reaches (the target node's `emitRank`, then the channel's own ROW), so the graph is read top to bottom in the order of the sockets the wires land on; dagre's own order stacked them Roughness, Color, Metalness, Normal, Emissive against rows reading Color, Emissive, Roughness, Metalness, Normal (owner, 2026-09-18). That first term is `emitRank` and NOT the old per-section index, which collapses to 0 under bare handles — nothing fails if it does, the materials' feeders simply interleave by CHANNEL again, reproducing the exact tangle this function was written to fix (the negative control reports `[2,1,2,1,2,2,1]`). A node feeding two materials (a shared ORM texture) belongs to the first, which is the one place it can be, so its second wire still crosses. Only the graph-STRUCTURE snapshot moved when the builder went per-node; every emitted-code snapshot is untouched. Occlusion, `normalTexture.scale`, clearcoat and the other PBR extensions are REPORTED (`notImported`, the closed `GltfFeatureId` vocabulary), never imported
    gltfImportFixtures.ts — the builder suites' fixtures, a non-test module imported only by tests (the shaderloaderHarness.ts precedent): the Blender-, scan- and AI-shaped GLBs and a deterministic FAKE `encodeImageFile`, so the builder's decisions run under node with no canvas
    fastShadersProject.ts — FASTSHADERS_PROJECT_V1 editor-state snapshot (node positions, groups, preview/UI prefs) embedded in / extracted from the downloaded `.js`; `imageRefs` (additive, node id → payload key) is resolved in `applyProjectToStore`, never by `extractProjectState`
    projectImageRefs.ts — the `imageRefs` contract (key = utils/imagePayloadRefs' `imageRefFor` over the canonical `src`): `moduleImageLiterals`, the reader `resolveProjectImageRefs` (every import, on the shared `RefBudget`), `splitImageLosses` and the owner-gated writer `referenceImagesInModule` behind `EXPORT_IMAGE_REFS = false`. It must not import imageAssets, which reaches the store
    imageAssets.ts    — image payloads ride generated code as short `fs-asset:<node>-<hash>` PLACEHOLDERS; `inlineImageAssets`/`inlineImageAssetsFromNodes` expand them back to the real `data:` URL at the surfaces that run or export the module as a standalone document (the XR popup, the A-Frame and Three.js tabs, Download Shader); the SANDBOXED preview no longer inlines — its document resolves the placeholders itself from the asset feed (`previewAssetFeed.ts`, GLB Phase 6 S3), and `IMAGE_PLACEHOLDER_RE` is the ONE exported copy of the placeholder regex that feed and Phase 7's asset table iterate. Also owns the LRU decode+re-encode memo graphToCode used to hold, and the payload→digest memo in front of it (`utils/digestMemo.ts`), so a graph→code pass that changed no payload runs no FNV round. `imageAssetCounters`/`resetImageAssetCounters` are test hooks (the reset empties BOTH memos, since `isolate: false` shares them across files)
    previewAssetFeed.ts — the sandboxed preview's IMAGE-ASSET FEED (GLB Phase 6 S3): `planPreviewAssetFeed` (pure — which placeholder keys the module references, present in the asset map, not yet sent to THIS document), `PREVIEW_ASSETS_MESSAGE` (`fs:image-assets`) and `PREVIEW_ASSET_RESOLVER_SCRIPT`, the in-document resolver that maps each key to a `blob:` URL once and rewrites the placeholders right before a module Blob is minted (the boot blob and every hot swap). Payloads cross into the preview ONCE per key per document instead of riding every `fs:shader`; the module text stays placeholder-only on the parent side. See the preview-rebuild convention
    imageTexturePlan.ts — `createImageTexturePlanner`, the ONE decision of which Image nodes share their module-scope `new Image()` (one per exact canonical payload) and `THREE.Texture` (payload × `readImageTextureSpec`); owner = the first node emitted, plus the two setup-line builders that are the only place an image's `THREE.Texture` is constructed. See the Image node bullet
    exportShader.ts   — shared export assembly, read imperatively from the store: `buildShaderBundle(opts?)` (module generation + project embed + exportBundle) is the ONE bundle builder — the toolbar EXPORT download, the NEW-shader save-first path, the desktop Work-folder Save and the study package all emit byte-identical bundles through it (`opts.includeMesh` overrides the session `exportIncludeMesh` flag for ONE build and is only ever passed `false`, by the pre-flight). `buildShaderBundleChecked(ask)` is the pre-flight wrapper the three USER surfaces go through; the study package (SusModal) calls `buildShaderBundle()` directly and never pauses on a dialog. `buildShaderExportChecked(asks)` is now the ONE wrapper those three surfaces call: for the bundle format it delegates to `buildShaderBundleChecked`, and for one `.glb` it runs `prepareSingleGlb` (engine/exportSingleGlb.ts, loaded by a dynamic `import()` so the repacker stays off the boot path) behind the GlbExportModal — under `isEvalMode()` it never reaches that path. `downloadShader(bundle?: ShaderExport)` is the blob/anchor browser-download tail and the `evalLog('export')` chokepoint, and it posts what the delivery has to say after the click (`announceExportDelivered`: a `.glb`'s report lines plus N1's desktop-only line, in ONE note, since `showImportNote` replaces)
  eval/               — the user-study mode (`…/eval`, `…/evalpro`, `…/evalp`): `evalMode` (the sessionStorage arm, sampled once at module init), `EvalGate` + `ConsentModal` + `DataDisclosureModal` (consent BEFORE any logging), `telemetry` + `telemetryModel` (the closed event vocabulary and the derived summary), `evalTask` + `evalContext` (which task, which price table, which device), `background` + `proQuestions` + `SusModal` + `susScore` (the questionnaire), `EvalFinishModal`, `previewShot`, `evalPackage` (the zip) and `evalUpload`. See the Eval-mode convention for what each one is FOR — this tree entry is only the map
  hooks/
    useSyncEngine.ts  — bidirectional graph↔code sync, undo/redo, complexity calc
    useLongPress.ts   — sustained touch/pen press → callback (touch context menus; mouse ignored)
    usePersistedState.ts — useState mirrored to localStorage (validate-on-seed, throw-safe)
    useDismiss.ts     — while open, close on Escape or a click outside every exempt ref; shared by every toolbar popover (contact, Download app, VR, export settings, work folder)
    useFitText.ts     — shrink a label until it fits its parent box (fixed-size nodes: the colour swatch). Wrapping is the CSS half; this writes font-size IMPERATIVELY (state would re-render → re-measure → loop), re-fits on document.fonts.ready (the woff2 subset swaps in after first layout), and no-ops on a 0-sized parent
  i18n/
    index.ts          — t()/node-label lookup: display-only EN/LV overlay, every key falls back to English
    lv.json           — Latvian descriptions, socket labels, and UI chrome (React-only)
    node-i18n.json    — SINGLE SOURCE for node + category labels; every consumer (incl. the Node Designer, via src/nodeDesigner/bridge) imports it through src/i18n — the old public/node-i18n.json copy + fs-i18n-sync plugin + i18nSync drift test are gone
  nodeDesigner/       — the Node Designer's module (node-designer.html at the repo root is a real Vite ENTRY — rollupOptions.input; there is no public/ copy and no sync plugin anymore):
    main.ts           — entry; imports nodeEditorBootstrap FIRST (same fs:graph-autosave trap as nodeEditor.tsx — DragNumberInput brackets history via store actions), then the app fonts/tokens/RF styles, then designerApp
    bridge.tsx        — the narrow React seam: renderNodePreview (mounts NodeVisual with the DRAFT design + preview states; onRendered → overlay re-measure), builtinGlyphSvg (renderToStaticMarkup over NodeGlyph's renderArt — the REAL art, replacing the hand-copied string table), label helpers over src/i18n, savedDesignsBaseline (live CUSTOM_GLYPHS as the read baseline when /__nd and the FS-Access folder are absent)
    ndData.ts         — pure data layer (node-env tested): designerNodes() derives the designable set from getAllDefinitions()+getFlowNodeType==='shader' — the drift class designerCoverage.test.ts used to police is structurally dead — plus live costs/colours
    glyphSource.ts    — PURE, node-tested: the tolerant markup tokenizer behind the source↔canvas link (element byte ranges, `tagsAlign` as the "does the DOM agree" backstop) + `formatGlyphSource`, the one-element-per-line formatter
    glyphPath.ts      — PURE, node-tested: the SVG path-data model (tokenizer, relative-command rebasing, serializer) MOVED out of designerApp so the "add a point" gesture can share it instead of copying it, plus `insertIntoPath`/`insertIntoPoly` (de Casteljau, so the drawn curve is bit-identical after a subdivision)
    glyphGeometry.ts  — PURE, node-tested: rotation about a pivot, `rotateTransform`, Bézier evaluate/split, nearest-point, RDP simplification + Catmull-Rom for the freehand tool, `penPathData`
    glyphPaint.ts     — PURE, node-tested: `GLYPH_PALETTE` (the eight NodeGlyph.tsx design tokens) + the value handling behind the fill/stroke bar (`rgb()`→hex, the leading-dot `.8` spelling an `<input type=number>` refuses, mixed-state folding)
    designerApp.ts    — the ported vanilla app (@ts-nocheck): inspector, glyph SVG point-editor modal, stash/dirty model, save paths (/__nd → FS Access API → download; customGlyphs.ts format unchanged). Stage gestures bind by DELEGATION on the React-rendered replica (data-port/data-io on handles, data-nd-glyph/data-nd-art on art) and only ever edit the model + re-render — never the React DOM
  registry/
    nodeRegistry.ts   — 98 hardcoded node definitions (22 input [incl. `imageNode` — the Image (Texture) node, category `input` since GLB Phase 4 so the optional Textures switch cannot gate it, addable EMPTY from the palette, and OFFERED there since 2026-09-18 (it shipped hidden through editorVisibility.json while unfinished — see the Texture-node bullet), `rayDirection` — the world-space view ray as a module helper, `property_color`, the range-configurable `slider`, `normalWorld` — see the world-normal convention, `vertexColor` — the model's OWN painted colours, which every built-in shape lacks (it renders plain white, silently) and a dropped `.obj` loses, so it is only ever meaningful on a dropped `.glb` — and `soundNode`, the live-audio **Sound** node, see the Sound-node convention], 8 type [incl. the `hsl`/`toHsl` color conversions — the standalone `color` category was retired], 4 arithmetic, 15 math, 4 interpolation, 3 logic, 7 vector, 19 sdf [primitives `sdCircle`, `sdBox` (2D/3D by input width), `sdTorus`, `sdCylinder`, `sdCapsule`, `sdCone`, `sdPlane`, `sdOctahedron`, `sdStar`; operators `sdCombine` (union/subtract/intersect/xor by MODE), `sdfTransform`, `sdfRepeat`, `sdfRepeatPolar`, `sdfMirror`, `sdfModify` (round/shell/scale), `sdfDeform` (twist/bend/elongate), `sdfExtrude`, `sdfRevolve`, `sdfMask` — see the distance-field and helper-variant conventions], 8 noise, 6 dataviz [`stripes`, `dataviz`, `colormap`, `dataRange`, `isolines`, `wireframe` — see the scientific-colormap convention], 2 output [`output` and the marching `raymarchOutput` — see the Raymarch Output convention.
```
