It moved OUT of `sdf` on 2026-09-09: a sink belongs with the sinks, and Distance fields is an OPTIONAL category that is off by default, which left the only marching output unreachable until you found the switch that reveals it — and on 2026-09-16 it became that family's COMPANION (`OPTIONAL_CATEGORY_COMPANIONS` in registry/optionalCategories.ts, with `rayDirection`): it lives in `output`, but while Distance fields is switched off it is withheld from every add surface with the family, because a whole family hidden with one door left open — the second tile on the Output tab, the one hit for "sd"/"distance"/"march" — is what the owner reported as the search revealing a disabled group's nodes. Both Add-node surfaces skip the `output` category wholesale — the Output row is hardcoded — so each renders the category's OTHER defs beside that row itself, in the browse list AND in the flat keyboard list; `outputFocus.test.ts` pins both, since a sink missing from the menu is findable only by typing a name you would have to already know]) + hidden `unknown` and `dataNode` (CSV drop) defs, both excluded from allDefinitions (`imageNode` joined the `definitions` literal in GLB Phase 4 — INSIDE the literal, because the Node Designer's label/description splice locators scan only it). NB: the 9 unary-math nodes are generated via a `.map()`, so a naive grep of `category: 'math'` undercounts. The vector `append` is `variadic` (grows sockets like the arithmetic folds, emitted via `buildAppendConstructor`) — and it is the ONE GLYPHLESS node in the operator/list layout, so that branch is gated on `usesOperatorLayout(def)` (`hasNodeGlyph(type) || growsOperands(def)`, NodeGlyph.tsx) and never on the glyph alone. Gating it on the glyph is what made append render two sockets FOREVER while every layer beneath the renderer — `effectiveInputs`, `pickSpliceInputPort`, `normalizeChainOperands`, `resolveArguments`, `cpuEvaluator` — had already been built and tested for growth: append is deliberately glyphless (`glyphCoverage.test.ts`'s exempt list — its socket set is its whole meaning), so it fell into the ROWS layout, the one branch that never calls `effectiveInputs`. Four gates must stay on that one predicate: `ShaderNode`, `NodeVisual` (the one-node-one-look replica), `layoutEngine`'s footprint estimate, and the Node Designer's `layoutIsOp()` (which mirrors it over its own draft glyph, via the `grows` flag `ndData` puts on every node) — `operatorLayoutGate.test.ts` greps all four. **Its cap is CHANNELS, not sockets** (`utils/appendCapacity.ts`): `maxOperands: 4` bounds the socket COUNT, which is the right number only when every operand is a scalar — one wired vec3 already spends three of the four channels, so the trailing grow socket is withheld rather than offering a wire that `buildAppendConstructor` then silently trims (measured: `append(vec3 -> a, float -> b, float -> c)` emitted `vec4(vec31, float1)` and the `c` wire simply vanished). An ALREADY-WIRED over-budget socket keeps its handle — unmounting a handle an edge points at is the worse failure — and the emitter's trim stays as the adversarial-input backstop. Operand WIDTHS come from the per-handle `shapeOfEdgeSource` on BOTH sides — codegen's `appendOperandChannels` and `cpuEvaluator`'s `computeShape` append branch, which ask the one shared `portShapeForHandle` — never a node-level count: the argument text beside them is built by the handle-aware `resolveEdgeRef`, so a node-level count desynchronised the two for every source whose non-head output narrows (`toHsl`'s h/s/l, `dataviz`'s `value`). It emitted `vec4(toHsl1.x, float1)` — a four-slot constructor handed two components, i.e. a module that does not compile — and wiring all three of h/s/l gave `vec4(toHsl1.x, toHsl1.y.x)`, silently dropping the Lightness wire and putting `.x` on a float; the evaluator meanwhile told the CARD 4 channels for the same graph, so the two surfaces disagreed about one wire. It is `variadic` and deliberately NOT `chainable`: a constructor is not a fold, so no N−1 pricing and no `chainIdentity` (an unwired operand emits the bare `0`)
```
    nodeCategories.ts — 14 category definitions (input, type, arithmetic, math, interpolation, logic, vector, sdf, noise, dataviz, texture, presets [backed by builtinPresets.ts — no node defs of its own], unknown, output)
    builtinTextures.ts — 8 built-in texture groups (polka dots, grid, tiger fur, static noise, crumpled fabric, gas giant, marble, wood) — TSL code parsed to node graphs at startup
    builtinPresets.ts — 24 built-in preset groups (Presets tab), three tiers ordered easy→advanced: primitives (gradient, stripes, checker, **circle**/distance-field, edge-glow/fresnel, pulse, uv-scroll), composites (color-ramp/cosine-palette, **hue-shift**/hsl-round-trip, noise-mask, **mosaic**/cell-noise-id, toon-ramp, vertex-wave, **noise-blob**/vertex-noise-displacement, top-cover, **distance-fog**/camera-depth), effect recipes (**iridescence**/fresnel-palette, dissolve, **lava-crust**/ridged-noise, hologram, **teleport-beam**/scanline-cutout, force-field, stylized-water, **studio-shine**/matcap-basis). Four of the nine added 2026-08-25 carry a lesson nothing else did: `hue-shift` is the FIRST shipped asset that compiles the hand-emitted `hsl`/`toHsl` helper Fns (see their convention — nothing else exercises that emitter, so it is the one preset whose backends are worth re-checking after a codegen change); `teleport-beam` is the only preset that reaches the Output node's **Discard** channel or the `logic` category, and its mask is a `lessThan` rather than a ramp precisely because Discard is a truthiness test; `mosaic` exists to price the Voronoi family out loud — `mx_cell_noise_float` is 7 points because it returns one flat value per cell and no distance field, where `mx_worley_noise_vec2` is 235 (a worley 'cracked cells' preset was authored, measured at 256 points against the Quest 3 CostBar's 200, and CUT — Stylized Water's ranked-sine fake is the library's F2−F1 lesson and costs 49); and `studio-shine` is the matcap Presets deliberately dropped in 2026-08-01, rebuilt out of two `cross` products and three `dot`s — a camera-aligned basis is what a matcap's UV lookup really is, so the absent `normalView` node turns out not to be a blocker. `distance-fog` measures depth RELATIVE to the model centre (distance(camera, fragment) − |camera|) rather than absolutely: the preview camera sits at z=8 with a 1.6-unit mesh, so absolute distances would leave the fog range the moment anyone zoomed, and its defaults (fogNear −0.8 = the nose, fogFar 0 = the centre plane) span the VISIBLE hemisphere rather than the whole sphere. A screen-space `vignette` was also authored and cut: on a centred, rotationally-symmetric sphere under an origin-targeted orbit camera, a screenUV radial mask is geometrically the same picture as the shipped fresnel, and its graph was six of eight nodes identical to `circle`'s. Same TSL→group pipeline as textures; every snippet is fully flattened, tunable params are NAMED uniforms (they surface in the preview's Uniforms overlay), and builtinPresets.test.ts asserts each parses with zero errors/zero unknown nodes. Fresnel presets use dot(**normalWorld**, normalize(cameraPosition − positionWorld)) — normalWorld is fed BARE, since `transformDirection` already normalizes it and `presetCost.test.ts` fails on a `normalize(normalWorld)` as 7 wasted points; see the world-normal convention below; there is no normalView in the registry vocabulary — `studio-shine` shows the workaround where one is really wanted
    codeGroupBuilder.ts — shared buildCodeGroup(entry, idPrefix): TSL code → codeToGraph → output-node filter → autoLayout → auto-expose → group wrap; the ONE implementation behind builtinTextures + builtinPresets. An entry may carry an optional `note` (heading + text) that ships as a real NoteNode member in a cleared band ABOVE the graph — presets all have one, explaining the NODE STRUCTURE (the card blurb covers the look). The band shifts the graph down without re-laying it out, the note is width-capped so a wide graph doesn't stretch it into an empty ribbon, and it is inert everywhere (`codeGroupBuilder.test.ts` pins the geometry + byte-identical codegen; the asset tile's node count and the size sort both exclude it)
    builtinPalettes.ts — the shipped starter palettes, same standing as builtinTextures/builtinPresets: read-only content in code, NEVER copied into a shader on first run (a fresh shader's saved payload stays empty, and editing this list reaches every existing project)
    colormapData.ts   — GENERATED colormap catalogue (14 maps as sRGB hex anchors + provenance/licence); regenerate with `node scripts/gen-colormaps.mjs`
    colormaps.ts      — pure colormap layer: sample, bake the 256-texel linear-light GPU LUT, build the CSS gradient the card/picker draw. The Colormap node deliberately has NO glyph — the ramp IS its art: the canvas node draws `.shader-node__colormap-strip` in ShaderNode and every preview surface (asset card, node-editor overview, the Node Designer's stage) gets the same strip through the ONE shared replica (NodeVisual), so no surface can go blank or drift again. The designer's dx/dy/scale apply to the strip via `nodeArtStyle` (movable node art — CSS-px transform, purely visual); `glyphCoverage.test.ts` pins the exemption that makes those px units unambiguous
    complexity.json   — per-node GPU cost values. **The noise family is MEASURED (Quest 3, 2026-07-23); the texture-sampling family is not** — the bench has had texture atoms since 2026-09, but no committed run has priced one yet (the Quest 3 protocol and its acceptance gates are `ShaderCarousel/benchData/METHODS.md` §6), so `imageNode`/`dataNode`/`colormap`/`stripes`/`dataviz` are authored. That is the class METHODS.md names as bandwidth-bound and therefore NOT additive, and it is how the Image node came to sit at **2** — below `colormap` (4), which samples a ~1 KB LUT, while an Image node samples a mipmapped texture of up to ~16 MB. Repriced to **10** on 2026-09-09 (owner: "recalculate the image nodes"): the texture-unit throughput the table already prices at 4 — corroborated independently at ~3.6 pts for one trilinear fetch over the 2064x2208 reference at ~30 GTexel/s — plus a comparable term for the DRAM traffic a multi-megabyte texture pulls and a LUT does not. The honest band is ~4 (cache-resident) to ~26 (incoherent, DRAM-bound); 10 is the conservative middle, and underpricing is the dangerous direction, since four image samples reading as 8 points is a budget that cannot warn. `textureSampleCost.test.ts` pins the ORDERING (image > LUT > ALU, under Perlin's MicroPlane-measured 27) rather than the value, but only while the value is authored: the bench now HAS texture atoms (`buildBenchRegistry(TSL, THREE)`, see the ShaderCarousel bullet), and the test's gate flips on its own, by file CONTENT, once a raw run carrying them is committed under `benchData/` — from then on the meta must name that run and `imageNode`/`colormap` must sit within 10 % of what `benchData/fit-core.mjs` derives from it, the Static run preferred (`pickShippingRun`). So such a run is committed only TOGETHER with that repricing; on its own it turns `npm test` red. **Since 2026-09-10 that entry is the ANCHOR of the Image node's price, not the price** (`imageNodeCost` in utils/nodeCost.ts): it is charged in full at a 2048 px geometric-mean side and above — the size the authored 10 was derived FOR ("up to ~16 MB"), so no existing image reprices upward — and DISCOUNTED log-linearly to ×0.5 at 256 px and below, so the settings menu's Resolution ladder steps 2048/1024/512/256 → 10/8/7/5 and is consequential. Four rules, each the residue of an audit that overturned the first cut. It is a MULTIPLIER on `getCost('imageNode')`, never a literal: `imageNode` is a real key a hand-authored profile can retype (`blankProfileCosts` seeds the whole table), and a curve built on literals left that edit counted by the CostBar badge and charged by nothing — both branches, sized and dimension-less, anchor on the same entry. It does NOT re-purpose the meta's "~4 … ~26" band: that band sweeps CACHE BEHAVIOUR for one fetch (texture-unit throughput vs. every tap missing) and is size-free at both ends — mapping 256 px → 4 and 2048 px → 26 onto it priced a small image at exactly colormap's 4 (the very ordering `textureSampleCost.test.ts` guards) and every 2048² image at 13 % of the Quest 3 budget, and the shipping cap IS 2048 (Quest 3's `maxTextureDim`; `MAX_IMAGE_DIM` 1024 is only `encodeImageFile`'s default parameter, which neither drop path uses). The dimension gate is STRICT — a real positive integer ≤ 8192, `decodeImageNode`'s own rule — because `values.width`/`height` come out of a `.fastshader` and NO restore path validates them (`sanitizeImageNodes` inspects only the payload, the provenance keys and the glTF mapping keys): `Number(null)`/`Number('')`/`Number([])` are 0 and `Number(true)` is 1, so a coerce-then-isFinite gate lands junk on the FLOOR — a 50 % silent underprice — and `Math.max(0, NaN)` is NaN, so a clamp alone lets `sqrt(-1 × 4)` reach the badge as `#NaNNaNNaN` and the CostBar as `NaN / 200` (junk pays the flat entry instead). And the result is ROUNDED: every surface renders the number raw, and 18.666666666666664 across a 47 px card was measured. The shape between the two anchors is a judgement between physical bounds, stated in the function's comment: compulsory coherent traffic is linear in AREA (a shallower discount than this at the small end) while incoherent access makes size matter more, and log-linear sits between on the side that prices small textures higher — underpricing being the dangerous direction. The coherence axis itself (the `data` colour space turns mipmaps off — the every-tap-misses case) is the meta's real lever and is NOT priced yet. `utils/imageNodeCost.test.ts` pins all of it over `nodeCostPoints`, not the JSON: anchor, monotone ladder, integer, floor > colormap/sin/dataNode, a 20-value junk sweep → flat, and an override moving the whole curve. `PRESERVED_KEYS.imageNode` carries `width`/`height` now too — a Reset used to drop them, which rendered the image black AND would have priced it at the flat fallback, silently, from one right-click — and the eight glTF mapping keys (`utils/imageUvMapping.ts`), which say how the picture lands on the model it came with and cannot be rebuilt without that file
    citations.ts / citations.json — sparse per-node/texture academic provenance references (ref + optional DOI/url)
    legacyNodeTypes.ts — registry types FOLDED into a mode/width node (sdBox2/sdBox3 → sdBox, smoothUnion/sdSubtract → sdCombine): `migrateLegacyNodeTypes` on every restore path; the folded nodes' old CALL names parse back through `HELPER_ALIASES`
    editorVisibility.ts / editorVisibility.json — the "unfinished node" switch (see the convention below); the JSON lists what is HIDDEN, the module is the only place that reads it
    optionalCategories.ts — the palette's OPTIONAL categories (Textures, Distance fields): off by default, switched on per browser from the toolbar's right-click list; the SECOND add-surface layer above editorVisibility (see the optional-categories convention)
    glyphCoverage.test.ts — guards that every ShaderNode-rendered node HAS a glyph. A node added without one renders as a bare titled box and is indistinguishable from its neighbours on the canvas, in the content browser and in the node-editor overview — which is how a batch of recently-added nodes (the dataviz family, normalWorld, the normal/tangent pair, hsl/toHsl, half the position family) ended up looking unfinished. The exempt list is deliberate-only (float/int/vec* are plain number rows per NODE_DESIGN_REQUIREMENTS #15; colormap draws its real ramp instead; hsl/toHsl are glyphless by owner decision 2026-08-13 — their h/s/l rows/sockets are the identity) and a second assertion fails on STALE exemptions, so an entry cannot quietly excuse a future gap. It also checks each custom SVG is tag-balanced, carries no <svg> wrapper and references no external URL — the art is injected with dangerouslySetInnerHTML, so a stray tag corrupts the surrounding DOM rather than merely looking wrong.
    descriptionSplice.ts — byte-range splice of a `description` string literal in a registry source file (node-editor.html description editor; avoids @babel/generator reprinting the hand-formatted file)
  store/
    useAppStore.ts    — zustand store (graph, sync, history, UI, groups + saved-group library, preview/canvas/code-editor prefs, UI language, board drawings [ride history by reference and share the fs:graph autosave payload], per-headset texture caps, session-only previewMesh [custom 3D preview model — never history/autosave/embed], localStorage persistence). `loadGraph`/`loadSavedGroupsReport` are read + parse + `parseStoredGraph`/`parseStoredGroupsReport`, split so the desktop file store runs the same sanitisers (both reports carry `strippedImages` AND `outputSectionsTrimmed` — `StoredGraph`/`StoredGroupsReport` — so the desktop boot's documents count the same two things); `graphPayload` is the ONE `fs:graph` document shape both writers build; `installDesktopAutosave` routes both slots to the file store (writes gated on `graphPersistence` for BOTH there) and `flushPendingGraphSave` is the close/quit flush
    desktopAutosaveBoot.ts — desktop-only chunk (App.tsx's dynamic `import()` behind `__FS_DESKTOP__`): reads both autosave files before the seed, installs the runtime with writes OFF, and answers Rust's close/quit flush (`registerDesktopCloseFlush`, in every desktop session). See the desktop-autosave bullet under src-tauri
  types/
    node.types.ts     — AppNode union, ShaderNodeData, OutputNodeData, GroupNodeData, BoundarySocket, MaterialSettings, helpers
    tsl.types.ts      — ParseError (with severity), GeneratedCode
    sync.types.ts     — SyncSource ('graph' | 'code')
    index.ts          — barrel re-exports
  utils/
    idGenerator.ts    — generateId(), generateEdgeId() (4-part deterministic format)
    colorUtils.ts     — hex/RGB conversion, cost color gradient, type/category colors, getContrastColor() for auto-contrast text
    noisePreview.ts   — CPU Perlin/fBm/Cell/Voronoi noise rendering for thumbnails
    waveform.ts       — pure sin/cos waveform model (geometry + labels) behind nodes/WaveformSvg.tsx, the ONE renderer for the live Math Preview node and its asset-card twin. Replaced the old canvas repaint (`mathPreview.ts`, gone) — see the CPU-backed-canvas convention: vector content inside the React Flow viewport must be SVG
    clockFace.ts      — the same for the Time node's face (ClockFaceSvg live, phase 0 on the tile)
    graphTraversal.ts — hasTimeUpstream() BFS graph walker
    exposedPorts.ts   — single home for the opt-in parameter-socket (`exposedPorts`) rules shared by every surface (render, settings menus, connect/sync/import auto-expose); `effectiveNodeDef` (ramp filter, then `effectiveImageDef`) is THE def ShaderNode and NodeVisual both draw from
    chainOperands.ts  — compacts operand slots of grown variadic nodes after a disconnect (rewrites edges + remaps stored values; position is semantic for sub/div)
    costTable.ts      — the cost TABLE: authored prices (complexity.json), the measured override layered over them, and `sanitizeCostMap`. **A LEAF — it must import nothing but the JSON**, and that is load-bearing rather than tidiness: `useAppStore` calls `sanitizeCostMap` + `setCostOverrides` at MODULE SCOPE (so the first CostBar/badge reflects the selected device), while `nodeCost` sits in a real import cycle back through the store (`nodeCost → outputMaterials → exposedPorts → edgeUtils → useAppStore`). A cycle is harmless until something EVALUATES across it during initialisation — those two calls did, so whichever module the entry point reached first was re-entered mid-body and threw a TDZ ReferenceError (`overrides` / `OUTPUT_DEFAULT_EXPOSED` / `saveTimer` / `setState` of a half-built store), which MEASURED as 5–11 of the then-152 vitest files failing DIFFERENTLY every run — `vite.config.ts` sets `isolate: false`, so module instances are shared across a worker's suites and the file→worker assignment decided the race. `release.yml` runs `npm test` before building binaries, so it could fail a release for no reason. A leaf can never be caught mid-init (nothing to suspend on), so the cycle still exists but is now benign. `costTable.test.ts` pins both ways to undo it: an import here, or the store's two module-scope calls pointing back at `nodeCost`
    nodeCost.ts       — GPU cost READERS that need the graph: points per node instance (variadic op scales base × (N−1); else flat registry cost) + `computeReachableCost`. Re-exports the whole costTable surface, so every consumer keeps one import site for "costs"
    csvParser.ts      — strict adversarial-input CSV parser for the Data node (finite-only cells, bounded counts, column-major output)
    dataNode.ts       — build/decode the dataNode payload (numeric columns as a base64 Float32 blob; names are UI-only port labels)
    dataViz.ts        — pure DSP helpers turning a data column into Stripes lookup textures (cumulative phase ramp for tear-free density) + `columnStats`/`planNormalize` (the Data Range node's domain decision, made on the CPU where the whole column is visible)
    drawings.ts       — board-ink stroke model + sanitizeDrawings caps (adversarial localStorage/import); opacity quantized so same-opacity strokes don't compound (SVG group-opacity isolation), 6-digit hex only
    propertyConvert.ts — float/colour ↔ named-uniform property conversion (Node Settings "Convert to …"): in-place node rebuild carrying value/hex across, auto property naming
    uniformDefaults.ts — pure plan/apply for the Uniforms overlay's "Set as default" (bakes tuned preview values back into property_float/property_color nodes as authored defaults; varNames MUST come from a fresh graphToCode run — store.nodeVarNames is stale after a code-panel Apply)
    binaryCodec.ts    — binary ↔ base64 helpers for embedding numeric arrays into generated modules (pure; same encode host / decode iframe)
    edgeUtils.ts      — removeEdgesForPort(), unwrapCollapsedGroupEdges() (rewrites visual boundary edges back to real endpoints for engine consumers)
    edgeDisconnectFlag.ts — transient flag for edge disconnect suppression
    dragChrome.ts     — document-level chrome for a pointer drag (seams, and the canvas's connection drags): sets `.fs-dragging` on <html> and pins the cursor, which a `null` cursor skips for a gesture that owns none (a connect: React Flow owns the pointer's look there). beginDragChrome returns THIS gesture's idempotent end call (safe from pointerup, pointercancel, AND unmount) and the state is refcounted, so one multi-touch finger lifting can't strip the chrome from under the other seam's still-live drag
    nameUtils.ts      — toKebabCase() for export filenames
    newNodeValues.ts  — initial `values` for a USER-added node (the ONE shared path for the palette-tile drop + AddNodeMenu): property auto-naming + a hex from the "Vibrant Color Fiesta" palette (`NEW_COLOR_PALETTE`) for `color`/`property_color`, preferring one not already on the canvas. Also `GROUP_COLOR_PALETTE`/`randomGroupColor` — the same five hues DIMMED, for a group the user is creating right now (see the Groups convention). Deliberately NOT used by code→graph, project import, or built-in textures — those carry authored values
    imageNode.ts      — Image node payload: caps/validation (whitelisted data: URL, decodeImageNode, sanitizeImageNodes, collectImageFiles — one entry per distinct image) + `formatImageBudget` (a chars cap as the "KB" a notice prints) — pure, node-testable
    imageChannels.ts  — the Texture (Image) node's CHANNEL-socket table (`alpha`/`r`/`g`/`b` → swizzle + rgba index, `isImageChannelHandle`): a Map-only LEAF that imports nothing, read by cpuEvaluator's `handleChannels`, graphToCode and NodeEditor; the registry's Image output ids are pinned to its order
    imagePayloadRefs.ts — the ONE storage de-duplication for image payloads in `fs:graph` and `fs:savedGroups`: a node whose payload an EARLIER node of the same document already carries is written `imageB64: ''` + `imageRef: 'img1-<fnv>-<len36>'` (`imagePayloadsForStorage` / `libraryPayloadsForStorage`) and resolved back on every restore path (`resolveImageRefs`, before `sanitizeImageNodes`); see the payload-refs convention
    payloadDigest.ts  — `fnv1a32Hex` / `fnv1a32Bytes`, the ONE FNV-1a copy (moved verbatim out of `imageAssets.ts`, so the `fs-asset:` placeholders are byte-identical). A 32-bit BUCKET key, never an identity — a colliding pair of real equal-length payloads is pinned in its test — so anything treating two payloads as the same confirms by content. A leaf; `imageOriginCache.ts`'s seeded 128-bit hash is a different function
    imageTextureSpec.ts — `readImageTextureSpec` / `imageTextureSpecKey`, the ONE normaliser of an Image node's TEXTURE-OBJECT settings (colour space, Nearest, Repeat, and `Texture.flipY` — true, or false under the glTF orientation, the ONE glTF mapping key that lands on the Texture object — NOT the Flip Y checkbox, which is a uv mirror). graphToCode's image branch reads through it via the texture planner (`engine/imageTexturePlan.ts`, which re-exports it), byte-for-byte its old inline reads (junk-swept in the test). It is the texture-sharing key, so a new setting that lands on the Texture OBJECT must JOIN it. A leaf
    formatSize.ts     — `formatMiB(bytes, lang, rounding = 'up')`, the ONE MiB formatter every size notice prints through: MB means MiB, at most one decimal, Latvian decimal comma, never grouped. **Which rounding follows from what the number IS, and the two are opposites**: a MEASURED size takes 'up' (the default), so one byte over a cap never prints EQUAL to it (64 MiB + 1 B → 64.1); a declared CAP takes 'nearest', because rounding a cap up claims more room than is enforced. Both appear in one sentence ("{size} MB — max {limit} MB"), so getting them the same way round makes the pair contradict itself at the boundary. Every cap shipped today is a whole number of MiB, so the two agree and no string moves — it matters the first time one is not, and `exportPreflight.test.ts` pins the cap side. WorkFolder.tsx's local `formatSize` is unrelated
    fillTemplate.ts   — `fillTemplate(template, values)`, the ONE way a translated sentence gets two or more `{placeholders}` filled: a SINGLE pass over the template (own-property lookups, unknown keys left literal, values inserted verbatim). Chained first-occurrence `.replace('{name}', …).replace('{limit}', …)` let a file name spelling a LATER placeholder capture it, and the real one printed literally ("“96.zip” unpacks to more than {limit} MB", seen in both languages); `fillTemplate.test.ts` source-pins the notice modules against the chained shape. A leaf, so `previewMesh.ts` can use it without an i18n import
    importNote.ts     — the canvas import note's LINE model (`ImportNoteLine`, `importNoteLineText`, `importNoteHasConvertLine`); the note itself is store state — see the drop-time conversion bullet. The `glb-*` kinds (a build's report, `utils/glbImportReport.ts`) render through `glbImportCopy.glbReportLineText`, and `importNoteLifetimeMs(note)` gives a note carrying one of them 30 s (`GLB_REPORT_NOTE_MS`) instead of the ordinary 12 (`IMPORT_NOTE_MS`) — it is several lines long and the only place the build says what it did
    imageImportNote.ts — PURE, node-tested: what one image drop reports (`imageDropReport`, read off the PLACED payload) and how a drop's reports become ONE note's lines (`composeImportNoteLines`: budget, then lossy, then at most one WebP line)
    soundAnalysis.ts    — PURE sound→shader maths for the Sound node (node-testable): band bin ranges, the four-channel reduction, the emitted `<var>_<channel>` uniform-name contract shared by codegen and the pump
    soundSettings.ts        — the Sound node’s settings contract; `readSoundSettings` is the ONE coercion point (Web Audio THROWS on an out-of-range `fftSize`/`smoothingTimeConstant`, so the clamp is load-bearing)
    deviceCapture.ts     — DOM-only: the single `getUserMedia` call site in the codebase (`systemAudioCapture.ts` is the single `getDisplayMedia` one; `audioCaptureCore.ts` is the AudioContext/AnalyserNode half both capture paths share)
    imageCodec.ts     — PURE drop-time decisions (node-testable; `imageImport` is DOM-only and can't be): the power-of-two rules + the ordered codec candidate list, whose `losslessOnly` (GLB Phase 5, the import's data maps) filters out the lossy fall-through so a lossless source halves instead of going lossy; plus `encodeDimCap` and `hugeSourceDecodeSize`, the pure halves of `encodeImageFile`'s options
    imageImport.ts    — drop-time canvas re-encode (EXIF strip/orient, runtime-probed WebP incl. losslessness, quality ladder before any downscale-retry, wrap-aware power-of-two snap) — DOM-only. `encodeImageFile`'s 5th argument (`EncodeImageOptions`, GLB Phase 5) belongs to the GLB import and is absent on every drop: `maxDim` (the slot size, applied after the device cap and NOT lifted by ignore-limits, which lifts budgets only), `preferLossless` (replaces the file-name sniff, because the importer knows the SLOT), `losslessOnly`, and `allowHugeSource` + the header-read `sourceDims`, which decodes a source past the 64 MP guard straight to its capped size through `createImageBitmap`'s resize options (no `<img>` fallback, which would hold the full-size picture; an engine without them refuses as `pixels`) and reports `decodeDownscaled` (N10). Every field is read strictly, so junk is "absent". `imageImportEncode.test.ts` runs the REAL encoder in node against a fake canvas (every DOM global stubbed and restored, the caps probe reset through `__resetEncodeCapsForTests`) and pins the option-less result of 13 drops under both a Chromium-like and a WebKit-like encoder to goldens captured before the options existed
    imageOriginCache.ts — IndexedDB stash of the PRE-snap payload behind the Image node's "Revert to original" (previewMeshCache's shape; content-keyed, re-validated on read)
    textureSources.ts — the texture picker's source list (`projectTextureSources`: every DISTINCT valid image payload in the project, full-string keyed, with ONE self-consistent provenance holder each) plus `withImagePayload`, the ONE value shape "a picture becomes this node's image" takes (a pick and "From file…" both), and `pickTextureValues`. `TextureSource` is a discriminated union (today only `'project'`) and Phase 5's extension point for GLB-embedded images. Pure, node-tested
    imageUvMapping.ts — the glTF MAPPING of an Image/Texture node: orientation, UV set, the normal-map green flip and the KHR_texture_transform, over eight `values` keys. `readImageUvMapping` is the ONE reader, and `withUvMapping` / `gltfTextureValues` are the ONLY writers (the GLB importer must use the latter); every key is read exactly, and an absent, junk or default key means today's emission byte for byte. `gltfSamplerValues` (GLB Phase 5) turns a glTF sampler into the node's EXISTING texture keys: both axes CLAMP_TO_EDGE → `repeat: 0`, magFilter NEAREST → `filter: 'nearest'`, nothing else written; MIRRORED_REPEAT (read as repeat) and axes that still disagree are REPORTED (`wrapMirrored`, `wrapMixed`), never approximated silently, and the min filter is not reported, since the node's mip rule follows its colour space. An unknown or mistyped enum is REPEAT/LINEAR, as GLTFLoader and the reader read it. A leaf, node-tested
    zipWriter.ts      — dependency-free STORE-method ZIP writer (deterministic, CRC-32) for the shader+images download
    zipReader.ts      — ZIP reader (STORE + deflate via native DecompressionStream; central-directory-driven). Adversarial caps: 512 entries (folder entries included), 512-byte names, STORE/DEFLATE only, and a 96 MiB SUM of entries (`MAX_TOTAL_UNCOMPRESSED` = `MESH_MAX_BYTES` + 32 MiB, so the app's own largest legal export re-opens). Every cap throws a typed `ZipLimitError` {kind, limit, value}; corruption stays a plain Error. `MAX_ARCHIVE_BYTES` is the pre-read gate a caller holding a File applies before `arrayBuffer()`. podest.html's twin must carry the same numbers (drift-guarded, and the guard evaluates the constant's TEXT, so keep it a literal). That 96 MiB literal is the WEB cap; `DESKTOP_MAX_TOTAL_UNCOMPRESSED` (256 MiB) is the desktop room's, and `READ_MAX_TOTAL_UNCOMPRESSED`/`READ_MAX_ARCHIVE_BYTES` are what THIS build reads — `readZip(zip, maxTotal = READ_MAX_TOTAL_UNCOMPRESSED)`, and every `total-size` refusal reports the cap actually applied. Podest keeps the web cap inside the desktop app too
    zipImportNotices.ts — the words for a refused or partly-skipped zip: `zipLimitCopy` (LimitModal's `zip-limit` notice) and `zipModelSkippedLine` (the import-note line, whose `{reason}` is the model's own `meshRefusalMessage`)
    previewMesh.ts    — custom preview mesh (dropped .obj/.glb/.gltf): kind detect, 64 MiB cap (`MESH_MAX_BYTES`; the zip reader's SUM cap is this + 32 MiB) + glb magic check, name sanitize, createPreviewMesh (single constructor shared by the preview drop surface, zip import, and IndexedDB restore; it also DROPS a FastShaders payload — `extras.fastshaders` and its module/project views — from every copy it makes, see the GLB-restore convention), the compressed-glTF pre-check (`inspectGltfCompression` — see the custom-preview-mesh convention; it lives in `gltfCompression.ts` and is re-exported here) and the structured `MeshRefusal` every model refusal carries, so a notice can be translated (`previewMeshMessage.ts` renders it); the glTF model reader (`gltfReader.ts`) is the second, documented exception to its no-parse rule, and `createPreviewMesh` stores its `gltfPreviewFacts` as `PreviewMesh.gltf` (strings and numbers only; never persisted — `meshToRecord` keeps name + bytes, so a restore recomputes them; null on a refusal, absent for OBJ). It also re-exports the pre-read size gate `preReadModelGate` (see gltfCompression.ts), the one place that decides how large a dropped model may be before it is read: only a `.glb` on the build-from-materials path gets the larger `GLB_READ_MAX_BYTES` (the build's zip reader cap: 96 MiB on the web, 256 MiB on desktop), so extraction runs BEFORE the 64 MiB model gate and the texture-stripped copy is what passes it. `loadMeshFile` passes its OFFER predicate (`!isEvalMode()` and not paired with a shader and a glTF — `modelDropGate.test.ts` pins the call), so a study drop and a paired model keep exactly the 64 MiB cap
    gltfCompression.ts — a LEAF split out of previewMesh.ts, which re-exports all of it: the model caps (`MESH_MAX_BYTES` 64 MiB; `GLB_READ_MAX_BYTES`, the Phase 5 build path's pre-read cap for a `.glb`, pinned equal to THIS build's zip reader cap `READ_MAX_TOTAL_UNCOMPRESSED` — 96 MiB on the web, 256 MiB in the desktop room — as two literals picked on the guarded `__FS_DESKTOP__` define, since the leaf may import nothing; read by the glTF reader and the pre-read gate), `GLTF_JSON_PARSE_LIMIT`, and the compressed-glTF pre-check split into `inspectGltfCompression` (length cap + parse) and `inspectParsedGltf` (the rules over an already-parsed document, for the glTF reader). It also holds `preReadModelGate(kind, sizeBytes, buildEnabled)`, the ONE pre-read size gate (the larger cap for a `.glb` with a literal-true `buildEnabled`, `MESH_MAX_BYTES` for everything else, and a size that is not a finite non-negative number refused), together with the too-large refusal it returns: `MeshRefusal`, `MeshRejectReason`, `MESH_TOO_LARGE_KEY` and `modelTooLargeRefusal` moved here from previewMesh.ts, because a leaf cannot import what it must build. **A refusal carries the cap that was APPLIED** (`limitBytes`, and `MESH_TOO_LARGE_LIMIT_KEY`, the same sentence with a `{limit}`): the gate hands a `.glb` on the build path `GLB_READ_MAX_BYTES` — 96 MiB on the web, 256 in the desktop room — and reported through the fixed key, so a 120 MiB drop was refused with a ceiling 1.5x, on desktop 4x, BELOW the one it had just enforced. Two keys and not one so the first keeps its "64" LITERAL, which previewMesh.test.ts pins against `MESH_MAX_BYTES`, and so every existing caller and its lv.json entry stay byte-identical; an ABSENT `limitBytes` is the 64 MiB model gate. `fillMeshRefusal` fills `{limit}` as well as `{name}`/`{ext}`/`{size}`, so the English-only renderers (`validateMeshBytes`, `createPreviewMesh().error`) cannot ship a raw placeholder — `meshRefusalMessage` pre-fills it and that second pass is then a no-op. It exists to keep the model-reading graph ACYCLIC: the reader needs these, and `createPreviewMesh` calls the reader, so the caps living in previewMesh.ts would be an import cycle evaluated at init (the costTable TDZ lesson). It imports only `safeJson` and two meshDecoders TYPES; `gltfCompression.test.ts` pins that, and that previewMesh no longer defines what moved
    glbContainer.ts   — the GLB CONTAINER leaf (Phase 5; reached at runtime through the trusted-side glTF reader's preview facts in `createPreviewMesh`, and by the texture-stripping writer): strict parse/build (version exactly 2, JSON as chunk 0, BIN only as chunk 1, at most 16 chunks, unknown chunk types skipped, bytes after the declared length ignored, a 16 MiB JSON cap checked from the chunk HEADER before a byte is decoded, the JSON decoded with GLTFLoader's own default `TextDecoder` so both sides see the same names, `bin` a VIEW never a copy), the canonical-only base64 `data:` decoder (no whitespace, URL-safe or percent forms, non-zero discarded bits refused, the decoded size checked BEFORE decoding, `too-large` told apart from damaged), image magic sniffing (the format is decided by the BYTES, never by a declared MIME) plus bounded header dimensions (PNG/JPEG/WebP/KTX2; estimates only, decoding stays authoritative), and the 67-byte 1×1 placeholder PNG. It classifies and slices bytes: it decodes no picture, parses no JSON, resolves no URI, and imports only `binaryCodec`. **4-alignment is arithmetic (`Math.ceil(n / 4) * 4`) and NEVER `(n + 3) & ~3`** — here, in `glbRepack.ts`, and in every fixture builder: a bitwise operator coerces through ToInt32, so that spelling returns a NEGATIVE length from 2**31 up (measured: `pad4(2**31)` is `-2147483648`), which made BOTH u32 overflow guards dead code. The repacker's `totalBytes > 0xffffffff` could never be true, `planSingleGlbPreflight`'s `n(v)` then mapped the negative to 0, `0 <= limitBytes` returned null, and the one size the N1 dialog exists for was the one size at which it could not open — the build reached `new Uint8Array(negative)` and threw `RangeError: Invalid typed array length` out of `repackGlb`, which only converts `Refused`. `ceil4` sat two lines above the broken spelling in the same file. `glbContainer.test.ts` pins every refusal code and runs a seeded mutation sweep
    gltfReader.ts     — THE TRUSTED-SIDE glTF MODEL READER (Phase 5; `createPreviewMesh` calls its `gltfPreviewFacts` adapter for every glb/gltf load — drop, zip import, IndexedDB restore — and the composer `engine/gltfImport.ts` consumes a `readGltfModel` report, with no UI entry until the Step 9 dialog), the second documented exception to "model bytes are never parsed on the trusted side" (previewMesh.ts's two JSON-header readers are the first). `readGltfModel(bytes, kind)` returns a refusal (`too-large`/`unreadable`/`too-complex`/`external-data`/`invalid-model`, plus a machine `detail` never shown to a user) or a report of: materials (factors, the five core slots with the sanitized textureInfo `gltfTextureValues` takes as-is, alpha mode, the KHR_materials_* list and the textures those extensions reference), samplers, textures (webp → avif → core source order, KTX2 never extracted), images by bufferView or strict `data:` URI (a URI image is REPORTED, never fetched; the format is the SNIFFED one), primitive attribute KEYS only, the model signature, and the default scene's mesh → material mapping with the loader's predicted names (`meshNameIndex`; `mirrorNamesByMaterial` keeps only certain, usable, single-material names; `buildableMaterialIndices` is the set N11 counts). A signature over `SIGNATURE_TOTAL_CHARS_MAX` is refused, so every signature it accepts passes `sanitizeModelSignature`. It builds no geometry, reads no accessor data and never resolves a URI. Every index and view is bounds-checked, every array is count-capped BEFORE it is walked (the default scene's Mesh count included), JSON goes through safeJsonReviver, and it never throws. It is STRICTER than GLTFLoader: a second parent, a cycle, a bad reference in an unused material, a truthy non-string name, or a node on a camera with no parameters is refused, and a refusal only withdraws the build. Its report holds views into the dropped file and is TRANSIENT: it never enters the store, history, the autosave, IndexedDB or a postMessage. `gltfPreviewFacts(bytes, kind)` is the ONE adapter whose result may be kept: the signature plus usable predicted name → material indices, strings and numbers only; undefined for OBJ, null on a refusal. It imports the `gltfCompression.ts` leaf, never previewMesh.ts, so `createPreviewMesh` can call it without an import cycle
    gltfNaming.ts     — PURE, import-free: replays GLTFLoader r184's naming — one createUniqueName counter across scenes → nodes DFS (node, camera, light, children, skin joints) → animation targets → the remaining cameras, then per-primitive mesh names in first-reference order, then `_instance_<n>`, then the node-name override for a single non-bone object. Its counter is a plain `{}` ON PURPOSE, because the loader's is, so `constructor`/`toString`/`__proto__` become `…_NaN` there too. Two mesh defs sharing a sanitized base are suffixed in TEXTURE-DECODE order in the real loader (measured), so those names are `certain: false` and never become 0.6 mirror keys. `gltfNaming.test.ts` runs every expectation against the REAL GLTFLoader, with a textured variant of each
    gltfStrip.ts      — THE TEXTURE-STRIPPING WRITER (Phase 5; `engine/gltfImport.ts`, the composer, calls it): the preview copy of a model whose materials were built into a shader. `planTextureStrip(report, builtMaterials)` is a pure value (null when no built material carries a texture AND the document carries no `extras.fastshaders` — a payload alone yields a plan with `materials: []` whose whole effect is dropping it and reclaiming the module/project views plus the images only they used, which is what `dropPreviewPayload`'s compacting branch runs on — or when its walks would exceed `STRIP_WALK_BUDGET`), `stripGltfTextures(report, plan)` re-derives everything from the report and throws on a plan that does not describe it. It deletes the textureInfos of the BUILT materials only (the five core slots plus any `*Texture` inside their extensions; an unbuilt material keeps its textures), recomputes liveness by a CONSERVATIVE whole-document walk (any `texture`/`*Texture` object with a valid index keeps that texture, so an unknown extension's reference wins), and renumbers NOTHING: a dead texture becomes `{ source: P }`, a dead image the 1×1 placeholder `data:` URI, a dead view a one-byte view at offset 0. Image-only views are reclaimed by 4-aligned hole punching, so every live range keeps its residue mod 4; meshopt's own byte offsets shift with their bytes, and any other buffer or bufferView extension switches reclamation off (`'kept'`: an unknown extension may hold raw offsets). Only a PLAIN view on a buffer that has bytes can die. Texture-only extension names nothing uses are pruned from `extensionsUsed`/`extensionsRequired` (so a stripped KTX2-required model stops being refused), and `extras.fastshaders` is dropped from the root and every scene. The output keeps the container kind and re-reads with the same signature, certain names and accessor bytes (asserted in development). The CALLER must pass it through `createPreviewMesh` successfully BEFORE committing the built project, so a copy that still does not fit leaves the project untouched
    gltfFeatures.ts   — a LEAF that imports nothing: the GLB import's closed FEATURE vocabulary (`GLTF_FEATURE_IDS`, `orderFeatures`) — what a built material asked for that FastShaders has no channel for. The report prints a translated label per id and never a string out of the file, which is why the list is closed and P5c's `KHR_materials_*` name whitelist was dropped
    gltfImportPlan.ts — PURE, node-tested: the builder's decisions over the reader's ALREADY-SANITIZED report (no second material reader) — `planBuiltMaterials` (the requested BUILDABLE materials, capped at `MAX_INDEX_MATERIALS`; the rest keep their authored materials and are counted), the slot classes (`slotEncodeClass` over the ONE table `glbSlotPolicy`; occlusion never built, emissive only when it can shine — `emissiveIsUnused`), `normalGreenFlipFor` (tangent-less primitives, majority rule, a mix reported), `linearToSrgbHex` (a glTF factor is LINEAR; a Color node stores sRGB and `color(0x…)` decodes it back), `unsupportedFeaturesOf`, `encodeRequests` (each image once, first use, stored for its most demanding slot; textures with no extractable image reported) and THE estimator the dialog shares (`plannedTextureDims` / `estimateTextureChars`, from the slot table's INFERRED bits per pixel)
    gltfTextureEncode.ts — the builder's texture loop, DOM-only in production: `encodeGltfImages` runs `encodeImageFile` SERIALLY per extracted image with the slot's options (the recorded exception to the drop-time "Convert image?" question — see that bullet), enforces the PROJECT budget itself (an over-budget encode is retried at half the slot size, at most `BUDGET_RETRIES` times, then skipped as 'budget'; Ignore limits lifts the budget, never the slot sizes), checks the abort between images, stashes a pre-snap original through the drop's own `resolveImageDrop` rule, and returns the encodes plus one outcome per image (kept / downscaled and WHY — the project `budget` > the encoder's per-image cap `image-cap` > the chosen import size > the slot > the device / skipped and why; there is no "lossy" outcome, since a lossless request is always lossless-ONLY) and the N10 count. The encoder is INJECTABLE, so the loop's decisions run under node against a fake
    glbImportReport.ts — PURE: the builder's `GlbImportReport`, the ONE report type (materials, textures, shared, kept authored, the N10 count, `notImported`, per-texture outcomes), and how it becomes ONE import note's lines (`glbImportReportLines`: summary, kept-authored, N10, not-imported, at most `GLB_REPORT_TEXTURE_LINES` per-texture lines then a count; kept textures omitted; every file-supplied name re-sanitised by `sanitizeReportName`, every count clamped)
    glbImportCopy.ts  — the GLB flow's EN keys and their rendering through `fillTemplate` (a texture named `{reason}` is inserted verbatim): the REPORT half (`GLB_REPORT_KEYS`, the feature labels, the downscale reasons, the skip reasons = the reader's six fragments in `GLTF_IMAGE_SKIP_KEYS` plus the encoder's `decode`/`budget`/`image-cap`) landed with Step 8; the dialog's copy (N9/N11/N12) joins in Step 9. Its tables are `Map`s: the key is read off a value derived from a file
    glbImportGate.ts  — PURE, node-tested: `glbImportFactsOf` (the dialog's facts DERIVED from the reader's report — the buildable materials and one texture fact per Texture node the builder would make, through the builder's own `gltfTextureNodeKey`) and `planGlbImportDialog`, the dialog's every decision (offer, the N11 gate, the N9 estimate + Import-at rung, N12 memory through `textureMemory`, Model-only availability)
    glbImportLimits.ts — a LEAF that imports NOTHING, not even a type (the store will read `ALLOW_MANY_MATERIALS_KEY` at module scope — the costTable TDZ rule; `glbImportLimits.test.ts` pins it): the GLB import's material limit (`GLB_IMPORT_MATERIAL_LIMIT` 10, under `MAX_INDEX_MATERIALS`), the `fs:allowManyMaterials` key and its exact-`'1'` reader, the Import-at ladder (1024 → 128, starting at the largest slot cap), and THE slot table `glbSlotPolicy` (a `switch`, never a Record lookup; frozen shared objects): colour slots (base colour, emissive) at 1024 px, always lossy; data slots (normal, metallicRoughness) at 512 px, lossless-only from a lossless source. Occlusion is deliberately NOT a slot (the Output has no AO channel), so it answers null. Its bits-per-pixel are the higher of the two drafts (colour 1.5, normal 7.5, ORM 6, data lossy 1.8), INFERRED from libwebp-CLI figures and to be re-measured with the browser encoders. The pre-read cap and the untrusted texture side are NOT here (gltfCompression.ts, textureMemory.ts). Consumed by Steps 8 and 9 of GLB Phase 5
    glbShaderExtras.ts — the ONE trusted-side reader of a FastShaders single-GLB export (`readGlbFsExtras`/`readFsExtras` over root `extras.fastshaders`, the format in `engine/glbShaderContract.ts`): it validates every pointer against the BIN, refuses what loader 0.8 refuses for a module view, resolves the `"fs-asset:<key>"` placeholders from the GLB's own png/jpeg/webp bufferView images into canonical `data:` URLs, and holds the in-place payload drop `dropFastShadersPayload`, which zeroes the payload's bytes **wherever they live — the GLB's BIN chunk OR an embedded `data:` buffer**. The GLB path used to skip every range whose buffer index was not 0 while the `.gltf` path handled them, so a payload on `buffers[1]` survived a preview copy byte for byte (residue, not execution: `extras` is deleted either way, so loader 0.8 can no longer locate it); one shared `zeroEmbeddedBuffers` serves both paths now, so they cannot drift again. A buffer URI `decodeDataUri` refuses — a non-canonical spelling, or a media type outside `BUFFER_MEDIA_TYPES` — is still skipped, which no writer here produces but a crafted file can. Store-free, imports only leaves; it slices bytes and runs nothing. podest.html carries a vanilla twin that `podestGlbReader.test.ts` runs against it, fixture for fixture and byte for byte
    previewMeshCache.ts — IndexedDB mirror of the custom mesh so it survives a reload (save/load + the pure `meshToRecord`/`recordToMesh` codec, which re-runs full drop-time validation on cached bytes); throw-safe everywhere; a QUOTA failure on saving the mesh on screen is the one outcome REPORTED (`fs:mesh-cache-full` → the preview's "will not be restored after a reload" line), and any failed write deletes the stale record so a reload cannot resurrect the previous model — but only while it is still the NEWEST save (`gen === saveGen`): readwrite transactions run in creation order, so a newer save's put can be queued ahead of an older save's follow-up delete, and an unconditional delete removed the model actually on screen, silently. No IndexedDB (incl. a private window whose engine refuses it — older Firefox/Safari fail the open), a failed open, a blocked upgrade, other failures or a 5 s hang stay silent. **Private mode is NOT detected, because it cannot be**: every current engine gives a private window a working, smaller IndexedDB, and its `QuotaExceededError` carries the same name as a full disk's — so a real quota error is announced even in a private window. That is truthful (private-window storage survives a reload within the session, so the model really will not come back), and it is a deliberate departure from the research doc's N7 "never in private mode", which assumed a private window has no IndexedDB. Also owns `bootGeometryWasCustom()`, the module-init snapshot of `fs:previewGeometry` — DOM-only apart from the codec
    meshInventory.ts  — the sub-mesh INVENTORY the sandboxed preview reports (`fs:model-meshes`) plus the mesh-name rules; pure, session-only data (see the per-mesh materials convention). It is still the ONLY source of mesh TARGETS: gltfReader's predicted names serve only the index-vs-name double-claim check, index-section dormancy (via `previewMesh.gltf`), the index chip's coverage marks and hover highlight (`highlightMeshes`, display only) and the 0.6 mirror keys — they never become mesh TARGETS and never reach emission
    outputMaterials.ts — the Output nodes' MATERIALS (ONE node, ONE material): the four predicates that answer "THE Output" (`defaultOutput`, `contributingOutputs`, `moduleSettingsOutput`, `findDefaultOutput` — see the several-outputs convention), `outputsInEmitOrder` over the re-exported `emitRank`, `outputMaterials`, `sanitizeOutputMaterials` for every restore path, and `unfoldOutputMaterials` + `UNFOLD_DY` — the one-node-per-material split every restore path runs, which replaced the deleted `foldExtraOutputs`, and which keeps `channelHandle`/`parseChannelHandle` alive as its only consumer (a saved `.fastshader` always carries the folded shape). Then the CROSS-NODE plans emission and the node's own marks share, each a running cap and one claim set inside ONE call (`planNamedPartsAcross` capped at `MAX_PART_ENTRIES`, `planIndexPartsAcross` at `MAX_INDEX_MATERIALS`, `countSectionsAcross`, `indexSectionCoverageAcross`, `defaultSectionUnusedAcross`, `materialPartsMirrorPlanAcross` + `mirrorPlanKey`, plus their per-node twins kept as the tested primitives the byte-stability golden is written over); `moduleSignatureOf` (the signature governing the module's table), `sectionLabel` (what one label for a material says; `nodes/sectionLabelText.ts` words it), `assignMeshTargetsAcross` (the cross-node mesh move, one `setNodes`), `previewWireTargets` (one wire per contributing node) and `outputEdgeIsDormant` (the scoped React Flow 008 swallow); and the restore half that ANNOUNCES what it drops (`sanitizeOutputMaterialsReport` → the `output-sections-trimmed` notice, `pruneOrphanMaterialEdges`, whose remaining job is an `m<k>:` handle the unfold did not touch). Import-built INDEX bindings (GLB Phase 5) ride the same file: `isIndexSection`/`gltfIndexOf`, `readModelSignature`, `carryModelMeshes`, `firstNamedOutputId`, and the trusted-side dormancy and double claim (`loadedModelOf` over `previewMesh.gltf`, `indexSectionsAwake`, `dormantIndicesForPreview`)
    meshHighlight.ts  — the `fs:mesh-highlight` CustomEvent an authoring surface fires to light a mesh up in the 3D preview (ShaderPreview forwards it into the sandbox). Since GLB Phase 5 Step 10 it also carries a LIST (`highlightMeshes`, `sanitizeHighlightNames`, capped at 256 non-empty strings): hovering an import-built index section's chip lights EVERY mesh of its glTF material, the coverage's predicted names, through the same path the mesh picker's rows use; the sandbox script takes one name or the list and matches by OWN property, since a name out of a file can spell `constructor`. Older preview documents ignore `names` and light the first one
    previewModelDrop.ts — the `fs:preview-model-file` CustomEvent the node canvas fires to hand a dropped `.obj`/`.glb`/`.gltf` to the preview's own model path (one validation, one notice, one geometry switch)
    previewMeshMessage.ts — the translated model-drop wording (`meshRefusalMessage`, the KTX2-fallback and cache-full lines, and `decoderLoadMessage` for a decoder the preview could not fetch), plus the glTF model reader's: `gltfBuildRefusalMessage` (why a model's materials cannot be built; an info line, since the model still loads model-only, and a `too-large` refusal reads as the ordinary too-large sentence) and `gltfImageSkipReason` (the per-texture skip fragment of the import report). Their key tables are `Map`s, since the key is read off a value derived from a file
    ktx2Header.ts     — the bounded KTX2 header/DFD/KVD reader, parity-pinned to three's ktx-parse; null on any violation, never throws. `isKtx2` is the ONE KTX2 magic check
    ktx2Encoder.ts    — the pluggable KTX2 ENCODER SEAM: interface, registry (null in every build — no encoder ships yet), per-slot request planning (always UASTC + zstd 18, never ETC1S), and `encodeKtx2Checked`, which never rejects and validates the output with `ktx2Header`
    ktx2Pixels.ts     — DOM-only: an Image node's stored payload → RGBA8 at the size the caller declares, for that seam (`createImageBitmap` with colorSpaceConversion/premultiplyAlpha/imageOrientation all 'none', so a normal or data map is not re-interpreted; a bitmap of any OTHER size is refused, `encodeGlbFallback`'s rule and the bound on what may be decoded). Never imported by a node test — `prepareSingleGlb` takes an injectable reader — so `exportSingleGlb.test.ts` pins the three options, `willReadFrequently` and that size check from SOURCE, reading the function's own text rather than the whole file, since the header comment names the same three options in prose
    meshDecoders.ts   — the trusted side of the Draco/meshopt decoders: `DECODER_FILES` (the loader's `FastShaders.decoders.FILES` and podest's literal, pinned equal), `decoderFilesFor`, and `loadDecoderPayload`, the memoized fetch whose bytes ShaderPreview pushes into the sandbox (see the decoders convention)
    exportBundle.ts   — pure "Download Shader" bundle assembly (js-vs-zip decision, entry list incl. models/, README text + A-Frame mesh-pairing snippet); every bundle also carries its size facts (`unpackedBytes`, `meshBytes`, `unpackedBytesWithoutMesh`), counted from the SAME entry list the zip is written from
    exportPreflight.ts — PURE, node-tested: `planExportPreflight`, the N1 decision, `planDesktopOnlyExport`, its non-blocking desktop variant, and `planSingleGlbPreflight`, the N1-GLB variant (see the export pre-flight convention)
    glbExportAvailability.ts — PURE LEAF (type-only imports): can the loaded preview model be packed into one `.glb` (`glbExportAvailability`, over the session-only `PreviewMesh.gltf`/`gltfReadRefusal` facts — no re-parse), and `effectiveExportFormat`, THE format every surface asks for. The flag is DERIVED at render and never written back (the `effectiveTab` precedent)
    glbExportCopy.ts — the single-GLB export's EN keys and their rendering: why a `.glb` could not be built, the N1-GLB dialog with its "Export WebP only" pair, the report lines a delivered file posts (as import-note LINES, so they re-render in the active language), the popover's Format rows and `glbUnavailableText`, the exhaustive availability sentence
    platformCaps.ts   — the ONE table of caps that differ between the web build and the desktop room (`WEB_CAPS`/`DESKTOP_CAPS`, picked by `__FS_DESKTOP__` into `PLATFORM_CAPS`): the per-image, project and library image budgets, the ref-resolution guard, the imageAssets decode memo and the imageOriginCache bounds all read it. Only the PER-IMAGE cap (6M) sits under the unchanged 8M per-image hard ceiling, which still bounds every single adversarial payload on both builds; the project, library, ref and cache totals are above 8M by design. The zip caps live in zipReader instead (`DESKTOP_MAX_TOTAL_UNCOMPRESSED`, `READ_*`). A zero-import leaf that reads the define as `typeof __FS_DESKTOP__ !== 'undefined' && __FS_DESKTOP__`, because the research doc's probe recipe runs these leaves in bare node (Vite still replaces it). vitest runs the WEB profile, so `platformCaps.test.ts` pins the web numbers exactly and holds the desktop table to its relationships (the reader opens a full desktop export, refs resolve a full project, …)
    digestMemo.ts     — `createDigestMemo(hash, maxEntries, maxChars)`: a bounded string→digest LRU, so `imageAssetFor` stops re-running the FNV round over every payload per Image node per graph→code pass (MEASURED in node at a 6M-char payload: ~7.6 ms per hash; a hit on the same string object ~0, on an equal copy ~0.12 ms). The character bound counts the KEYS, because a Map keeps them alive, and it keeps the entry just inserted even when that alone is over budget. It is PLATFORM-SIZED through imageAssets (twice the decode memo's bounds): two payloads that do not both fit evict each other on every lookup (`digestMemo.test.ts` pins 6M+3M alternating as 2 hashes under 40M and 20 under 4M). A zero-import leaf that never computes a digest itself, so it cannot become a second FNV copy
    desktopDownloads.ts — the desktop release assets, the ONE list (`DESKTOP_DOWNLOADS`, `releaseDownloadUrl`, `downloadsFor`, `desktopDownloadPlatform`), read by the toolbar's "Download app" dropdown and by DesktopAppNote; `desktopDownloads.test.ts` reads release.yml and fails when its upload names, header list or release-notes table drift from it. A zero-import leaf
    desktopAppNoteRules.ts — which LimitModal notices and which N1 refusals the desktop app LIFTS (`desktopLiftsLimit`, `exportLiftedByDesktop`); `LIFTS` is exhaustive over `LimitNotice['kind']`, so a new kind fails `tsc` until it decides. A `zip-limit` is lifted only for a total-size refusal whose `value` fits 256 MiB — and that value is a LOWER bound, so the note promises "much larger files", never this one
    desktopIpc.ts     — the frontend half of the desktop IPC contract: command and header names (`WORK_FOLDER_WRITE_BYTES`, `FILE_NAME_HEADER`), `DESKTOP_MAX_READ_BYTES` (= `DESKTOP_MAX_TOTAL_UNCOMPRESSED` + the 8 MiB archive slack = Rust's `MAX_READ_BYTES`), `parseDesktopError` (Rust's structured `E_<CODE> [value limit | detail]` errors; null for the English ones older builds and the untouched commands still return), `encodeHeaderName` (`encodeURIComponent`: header values must be visible ASCII, file names are Unicode) and `desktopBytes`. `desktopIpcContract.test.ts` reads the Rust sources, main.rs's handler list, Cargo.toml/Cargo.lock and the capability file and pins every shared value against this one: every invoked command registered and defined directly under `#[tauri::command]` (async unless it touches no picked file), the header string, the read cap, every `E_` literal parsing to its own code. It proves the TEXT agrees; the crate's `cargo test` proves the Rust behaves
    desktopAutosave.ts — the desktop room's file-backed autosave, frontend half: the bridge interface over the eight `autosave_*` commands (`tauriAutosaveBridge`), the WRITER (`desktopRefsForStorage` — every valid image payload stored once as `images/<sha256>.txt` and written `imageB64: ''` + `imageRef: 'fsimg-<sha256>'`, never an `img1-` ref), the READER (`materializeDesktopRefs` — refs back to payloads before `parseStoredGraph`/`parseStoredGroupsReport` run the web's own sanitisers, bounded per document by `MAX_REF_RESOLVED_IMAGE_CHARS` charged ONCE per DISTINCT image — per instance it bounded nothing, since every instance shares the one cached string, and it stripped at boot what the writer had accepted: the library is budgeted per UNIQUE payload, so eleven groups sharing one 6M image passed with no notice, the eleventh's ref failed a 64M budget charged per instance, was counted dangling, and was written back without its image; an image that is MISSING or CORRUPT is left dangling for N8, while any other failed image read — I/O, a permission, the boot timeout — is a failed READ that pauses writes, since the next write would otherwise drop a still-good ref and the next GC its file), `stringifyDesktopDocument` (the file's JSON keeps an `imageRef` key ONLY on the values objects the writer put a ref into — autosave.rs refuses a document whose `"imageRef":"fsimg-<hex>"` names an image not on disk, so a stray key a crafted `.fastshader` left on any other node would otherwise refuse that document's every later save), `createSerialSaver` (one run at a time, any number of requests during it cost one more run) and the runtime the store routes both slots to — whose two savers run CONCURRENTLY and share ONE in-flight put per payload (the first desktop boot migrates a graph and a library that share an image, and two puts of one digest raced in Rust). Everything is behind an injected bridge, so `desktopAutosave.test.ts` drives the REAL store against a fake autosave.rs; the store imports only its TYPES
    sdfPartition.ts   — PURE, node-tested: THE ACTIVE SINK (`activeSink`, `drivingMarchOutput`, `normalizeActiveOutput`, `clearActiveOutput`, `hasActiveFlag` — see the several-outputs convention) and which nodes the RAYMARCH OUTPUT re-evaluates PER RAY STEP — `marchPartition(nodes, edges, sink)` gives one set per per-step socket (`MARCH_SCOPES`: ancestors that depend on that scope's ROOTS — `MARCH_ROOT_TYPES` positionLocal/positionGeometry for the `p` scopes, `DIR_ROOT_TYPES` rayDirection for Background's `dir` scope) plus `mainAlso` (roots, and members with a consumer outside every set); `drivingMarchOutput` (Field OR Density WIRED) is the ONE predicate every surface asks, `marchWindowRadius` its Window. Read by graphToCode (emission scopes) AND nodeCost (steps × body pricing) so the two cannot disagree
    sinkCarry.ts      — PURE, node-tested: the resync's carry of INACTIVE output nodes and their incoming edges (utils/activeOutput.test.ts pins apply∘apply stability)
    workFolderFile.ts — pure file-identity rules for the desktop Work folder's open/save loop (which file a load adopts its name from, whether a rename means save-as, which file Save writes). Desktop-only in use; its byte budget restates `safe_name` in `src-tauri/src/work_folder.rs` — see the src-tauri bullet
    viewportMemory.ts — the canvas's remembered pan/zoom (`fs:viewport`); see the viewport-memory convention
    nodePreview.ts    — PURE, node-tested: Preview mode's derived graph (`previewGraph` — every sink input dropped, a clean flagged Output, one socket→Color edge), what a node offers to preview, and whether a stored target is still live; see the Preview-mode convention
    costOverride.ts   — the measured/authored cost PROFILES themselves (parse, identity, provenance, persistence to `fs:costProfiles`); `costTable.ts` only applies the winner, and stays a LEAF for the import-cycle reason above
    textureMemory.ts  — the texture-memory FIGURE: bytes of GPU memory the ACTIVE sink's textures take (`textureMemory` is the core the GLB import dialog N12 reuses, `graphTextureMemory` the graph adapter over UNWRAPPED edges, `textureMemoryLine` the one/other sentence). Each unique texture is width × height × 4 over its exact mip chain for a colour image, ×1 for a data map (no mipmaps); an image wired from its Color socket into Environment adds three's two half-float PMREM targets (an Alpha/R/G/B wire is a scalar ambient and adds none), 3·max(c,112) × 4c × 8 B each with c = 2^floor(log2(W/4)), which is 48 MiB for a 2048-wide equirect (the test reads three's own source, so an r186 bump fails loudly instead of drifting). It is a NUMBER, not a verdict: no headset memory budget has been measured. 'Unique' is the emission sharing key (payload + `readImageTextureSpec`, the ONE normaliser; keyed on the RAW stored string, so a crafted non-canonical duplicate over-reports by one texture, the safe direction), and a test pins the count to graphToCode's emitted `new THREE.Texture(` count. Stored dimensions are adversarial: a texture exists iff decodeImageNode's gates would emit it, and a size failing imageNodeCost's strict gate is counted as 2048². It reaches the store's import cycle through `outputMaterials`, so the costTable lesson applies: nothing is evaluated across the cycle at module scope, and it imports neither edgeUtils, nodeCost nor the store directly (pinned). Shown as one muted line under Total Cost, in the Output node’s **Material Settings** menu under its "Shader (whole document)" heading — which is where the three whole-graph figures live, since the rest of that menu is one material’s — only (hidden in every study arm; if ever shown there it must also honour `pointsVisible`, since it is cost feedback)
    palettes.ts / paletteUi.ts — the colour-palette model, and the Palettes modal's DECISIONS kept out of the component (pre-decode file-size gate, export file name, how many imported palettes fit, the four colour-array edits) — the vitest env is `node`, so a React dialog cannot be rendered in a test and only the pure half can be pinned
    asciiStorage.ts   — `toAsciiStorageJson`, the ONE writer path for `fs:graph` and `fs:savedGroups`: stringify, escape every code unit above U+007F, re-encode so WebKit stores it 8-bit (see the ASCII-storage convention)
    groupFrame.ts     — group FRAME geometry, a zero-import LEAF (type-only imports): `groupFrameSize` and the resize grip's `MIN_GROUP_W`/`MIN_GROUP_H`, MOVED out of useAppStore and RE-EXPORTED from it so GroupNode.tsx and groupCollapse.test.ts keep their one import site; `absoluteNodePosition` (the parent-chain walk — React Flow positions are parent-RELATIVE, and comparing two readings of that number is how a "lowest node" search picks the wrong one); and `growGroupFrames`, the scoped, unpadded, height-only fit the unfold runs. It is a leaf because `outputMaterials.ts` needs it and sits inside the store's import cycle — a second edge back into the store from inside that cycle is the costTable TDZ lesson (`groupFrame.test.ts` pins the type-only imports, the re-export and that outputMaterials never imports the store)
    graphShape.ts     — element-shape gate for the three untrusted graph restore paths (`loadGraph`, `applyProjectToStore`, `loadSavedGroups`). The failure it closes is DATA LOSS, not a wrong picture: React Flow dereferences `node.position.x` unguarded while committing
    graphSemantics.ts — O(N+E) reference-scan predicates classifying a store update by what it can possibly affect, so consumers keyed on array identity stop re-running at pointer rate (every drag frame replaces the nodes array while REUSING each `data` object)
    idbSafe.ts        — the throw-safe IndexedDB plumbing `previewMeshCache` and `imageOriginCache` share; every failure mode (private mode, a context where reading `globalThis.indexedDB` itself throws, a blocked upgrade, quota, a factory that never answers) degrades to the session-only behaviour that predates the caches; `idbWrite` REPORTS its outcome (`complete`/`quota`/`failed`/`timeout`, via the pure `classifyIdbFailure`) but still never rejects
    soundSession.ts     — THE live-capture singleton: one capture, one analyser, and the source it is pointed at (`SoundSourceRef`, session-only). It was TWO sessions until the Audio Input node was folded into the Sound node (2026-09-08) — a microphone is simply a `device` source, so the second one had nothing left to own. Module state rather than a store field, so it can never ride undo history, the `fs:graph` autosave, saved groups or the project embed, and so the node’s arm light and the preview’s SoundControl read ONE truth. That there is exactly one analyser is also why the node is a singleton on the canvas — see `components/NodeEditor/singletonNodes.ts`
    historyGesture.ts — run a composite handler as ONE undo entry (the non-hook sibling of `useHistoryBracket`, which is for bursts with no reliable "done" event)
    tauriBridge.ts    — the app's ONE Tauri IPC boundary. Both desktop call sites used to hold a byte-identical copy of the guard, and desktop paths can only be exercised on a real `tauri build` — exactly the shape of correction that goes unnoticed as missing from the other copy. `invokeDesktop(cmd, args, options)` takes either a JSON object or a RAW body (`Uint8Array`/`ArrayBuffer`), which the command receives untouched as `ipc::Request::body()`; a raw body has no room for named arguments, so anything else rides `options.headers`
public/
  js/                       — VENDORED, do not hand-edit: synced from a-frame-shaderloader/js/ by the fs-vendor-sync vite plugin (vendorSync.test.ts fails on drift)
    a-frame-shaderloader-0.8.js — the shaderloader: the framework-agnostic FastShaders core (`globalThis.FastShaders`, use/load/apply for plain three.js), the thin A-Frame `shader` component that IS the core's state object, and the GLTFLoader plugin behind `materialParts` (see the loader-0.8 and glTF material-index bullets). It rewrites three/tsl imports → globalThis.THREE.TSL, TDZ fix + auto-import + typed property schema: number/color/map, per-sub-mesh `parts` dispatch, and the vertex WELD for displaced primitives — see the weld convention. 0.4, 0.5 and 0.6 are FROZEN in the SUBMODULE (already-exported shaders fetch them from the CDN) and are no longer copied here
    a-frame-180-a-01.min.js — A-Frame 1.8.0 IIFE bundle, Three.js r184 WebGPU. Carries ONE patch, applied by `a-frame-shaderloader/build/build.mjs`'s `patchAframeRenderer()`: the **`backend` renderer property** from the still-open aframevr/aframe#5847, so `<a-scene renderer="backend: webgl">` maps to `WebGPURenderer`'s `forceWebGL` and forces the WebGL2 backend. That is the ONLY way to enter WebXR on this bundle — three refuses XR on a WebGPU backend in as many words — and it replaces hiding `navigator.gpu` in an inline `<script>` per document. r185 does NOT lift the restriction: it added native `XRGPUBinding`, but a WebGPU backend still throws unless the session was granted the `"webgpu"` feature, which A-Frame never requests and Quest Browser cannot grant. The patch targets the PREBUILT `dist/aframe-master.module.min.js` (A-Frame's `exports` resolves `import` to it, so the `src/` tree is never read), fails the build on a missing or non-unique anchor, and is proven inert for existing pages: the rebuilt bundle minus its two insertions is byte-identical to the previous one, and the new branch only runs when a `renderer` attribute explicitly spells `backend:`. `aframeBackendProperty.test.ts` guards all of it
    aframe-orbit-controls.min.js — orbit controls for preview
  models/
    teapot.obj              — Utah teapot, 1976 version, WITH texture coordinates and normals: GENERATED by `npm run gen:teapot` (scripts/gen-teapot-obj.mjs → engine/teapotGeometry.ts at STATIC_TEAPOT_RESOLUTION 16; teapotGeometry.test.ts fails on drift). The EDITOR no longer loads it — it tessellates the teapot live (see the runtime-teapot convention); podest and the XR bunny path are what still fetch model files
    stanford-bunny.obj      — Stanford bunny (preview geometry)
  podest.html               — standalone full-screen shader viewer (drop .js/.zip shader or .glb/.gltf/.obj model; a dropped FastShaders `.glb` that carries its shader ASKS, naming the file, before running it (a parent-side reader twin; a shader dropped with it wins, zips and the work folder never ask, and the boot replay runs the accepted text without asking again), and every `.glb` is stripped of its payload in `loadModelBuffer`; sandboxed iframe bridge; fullscreen presentation mode + optional title; kiosk-sized "Work folder…" button scans a picked folder [FS Access API, else webkitdirectory] into a shader dropdown with Cycle/Shuffle/seconds-per-shader playlist [prefs persist, the folder pick itself cannot]; on a CONFIRMED immersive-vr device the panel's green button becomes "Enter VR" → a confirm-gated top-level real-origin popup [WebXR can't start in the sandboxed stage — Chromium denies permissions to opaque origins, Quest Browser crashes on sandboxed-iframe WebXR, parent gestures don't cross; popup bakes state, forces WebGL2/GLSL, auto-enters + in-document gate, origin-widened loader-URL guard, `vr-nav` locomotion — see the VR-locomotion bullet] while the floating ⛶ stays the presentation-mode escape hatch; panel explanations live in title tooltips, not inline hints; the Spin button owns a row under the geometry picker and REVEALS its speed slider + `n×` readout only while spinning; a dropped model carrying glTF clips gets a bottom-left play/in-place pill + scrubber with a right-click clip picker, hidden in presentation mode — see the model-animation convention; an opt-in "Show FPS and frame time" checkbox draws a top-left readout fed by an `fs:stats` report the stage posts ~4×/s ONLY while enabled (`fs:stats-on`, replayed by `applyStateToStage`) — the number is the presented frame PERIOD, so it reads the panel's refresh rate until the shader really misses frames, and it deliberately survives presentation mode since that is the resolution worth measuring; see the Preview-backend and Podest-unattended bullets)
  webgpu-xr-demo.html       — standalone WebGPU/TSL + immersive-WebXR demo page (documents the forceWebGL rationale)
  images/                   — every served picture, with a README covering provenance/licensing: funding logos (`logo-eu-cofunded.svg`, `logo-nap2027.svg`, `logo-via.svg` — the About/Contact popover, whose `<img>`s carry an `onError` that HIDES a missing file, so a bad path shows nothing rather than a broken icon) + tab icons: `favicon-app.svg` (the three Vite-served app pages) + `favicon-podest.svg`. `ShaderCarousel/images/favicon-carousel.svg` is a SEPARATE folder on purpose — the carousel is copied as a self-contained tree (dist, the desktop LAN resource, any static host), so an icon reaching up into `public/` would 404 everywhere but dist. `favicons.test.ts` pins each page's href AND its reference FORM: the Vite entries use root-absolute `/images/favicon-app.svg` (base-prefixed at build), while podest.html and the carousel page are copied VERBATIM and must stay RELATIVE or they resolve against the server root on every deployment that isn't at `/`
docs/                       — the images and prose the repo's OWN markdown links (fastshaders-function-diagram.{png,svg}, PODEST.md, plus a README explaining both). Deliberately NOT in `public/`, which is "everything the app serves" and is copied into `dist/` on every build — nothing here ships with the app. The root README links the PNG on purpose: the SVG is a draw.io export whose every label is HTML in a `<foreignObject>`, which an `<img>`-loaded SVG does not draw in several browsers, so it would show boxes and arrows with no text at all
```

### Subprojects (outside src/)

- `ShaderCarousel/` — three purpose-built benchmark pages (no auto-play; centred Start gate each):
  - `bench-inout/` — A-Frame WebGL + WebXR, sphere-mover ping-pong, gate triggers `enterVR()`; logs rAF deltas via the bench-tick A-Frame component so frames are captured during XR too. UA-sniff headset detect + text override
  - `bench-static/` — Three.js WebGPU, static full-coverage sphere @ Quest 3 per-eye (2064×2208), `onSubmittedWorkDone` fence + multi-pass (default 30) defeats vsync clamping
  - `bench-microplane/` — Three.js WebGPU, 1024×1024 ortho quad, multi-pass; defaults to noise atomics only (per-node calibration via baseline subtraction)
