  - Shared infra in `lib/`: `bench-style.css`, `bench-driver.js` (shared WebGPU boot/measure/report driver for static+microplane), `bench-timing.js` (multi-pass wall-clock + timestamp-query GPU timing), `bench-stats.js` (computeStats + exportResults emitting raw JSON + summary CSV + **complexity-suggestion JSON** mapping marginal ms → suggested points), `bench-registry.js` (baseline + 8 presets + 8 noise atomics + DCE-safe calibration k-sweeps [scaffold controls + per-node ×k, plus combos] + TEXTURE atoms, built only when the bench passes THREE as well as TSL (all three do): two per-fetch profile atoms carrying `copies: 16` (the driver and InOut copy it onto the result, and bench-stats divides `marginalPoints` by it) and a `calib_tex_*` k-sweep over its OWN `calib_tex_scaffold`, on generated incompressible textures (RGBA8 images configured as `imageTexturePlan` configures the app's; the LUT an RGBA half-float 256×1 as `bakeColormapTexture` bakes it, except Repeat on S), sampled at `uv = fract(screenCoordinate.xy / span + offset_i)` (span 2048; 512 for the quarter-size `…q` atoms), the LUT at `vec2(uv.x, 0.5)`, and never wrapped in `safeWrap` (its magenta fallback would measure as a free fetch); `benchTextureAtoms.test.ts` pins them + saved-groups loader stub — reads `fs:savedGroups` but greys entries lacking a `tslCode` field), `bench-ui.js` (grouped picker with master checkboxes — the `texture`/`texcalib` groups are OFF by default in every bench until a Quest run passes the acceptance gates — settings persistence, Reset-to-defaults, start gate, done popup, headset detect)
  - `benchData/` — committed calibration runs + `fit-calibration.mjs`, a CLI over the pure `fit-core.mjs` that the vitest suite imports (`fitCalibration.test.ts`). It regresses the k-copy sweep exports into per-node complexity.json point suggestions: `tex_*` ops subtract `tex_scaffold` and every other op subtracts `scaffold`. It also prints the texture size curve (suggested `IMAGE_COST_MIN_SCALE`, the log-linear residual at 1024, the 4096/2048 flat check), the coherence ratios and a texture/ALU additivity check, and `--texture-json` writes what the repricing step consumes. `fit-core`'s `IMAGE_CURVE` restates nodeCost's `IMAGE_COST_*` and is drift-pinned. METHODS.md documents the protocol, and its §6 is the Quest 3 texture run with its acceptance gates (not yet run); the `quest3-20260723` run is the measured basis for the current noise prices
  - Three.js **r184** WebGPU ESM at `lib/three/` (the official `.min` builds — `three.webgpu.min.js`/`three.core.min.js`/`three.tsl.min.js` — copied from `node_modules/three@0.184`; used by the static/microplane benches via import map), A-Frame 1.8.0 IIFE at `components/three/a-frame-180-a-01.min.js` (synced from `a-frame-shaderloader/js/` by fs-vendor-sync; used by InOut), `sphere-mover.js`
  - Launcher at `ShaderCarousel/index.html` adopts each iframe's `<style>` AND `<link rel=stylesheet>` so adopted controls keep styling; `#bench-start-gate` + `#bench-done-popup` stay in the iframe (XR-entry needs the gesture origin to be inside the iframe document). **The front door is one button** — "Benchmark this device" runs MicroPlane with shipped defaults (the ONE mode/config whose export prices nodes for the cost bar) by programmatically pressing the iframe's gate; safe because MicroPlane needs no gesture, gated on the driver's `window.__benchReady` flag (clicking during init throws) + a visible, enabled gate (hidden = already running), with a poll timeout that degrades to "press ▶ Start yourself". The mode dropdown (relabelled with each mode's pricing capability; InOut marked not-for-pricing) lives in a collapsed Advanced `<details>`; `DEFAULT_MODE` is `bench-microplane`. The per-page "no auto-play; centred Start gate" rule still holds — auto-press is a LAUNCHER behavior, and only for the primary flow. Bench export filenames carry a device slug + date (`bench-stats.js deviceSlug` — UA headset name first, adapter string fallback); the done popup's `.popup-hint` tells users to drag any downloaded .json onto the editor's points box
- `a-frame-shaderloader/` — **single source of truth** for the A-Frame bundle (`build/build.mjs` emits it), the shaderloader component, and orbit-controls. The `fs-vendor-sync` vite plugin copies these into `public/js/` (all three) and `ShaderCarousel/components/three/` (the bundle) at dev/build start — edit only here, never the copies. Also a git submodule + the jsdelivr CDN source for exported shaders.
- `src-tauri/` — Tauri v2 desktop shell. Four custom Rust modules. `src/podest_window.rs` — `podest_open`, which opens `public/podest.html` in its OWN app window (`WebviewUrl::App("podest.html")`, which resolves against `frontendDist` in a built app and `devUrl` under `tauri dev`, so it needs to name neither `tauri://localhost` nor `http://tauri.localhost` nor the dev port). It exists because `target="_blank"` is meaningless in a Tauri webview — neither WKWebView nor WebView2 honours it without a host handler — so the toolbar's "P" anchor was simply INERT on desktop; `Toolbar.tsx` therefore branches the whole ELEMENT on `__FS_DESKTOP__` (an `<a href>` with a click-preventing handler still shows a bogus status-bar URL and still offers "Open in new window"). Deliberately a Rust command rather than the frontend's `WebviewWindow` API: creating a window from JS needs `core:webview:allow-create-webview-window`, and app commands registered in `invoke_handler` are covered by bare `core:default`, so this adds a window WITHOUT widening the webview ACL. Two things are load-bearing: the builder must call **`disable_drag_drop_handler()`** — note the no-argument `disable_…` spelling, NOT the config key's `drag_drop_enabled(false)` — or the OS drop handler swallows every HTML5 drop and podest, which is drag-drop-FIRST, cannot be given a shader at all; and a second click FOCUSES the existing window (`get_webview_window` → `unminimize` then `set_focus`; unminimize first, or focusing a minimized window does nothing visible and reads as the button being broken). The `podest` label is deliberately absent from `capabilities/default.json`'s `windows` array, so that webview gets NO IPC permissions — correct, since podest.html never invokes a command. Known rough edge: podest's own "Enter VR" calls `window.open`, which returns null in a webview, so it surfaces its popup-blocked message there. `src/work_folder.rs` — the desktop Work-folder commands (`work_folder_status/pick/forget/list/read` and `work_folder_write_bytes`): a native folder picker via tauri-plugin-dialog's RUST API (registered in main.rs; the webview gets NO dialog/fs permissions — capabilities stay bare `core:default`, and the IPC surface takes only `safe_name`-vetted bare file names, never paths — `.js`, `.zip` and, since GLB Phase 7, `.glb`, the single-GLB export, so the listing shows every `.glb` in the folder and `loadEntry` sends one to `importShaderGlb` (a `.glb` carrying no FastShaders shader changes nothing and says so; a study session neither lists nor opens one) — ':' rejected too, since `Path::join` REPLACES the root for a Windows drive-relative "C:x.js"), the picked path persisted to `app_config_dir/work-folder.txt` and re-validated on every resolve (a vanished folder silently unlinks), saves staged via `create_new` (O_EXCL — a planted symlink is never followed) then a DIRECT rename (no remove-first: std's Windows rename already replaces, and a remove would open the very no-file crash window the tmp dance closes), and `.js` display names recovered by a byte-safe scan of the FASTSHADERS_PROJECT_V1 block (a fixed-offset `&str` slice PANICS on a multi-byte char boundary — Latvian names make that routine). **Bytes cross IPC RAW both ways**: a read returns a `tauri::ipc::Response` (an ArrayBuffer in the webview), and a save is `work_folder_write_bytes`, whose request BODY is the file and whose name rides the `x-fs-file-name` header, percent-encoded by `encodeHeaderName` and decoded by `decode_header_name` into the same `safe_name` gate (so an encoded `/` or `..` gains nothing). It replaced a base64 `work_folder_write` that inflated a 256 MiB zip by a third into one JSON string; the command and the `base64` crate are gone, so the frontend and the binary must ship in ONE release. `MAX_READ_BYTES` is 264 MiB (the desktop reader's 256 MiB zip cap plus the 8 MiB archive slack), and a larger file is refused before it is read with `E_TOO_LARGE <size> <limit>`, which `WorkFolder.loadEntry` turns into the N2 `zip-limit` notice for a zip (limit minus the slack, i.e. the unpacked cap) and into its own sentence for anything else; `WorkFolder.save` turns the structured `E_BAD_BODY` (the raw body did not arrive as bytes — Tauri's postMessage fallback re-serialises it as JSON for the rest of the session once its custom-protocol fetch fails, so EVERY raw-body command fails then, the autosave's included) and `E_BAD_NAME` into one sentence rather than the literal code; the other command errors are plain English and shown verbatim. `desktopIpcContract.test.ts` pins these literals against `utils/desktopIpc.ts`, and `cargo test` covers `safe_name` and `decode_header_name`; neither can exercise a raw body through WKWebView or WebView2, which only the owner's desktop build can (a ~150 MB save and a 250 MB read are the checks worth timing). File-touching commands are `async` ON PURPOSE: sync commands run on the main thread and a folder of multi-MB exports would freeze the window. The toolbar control is `Layout/WorkFolder.tsx` (desktop-only, beside EXPORT; the WEB build renders an inert greyed twin in its place — inlined in `Toolbar.tsx`, not a branch inside `WorkFolder.tsx`, because `__FS_DESKTOP__ && <WorkFolder />` is what keeps that module and its Tauri bridge calls out of the web bundle. It carries `aria-disabled` rather than the `disabled` attribute: a genuinely disabled control doesn't reliably surface its native `title` — WebKit skips the tooltip outright — and the tooltip, which names the desktop app and what the folder does, is the only reason to render the button at all): pick → split Save/caret button; the caret lists the folder (FILE names, newest first, size on the second line). **The folder is an open/save surface, not an export target** — the rules are pure and tested in `utils/workFolderFile.ts`, because `work_folder_write_bytes` replaces unconditionally and the folder has no undo. Loading an entry ADOPTS that file's identity as the shader name (`adoptShaderName`: the authored name is kept when `toKebabCase` says it denotes the same file, so `my-shader.js` stays "My Shader"; the DISK name wins when they diverge, so `waves.js` whose graph is called "Untitled" becomes "waves"). **Opening a `.glb` adopts its FORMAT with its name** (`setExportAsGlb(true)` in `loadEntry`'s glb branch; `effectiveExportFormat` still falls back to the bundle if the restored mesh cannot be packed): the session flag decides `bundleKind`, so without it the next Save wrote a `waves.js`/`.zip` sibling — silently, the folder holding no such name to confirm over — and left `waves.glb` on the pre-edit shader for the next open to load. Save then writes back to the file it was opened from — the tracked name VERBATIM, preserving the on-disk casing that makes the write land on it rather than spawn a lowercase sibling — and the name box is the save-as control: rename the shader and Save forks a new file, which is the only way to make a copy. Save runs the export pre-flight (N1) BEFORE it derives that target, because answering "Export without the 3D model" can turn a `.zip` into a `.js`; cancelling reports "Save cancelled — nothing was written." the way a declined replace does. That "did the user rename it?" test is `toKebabCase` on both sides, never raw strings (`exportShader.shaderBaseName` is the shared derivation, so a predicted name can't drift from the written one): a file name is what identity MEANS here, so "My Shader" → "My  Shader" is not a rename, while re-typing a prettier spelling of an adopted stem would otherwise take the save-as branch back to the file it was already on. The flip side is inherent to deriving names by kebab and cannot be fixed in the rules — `toKebabCase` is many-to-one, so **"Zīle" and "Zāle" are one file** (`z-le.js`) — which is why a save that would land somewhere OTHER than the tracked file lists the folder first and `window.confirm`s when that name already exists. **The provenance of the document on screen is therefore THREE states, not a nullable file name** (`DocOrigin`), because two of them are indistinguishable if you only track a name: `file` (loaded from / last written to it — writing back is the point and stays silent), `new` (NEW built a blank document, which resolves to `my-shader.js` via `DEFAULT_SHADER_NAME` often enough that Save must look before replacing), and `null` — unknown provenance, a session restored from the autosave or an import from another surface — which writes the derived name SILENTLY, exactly as it did before any of this existed. That last one is a deliberate limit rather than an oversight: the app cannot tell the user's own file from a stranger's there, so prompting would fire on the first save of every launch, which is noise, not safety. The listing is fetched only on the asking branches, so ordinary re-saves pay nothing for it; on confirm the write uses the spelling already on disk. Four things ride on this. The origin is COMPONENT state, not a store field (one reader, one writer, the control never unmounts on desktop, and the web build never mounts it at all). It is dropped on `fs:graph-imported` and set to `new` on **`fs:graph-new`** — a dedicated event `newGraph()` dispatches, deliberately NOT a reuse of `fs:graph-imported`, which would arm NodeEditor's import auto-fit on top of the one `startNewShader` already schedules. Both listeners are guarded by a `loadingRef`, because `announceGraphImport` runs SYNCHRONOUSLY inside `importShaderText` and would otherwise clear the very origin the load is about to set. Re-tracking happens ONLY in the write's success path AND only if an `originEpoch` counter is unchanged across the awaited write: claiming a file that was never written — or one belonging to a shader some other surface imported during the await — would aim the next Save at whatever really is at that name. And `newGraph()` also resets `shaderName` (see the NodeEditor bullet); the reset alone is NOT what severs the tracking, and assuming it was is the trap — a document already AT the default name kebabs to the same file, so the tracked target survives the reset and only the event breaks the link. **A kind flip forks the pair**: adding an image turns `waves.js` into `waves.zip`, and switching the EXPORT popover's Format to one `.glb` makes it `waves.glb`; there is no delete command in the IPC surface, so the stale sibling stays; the Save tooltip names the exact target so at least the flip is visible before it happens. The row still shows ONE name — it used to lead with the AUTHORED name and repeat the file name underneath, and `waves.js` whose graph said "Untitled" read as an unrelated shader; loading now closes that divergence at the SOURCE, so the example survives only for foreign or hand-renamed files, where the disk name wins and the authored name is overwritten on the next save (not undoable — `shaderName` is absent from `snapshotOf`). `display_name` is no longer dead: it is the exact answer to "did THIS file supply a name?", which the store cannot give (`applyProjectToStore` skips an empty `shaderName`, so a post-import read cannot tell "the file said nothing" from "the previous shader was called this" — which degraded "My Shader" to "my-shader" on the most ordinary flow there is, reopening the shader you just saved). The `MAX_NAME_SCAN_BYTES` scan behind it therefore stays, and the store before/after diff is only the fallback for zips and oversized `.js`. `src/autosave.rs` — the file-backed home of `fs:graph`/`fs:savedGroups` in the desktop build (GLB Phase 6, S6), because localStorage holds ~5.24M characters per origin and a desktop project may carry ~32M of images. Layout under `app_data_dir()/autosave/`: `graph.json` + `graph.prev.json` and `saved-groups.json` + `saved-groups.prev.json` (a one-deep backup rotated on every write), `*.corrupt-<ms>.json` (quarantined, never read back, never deleted) and `images/<sha256>.txt` (content-addressed `data:` URLs). Rust hashes every image it stores and re-verifies the hash on every read; the webview never names a file, only a closed slot vocabulary (`graph`/`savedGroups`, the `x-fs-slot` header on a RAW write body) and 64-hex digests. A document whose `"imageRef":"fsimg-<hex>"` keys name an image that is not on disk is refused (`E_MISSING_IMAGE <digest>`; the frontend forgets that digest and puts the image again once) — the EXACT key the webview writes (`IMAGE_REF_TOKEN`, pinned by `desktopIpcContract.test.ts`), never any string that merely ends in `fsimg-<hex>`: a note's text or a group name spelling one used to refuse every later save of that document, silently. GC keeps the broad `"fsimg-` scan, where a false positive only keeps a file. **Every write stages under its own name** (`<name>.<pid>-<seq>.tmp-fastshaders`, `staging_path`): async commands run concurrently and the graph and the library can put the SAME new image at once — with one shared staging name one call removed or renamed the other's file (measured: one of two concurrent puts failed with `E_IO` in 40 of 40 rounds, and the loser's rename fallback could delete the image the winner had just committed). `store_image` also takes a destination that already hashes to the digest as success and never removes it. GC keeps every image any `.json` in the directory names — the backup and a quarantined file included — and a `.json` it cannot read aborts the pass before anything is deleted. **It also keeps every digest THIS PROCESS has stored** (`StoredImages`), and that is not belt-and-braces: GC reads the documents in one loop and deletes in a SECOND, the two commands run on separate async workers, and the webview's gate cannot serialize them because `desktopAutosaveBoot.ts` BOUNDS its `gc()` call and a pass that merely takes too long opens the gate exactly as a finished one does. A put committing between the loops named an image no document mentioned yet, GC deleted the file, `autosave_write`'s `E_MISSING_IMAGE` check had already passed, and the document committed a ref whose pixels were gone — a dangling ref at the next boot and nothing else said so. `store_image` CLAIMS its digest before it touches a file and `gc_dir` consults the set INSIDE the same critical section as its `remove_file`, so the two serialize. The staging sweep is deliberately NOT claim-guarded: a live `<digest>.txt.<pid>-<seq>` swept mid-`stage` fails the rename with `E_IO`, which surfaces as the `autosave-file-failed` notice and rewrites on the next edit — loud and recoverable, where collecting a committed image was silent and permanent. main.rs intercepts `CloseRequested` on the MAIN window and a user-initiated `ExitRequested { code: None }` (Cmd+Q, the last window closing), emits `fs:autosave-flush`, and waits up to 3 s for `autosave_close_ready` before it destroys the window or exits (`request_flush`/`finish_close`; programmatic exits carry `Some` and pass). File commands are `async`; `autosave_close_ready` is sync because it touches no file. Capabilities stay bare `core:default` (`core:event:default`, which the listen needs, is part of it). The boot (`store/desktopAutosaveBoot.ts`) reads both documents before the seed: a document that does not parse is quarantined and the backup read, silently; a REJECTED read pauses file writes for the session and raises `autosave-file-read-failed`, since an unreadable file must never be overwritten — and so does a slot where a document EXISTED and none parsed (current corrupt and the backup corrupt or absent, or a 0-byte current beside a corrupt backup; its `{reason}` is the one the boot words itself, translated), because migrating silently would show a months-old localStorage copy as the session and the second write would rotate the unreadable backup away; ONLY both files absent → the localStorage copies are the one-time migration source, left in place and never written again; and writes start only after the seed (`start()`), dropping whatever a mount effect's store change left pending, so the first write is the seeded graph and never an empty store rotated over the real one — then re-persisting the library when it was migrated OR when the load had to repair it — an image stripped (N8) or an Output material trimmed (`outputSectionsTrimmed`, decision 9 — the field keeps its historical name), the two conditions of the web's own write-back in `seedFromStored` (App's own write-back of a repaired library is captured while writes are off and dropped by that same step, so without the re-persist the file kept its dangling ref or its over-cap sections and the saved-groups notice fired on every launch). The boot overlay stops the pointer only — NodeEditor's shortcuts listen on `window` — so App's desktop seed clears `past`/`future` after seeding: a node added by keyboard during the read pushed an undo entry holding the EMPTY pre-seed store, and the first Cmd+Z would have restored and autosaved it. A failed write raises `autosave-file-failed` once per failure streak. `cargo test` covers the pure file-system half (`is_digest`, `slot_files`, the SHA-256 vectors, `ref_digests` and the narrower `image_ref_digests`/`missing_image`, `is_image_payload`, `write_atomic`'s rotation, `stage`'s planted-link refusal, unique staging names, `store_image` incl. two threads putting one digest, `gc_dir` keep/delete) in a temp dir; the IPC bodies, the window/quit events (Cmd+Q raising `ExitRequested { code: None }` on Tauri 2.11 is the one unverified hook) and the real app data folder need the owner's desktop build. `src/bench_server.rs` — the LAN bench server (`bench_server_start/stop/status` commands; GET-only, path-sanitized tiny_http file server on 0.0.0.0:5199, ephemeral-port fallback) that serves the bundled ShaderCarousel to headsets on the local network; the suite is staged into `src-tauri/carousel-dist/` (gitignored) by the vite desktop profile and bundled via `tauri.conf.json`'s `bundle.resources`. The frontend reaches the commands through `withGlobalTauri` (`window.__TAURI__`, typed in `vite-env.d.ts`) — only ever touched behind `__FS_DESKTOP__` (the Toolbar "VR" popover, which also documents the WebXR/WebGPU secure-context workarounds: Quest Browser insecure-origins flag or `adb reverse`; plain HTTP is deliberate — a self-signed cert would interstitial anyway). `dragDropEnabled: false` in `tauri.conf.json` is REQUIRED — without it the OS-level drop handler swallows HTML5 drag-drop and every file-import surface silently dies. App version comes from `package.json` via `tauri.conf.json`'s `"version": "../package.json"`. Releases: `.github/workflows/release.yml` triggers on `v*` tags (flow: `npm version patch && git push --follow-tags`), tests (`npm test`, then the crate's `cargo test` on each desktop leg after its build and before any upload), builds a macOS-universal `.dmg` + Windows NSIS installer + portable `.zip` (exe + the ShaderCarousel resource folder — a bare exe would break the VR bench, since Windows `resource_dir()` is the exe's directory) on native runners, deploys the web app to gh-pages in lockstep (the `web` job is gated on `desktop` so a failed binary build never leaves the site advertising unpublished binaries), and uploads assets under FIXED names (`FastShaders-macOS.dmg`, `FastShaders-Windows-Setup.exe`, `FastShaders-Windows-Portable.zip`) — the toolbar's "Download app" dropdown and the web build's desktop-app note link to `/releases/latest/download/<name>`, so keep `DESKTOP_DOWNLOADS` in `src/utils/desktopDownloads.ts` in sync with the workflow's asset names (pinned by `desktopDownloads.test.ts`).
- `Tests/` — test shader JS files + test HTML page

## Key Conventions

- **The supported browser floor is Chrome 111 / Safari 16.4 / Firefox 126, and each engine is held there by a DIFFERENT feature** (measured 2026-09-08 against the built `dist/`, not inferred from caniuse). `build.target` is `esnext` with no polyfills and nothing transpiled down, so the floor is simply the newest thing actually used — which means it MOVES the first time someone reaches for a newer CSS function or JS syntax, silently, with a green build. The three that set it today: **`color-mix()`** (CostBar.css, Chrome 111 / Safari 16.2 / Firefox 113) — the newest CSS in the codebase and the ONLY thing holding Chrome that high, but it carries an explicit `rgba()` literal on the line above, so below 111 the app is whole and a drop-target tint is flat; **ES2022 class static blocks**, which `monaco-editor` 0.55 ships and Safari did not take until 16.4 — the one HARD break; and the non-standard **`zoom`** (NodePreviewCard.css / ContentBrowser.css / GraphsPage.css), which Firefox did not implement until 126, below which every asset tile renders at 1/0.67 and is clipped by the strip (that file's head comment explains why no `@supports` fallback was attempted). **The Safari case is the one to remember, because it does not look like a browser problem**: `static {}` appears ONLY in the `monaco` chunk and the editor worker — verified by grepping every built chunk — and CodeEditor is `lazy()`-imported (AppLayout.tsx), so nothing on the boot path needs ES2022. Safari 16.2 therefore boots the app, renders the canvas, and fails when the CODE PANEL is opened. index.html's boot watchdog cannot help there: it speaks only while `#root` is still empty, and React mounted. That watchdog (a bilingual "the browser is too old" note, shown after 15 s and taken back down if a slow boot arrives) is the ONLY enforcement anywhere — nothing feature-detects, deliberately, because every candidate test has browsers where the app boots and stays useful and blocking those would be a regression. The numbers are stated for READERS in three places and are a drift set: index.html's fallback text (bilingual, user-facing), README's Browser support table, and here. `public/eval/index.html` deliberately restates NONE of them — it `location.replace`s to the app, so a study participant on an old browser gets index.html's note by construction, and a fourth copy of the numbers would only rot.
- **Offline/desktop**: the app must run with NO network. Monaco is bundled locally (`CodeEditor/monacoSetup.ts` — `loader.config({ monaco })` + Vite `?worker` workers; never reintroduce the jsdelivr CDN loader) and fonts are self-hosted (the `@fontsource/inter/*.css` + `@fontsource/jetbrains-mono/*.css` weights are imported directly in `main.tsx` AND `nodeEditor.tsx`, and the `fs-fontsource-woff2-only` vite plugin strips the legacy `.woff` fallback `src` from the vendored @fontsource CSS at build time so only the woff2 subsets ship — never reintroduce a jsdelivr/Google-Fonts CDN link). `FS_DESKTOP=1` — set automatically for `tauri dev`/`tauri build` via the CLI's `TAURI_ENV_*` env — switches vite to the desktop profile: base `/`, CSP meta suppressed, ShaderCarousel excluded from dist (it ships instead as a Tauri resource staged in `src-tauri/carousel-dist/`, served over LAN by the in-app bench server — see the src-tauri bullet under Subprojects), and the `__FS_DESKTOP__` define hides the "Download app" button + SC link and shows the "VR" bench popover. The desktop build also raises the size caps — 6M chars per image (the one figure still under the unchanged 8M per-image hard ceiling, which bounds every single payload on both builds), 32M per project and library, a 256 MiB zip read — through `utils/platformCaps.ts` and zipReader's `READ_*` constants. vitest always runs the WEB profile, so desktop values are tested only through the `DESKTOP_CAPS` table or an explicit parameter, and every web cap is pinned unchanged. Preview-iframe asset URLs resolve via `new URL(base + path, location.href)` (`resolveAssetUrl` in `tslToPreviewHTML.ts`) so they survive non-http schemes (tauri://) — don't revert to `location.origin` concatenation.
- **Feedback ("!" toolbar button)**: the red `!` in the toolbar's right cluster opens `Modals/FeedbackModal` — a composer that assembles a plain-text report and hands it to the user's own mail client via `mailto:`, with a clipboard fallback. **Nothing is uploaded, and that is a constraint rather than a preference**: the build CSP is `connect-src 'self' blob: <deploy origin>` + `form-action 'self'` (`vite.config.ts`), so a POST to Formspree / Google Forms / any hosted endpoint is blocked by the app's OWN policy; the desktop build must work with no network at all; and a third-party form would make someone else a data processor for grant-funded research feedback. A `mailto:` navigation is CSP-exempt (navigation, not a fetch) and works in every build. If a server-side endpoint is ever wanted, it needs a `connect-src` widening plus the mailto path kept underneath for the offline case — don't quietly swap one for the other. Report assembly is PURE and tested in `utils/feedbackReport.ts` (the DOM reads live in the modal): the opt-in technical block carries version/build, **graph counts (nodes, how many are connected, connections, groups, notes), cost points vs the selected headset profile, and the preview mesh (built-in geometry name, or a custom model's name + vertex count + size)**, then browser/platform/renderer/display. Counts only — no graph content leaves. Edges are counted on the UNWRAPPED graph (`unwrapCollapsedGroupEdges`), or the same project would report differently depending on which frames happened to be collapsed. `previewMesh.countMeshVertices` (and the compressed-glTF pre-check `inspectGltfCompression`, which reads only the two top-level extension-name arrays) is a **deliberate, narrow exception** to that module's "bytes are never parsed on the trusted side" rule: it counts OBJ `v` lines or reads integers out of a length-capped glTF JSON header — no geometry built, no URI resolved, no buffer touched, every path bounded, every failure returning null. The glTF MODEL reader (`utils/gltfReader.ts`) is the separate, larger documented exception, and the feedback report does not use it. It reports the AUTHORED vertex count, not what THREE ends up with (the OBJ loader emits non-indexed geometry and `fit-bounds` then welds duplicates), so don't reason about GPU cost from it. Also: `MAILTO_SAFE_CHARS` steers over-long reports to "Copy report" because Windows' shell truncates `mailto:` URLs around 2000 chars — a silently truncated bug report is worse than none, since the user believes they sent it. `previewBackend()` RESTATES the `__fsForceWebGL2` rule that `tslToPreviewHTML` emits as source text into the iframe (it can't be imported and called); `feedbackReport.test.ts` extracts that emitted function from the generated HTML, evaluates it, and fails if the two disagree — so a change to the WebKit-forcing rule can't silently make feedback reports lie about which renderer the user was on.
- **Eval mode (`…/eval` — the user-study switch, `src/eval/` + `public/eval/index.html`)**: a research mode for the paper's SUS + telemetry study (design + references in `EVAL_MODE_PLAN.md`; deviations in its §7 are what shipped). The redirector arms a **sessionStorage** flag (`fs:evalArm`, sampled ONCE at module init — never localStorage, or the browser stays in eval mode forever) and drops the previous session record so every `/eval` visit re-runs consent; a mid-session reload of the APP url resumes instead. App-side: `EvalGate` (mounted in App.tsx behind `isEvalMode()`) shows the ConsentModal BEFORE any logging, and Agree adopts the participant code as the SHADER NAME (so the artifact inside the package is `shader/p01.js`, identifiable without opening the zip — set AFTER the clean slate, since `newGraph()` resets the name; an empty code keeps the default) and runs `cleanSlateForStudy()` — `newGraph()` alone is NOT a clean slate: the preview mesh (store + IndexedDB), `fs:previewUniformValues`/`Bounds` (persist BY NAME; auto-names collide, and both ride `buildShaderBundle()` into the study zip) and the undo history (newGraph's own entry = one Cmd+Z from the previous user's document) must all be cleared; `fs:savedGroups` deliberately survives (owner's library — study-machine hygiene is a researcher step). **Telemetry**: `evalLog(type, payload)` — no-op outside a session — called from a REVIEWED chokepoint set pinned by `evalHooks.test.ts` (store graph actions; NodeEditor's `applyConnection`/keydown-delete/`placeTilePayload`; CodeEditor's two Apply gestures — deliberately NOT `requestCodeSync`, which projectImport also calls, so imports would count as Applies; `downloadShader`). telemetry.ts must never import the store (the store imports IT — EvalGate registers a bridge for snapshots and the 250 ms-debounced `preview-rebuild`, since raw `previewCode` advances per scrub frame). Events sit on ONE monotonic clock: the journal persists a wall anchor (first page's `performance.timeOrigin`) and recovery REBASES new timestamps — without it a reload mixes two `performance.now()` epochs and every duration is garbage; recovery also re-logs current visibility/focus (a page that loads visible fires no `visibilitychange`, so the flushed pre-reload `hidden` would otherwise close presence forever), and blur INTO the app's own preview iframe is tagged `target:'iframe'` = still present. The journal is **sessionStorage** (`fs:evalJournal`) — a consent decision: it survives the reload recovery exists for, dies with the tab (the promised withdrawal), and stays out of the fs:graph localStorage quota. **The single-GLB export is never offered in a study session**: the EXPORT popover renders no Format row (the popover itself IS reachable there by right-click), `buildShaderExportChecked` returns the bundle path under `isEvalMode()` before any `.glb` call, the desktop Work folder neither lists nor opens a `.glb`, and SusModal keeps bare `buildShaderBundle()` — so the study zip is unchanged and `CONSENT_TEXT_VERSION` does not move. **EXPORT is the finish control and the action is SUBMIT** — in a study session it opens `EvalFinishModal` ("Are you finished?" → Continue working / Submit) rather than downloading a bare shader, because that is the button a participant reaches for when they think they are done; Submit opens the questionnaire and the ONE package is built at ITS submit, whose thank-you screen OFFERS the addressed email as a button, never opens it (sending from the participant's own client hands the researcher an identifying address, hence opt-in since consent-3; a mailto navigation is CSP-exempt, and the attachment is the one step mailto cannot do, hence the download first + the file named in the body) (the SUS answers belong inside it, and a second pre-questionnaire zip would give the researcher two files per participant that `eval-analysis.mjs` counts twice). The questionnaire opens with FOUR EXPERIENCE QUESTIONS (`background.ts` — Blender / Unreal / other node editors + free text / shader code, one none→expert 0–4 scale) BEFORE the SUS items: they are the covariate a usability score is read against, and asking them first keeps self-assessment from colouring the SUS. Scales required (they gate submit with the ten items), free text optional; they ride `sus.json`'s `background` block — one questionnaire, one file, so there is no new zip entry to disclose, but the sus.json disclosure line and `CONSENT_TEXT_VERSION` move together. The `!` button also opens `SusModal` instead of FeedbackModal (Brooke's original items, item-8 "awkward"; LV draft NOT yet back-translated; all 10 required; score computed into the package and shown NOWHERE the participant reads — including the mailto body); submit assembles the zip (`evalPackage.ts` — sus + raw events + derived summary + quality checks + `buildShaderBundle()` verbatim + README with formulas; `summary.csv` is formula-escaped, participant cell is typed input) → download + an OFFERED prefilled mailto to `EVAL_STUDY_EMAIL` (mailto can't attach) + the upload below, then `clearEvalMode()` — a post-submit reload must boot the NORMAL app, not re-record under the finished identity. **Delivery is THREE-WAY and the download is the floor**: the zip downloads at submit, the addressed mail is offered, AND (since 2026-08-29) the package POSTs to `EVAL_UPLOAD_URL`, the RELATIVE path `/fastshaders-eval/upload.php`, collected at `https://alvismisjuns.lv/fastshaders-eval/list.php` (HTTP-Basic). Relative means it resolves against the serving host, and only alvismisjuns has the endpoint: **from fs.sferas.lv, the study host, it 404s**, and from GitHub Pages it 405s. It is `'self'` under the CSP everywhere, so nothing is blocked; it simply reaches nothing, and both report `'failed'`. Pointing sferas at alvismisjuns needs CORS on upload.php for the OPTIONS preflight AND the POST, `https://alvismisjuns.lv` in the sferas build's `connect-src`, and an absolute URL. A package over `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` (64 MiB, the server template's `$MAX_BYTES`, pinned together in `evalUpload.test.ts`) is refused before any bytes ship and reported as `'too-large'` (decided synchronously by `precheckEvalUpload`, so no "Uploading…" frame). Its line names the size in MiB rounded UP (`formatMiB(…, 'up')`: a round-to-nearest formatter prints 64 beside "accepts up to 64 MB") and then gives the same hand-it-to-the-researcher instructions. `scripts/deploy-eval-endpoint.sh` renders the public `server/*.php` templates with secrets from the gitignored `.vscode/eval-endpoint.json`. The inbox MUST be the CONTAINER path `/app/eval-inbox-<token>` (the site is a Docker `php:8.5-apache` behind Traefik; host `/var/www/alvis/src` → `/app`, DocumentRoot `/app/public`) and MUST sit outside the docroot: measured, an inbox under it served stored packages by URL with no password, because `AllowOverride` is None there (so `.htaccess` is ignored) and Apache runs as the same `www-data` that owns the files (so 0700 is no barrier). Dev needs the serve-only `fs-public-entry-dev-index` vite plugin (SPA fallback otherwise swallows the `/eval/` directory URL that static hosts serve fine). **Three study entries**: `/eval`, **`/evalpro`** (adds `proQuestions.ts`'s professional block — role, years, shader frequency, GPU-budget pressure, platforms, would-you-use — between the experience questions and the SUS; opt-IN via `?pro=on` so an absent parameter never hands a student the block, and its scales gate submit while its free text never does; DRAFT content, one array to replace) and **`/evalp`** — the "pointless" no-cost-feedback arm, which forces `points=off` and removes EVERY point figure (cost bar, node badges, asset-tile badges, the Add-node menu's per-row cost, the shader-settings Total/Budget lines). Stronger than `?costbar=off` on purpose: that hides the total but leaves per-node prices to add up, so `pointsVisible` implies `costBarVisible`. Suppression is ONE CSS sweep on `:root[data-fs-points='off']` (stamped at boot by `applyEvalTaskFlags`) for the badges — a dozen components draw them — plus React gates for the two PROSE figures, where display:none would leave a blank row. DISPLAY ONLY: snapshots keep recording `costPoints` and the package keeps the cost table, or the arm where the participant cannot see the price could not be priced afterwards. Both entries need the serve-only `fs-public-entry-dev-index` plugin in dev (`PUBLIC_ENTRY_DIRS` — the three study arms; the `/textures` unlock that once sat beside them was replaced by the toolbar's optional-categories switch on 2026-09-04). Deployed to **fs.sferas.lv** via `npm run deploy:sferas` (base `/`, docroot `/var/www/fs/src` — NOT `/var/www/sferas/src`, which is a shared directory of unrelated projects). **Experiment identity (2026-08-29)**: `…/eval/?task=T7-fire&budget=200&costbar=off` — the redirector stows the query, `evalTask.ts` parses it once, and it reaches `session.json.task` + a `task-start` event; **`costbar=off` actually hides the CostBar** (the cost-feedback manipulation), and `briefBudget` is deliberately NOT the device budget. `session.json` also carries `costTable` (which price table valued the graph — a point total is meaningless without it) and `device` (cores/GPU/screen/prefs). `budget-crossed` events are edge-triggered from a store subscription, giving `overBudgetMs` for free. `preview.png` is captured at submit for blinded quality rating: the sandboxed stage cannot be read by the parent, so it is ASKED (`fs:shot`) and must re-render in the SAME task before `toDataURL` (no `preserveDrawingBuffer` = cleared at composite), with the reply magic-checked and every failure degrading to no image. The stage also posts throttled `fs:activity` pings, without which input inside the preview iframe is invisible to the parent and long shader inspection idles out of active time. Three SUS-integrity checks ship per package (`sus-in-one-sitting`, `no-edits-after-sus-open`, `response-pattern` — straight-lining always scores exactly 50) as FLAGS, never blocks. Pure/tested: `telemetryModel` (closed vocabulary, active time derived under a DECLARED 60 s idle threshold — recomputable from raw events, deliberately unclamped so the active≤wall quality check stays real; null-proto count maps), `susScore`, `evalPackage`, plus the `evalHooks` source pins. Analysis: `scripts/eval-analysis.mjs` (dependency-free) ingests the study zips → per-participant table + SUS mean/SD/95% t-CI/grade + CSV, recomputing each stored score from its raw items so a corrupt package fails loudly.
- **Sync engine**: `syncSource` field in zustand (`'graph'` | `'code'`) prevents infinite sync loops. `useSyncEngine` hook manages bidirectional sync with `lastSyncedCodeRef` to skip no-op updates
- **Node values**: always use `getNodeValues(node)` from `@/types` — never cast `node.data as ...`
- **Edge IDs**: always use `generateEdgeId(source, sourceHandle, target, targetHandle)` from `@/utils/idGenerator`
- **Single ShaderNode**: one component handles all TSL node types dynamically via registry
- **English is the DEFAULT UI language** (`fs:lang` fallback `'en'`, 2026-09-08). It was Latvian from 2026-08-29, on the reasoning that this is a Latvian research project whose user study runs in Latvian — but the study arms carry their OWN language switch on the consent dialog, so that need is met without starting every other visitor in a language they may not read. A preference stored before the change still wins, so no existing session flips. The toolbar button labels the language it switches TO ("LV" while English is on), so it is an ACTION and carries no `aria-pressed` — a flipping label beside that attribute reads as a contradiction in a screen reader, the same trap the WGSL/GLSL toggle documents. The eval consent dialog carries its own copy of the switch: it is modal, so the toolbar's button sits unreachable behind the backdrop, and a consent form nobody can read in their language is not consent. `i18n.test.ts` pins both the fallback and the action-style label.
- **Latvian i18n is a display-only overlay** (`src/i18n/`): node types, TSL identifiers, generated code, and `data.label` stay canonical English — canvas node headers deliberately show the generated varName (`mul1`), so bilingual "Latviešu (English)" labels appear only where you pick/read about a node (Add-node menu, content browser, tooltips, settings menus, node designer). `language: 'en' | 'lv'` lives in the store, persists to `fs:lang`, stamps `<html lang>`, and is toggled by the Toolbar LV button; missing translations fall back to English. **The two output nodes render their own markup, so they do not get ShaderNode's `portLabel` path for free** — `OutputNode`, `RaymarchOutputNode` and their tile replicas in NodePreviewCard each translate the header (the Latvian node name, `formatNodeLabel(…, false)`), the section labels (`t()`) and the socket labels (`portLabel`); they printed raw English until 2026-09-11, and `outputNodeI18n.test.ts` pins all four files plus a Latvian entry for every label they draw.
- **Node search is RANKED, and `description` is part of the corpus**: `nodeMatchRank` in `nodeRegistry.ts` is the ONE match+order rule, shared by the AddNodeMenu (`searchNodes`) and the ContentBrowser (`rankDefs`) so both surfaces order a query identically. Tiers, lower wins: exact name (label / type / `tslFunction` / Latvian label) → name prefix → name substring → the `Also: …` alias tail → English prose → Latvian prose; `NO_MATCH` filters out. Ties keep registry order (stable sort). This exists because results used to come back in raw registry order, so a node that merely MENTIONED the query in its prose could outrank the node actually named that — writing "speed multiplier" into the Time description put Time above Multiply for "multip". So: keep UI-instruction wording out of `description`, put search aliases in the `Also:` tail (`displayDescription` strips it from the UI), and never let prose repeat another node's name casually. `nodeSearch.test.ts` pins the ordering and sweeps every description for name collisions.
- **Hiding an unfinished node is an ADD-SURFACE filter, never a registry filter** (`registry/editorVisibility.{ts,json}` + node-editor.html's **In editor** checkbox column). The JSON lists what is HIDDEN — default-visible, so a node added tomorrow appears without anyone remembering the file exists; an allow-list would make every new node invisible until someone edited it, which is the failure nobody would think to look for. `NODE_REGISTRY` and `getAllDefinitions()` keep every definition, so a hidden node still resolves for `codeToGraph`, `graphToCode`, `cpuEvaluator`, `loadGraph`, the built-in textures/presets that contain it, the Node Designer (you hide a node precisely so you can finish designing it) and node-editor.html itself — which is where it gets switched back on. Filtering the registry instead would turn every saved `.fastshader` holding that node into `unknown` nodes. **Exactly two accessors carry the filter** — `getEditorDefinitions()` (which `searchNodes()` also ranks over, so the search box can't type a hidden node back into existence) and `isTextureHiddenFromEditor` — plus `categoryEmptiedByHiding()`, which drops a content-browser tab whose every node is now hidden (guarded on "had any addable defs", or hiding anything would delete the def-less Presets/Textures tabs) and which `BROWSER_TABS` must be built from too, or a persisted `fs:assetTab` boots a returning user into a strip that renders nothing. **Hidden means "never OFFERED", not "unreachable"**: the code panel's Monaco completions stay unfiltered (a node's `tslFunction` is usually a real three/tsl symbol — `mod`, `abs`, `mx_noise_float` — and the code panel edits TSL, not the palette), "Convert to Property/Constant" transforms an existing node rather than adding one, and a built-in preset authored with the node still contains it (the overview row says **⚠ in N built-ins** rather than letting the checkbox imply it rewrote shipped artwork). `output` is not hideable — its Add-node row is hardcoded and its tile doubles as "take me to the Output" (the singleton rule, `outputFocus.ts`), so the checkbox is locked with the reason on hover and the endpoint refuses the key. Saves go through the dev-only `POST /__nd/visibility` (the `citations.json` whole-file-rewrite shape; keys validated against `locateRegistryDescriptions`/`locateTextureDescriptions`, deduped + sorted, compare-before-write since the file is imported by `nodeRegistry` and a touch reloads the app through HMR), so this is project SOURCE that ships in the build — deliberately not a per-browser preference, or a shared `.fastshader` would reference a node the recipient can't see the tile for. `editorVisibility.test.ts` proves the filtering with a MOCKED non-empty file (the shipped one was empty until GLB Phase 4, so every direct assertion would have been vacuous) and greps source for the two ways it rots: an add surface built from `getAllDefinitions()`, and any `engine/`/`store/`/`utils/`/`hooks/` module importing the visibility set at all. **The suite must stay green with nodes actually hidden** — `release.yml` runs `npm test` before it builds binaries or deploys, so a test that only holds in the empty shipped state makes the feature unusable in the one way it is meant to be used. **The shipped file is EMPTY again**: `imageNode` was the first (and so far only) node ever listed here, and the owner unhid it on 2026-09-18, so CI is back to the mocked branch of `editorVisibility.test.ts` for the filtering itself (`registry/textureNodeOffered.test.ts` pins that ONE decision — see the Texture-node bullet). Every figure that node moves still follows the FILE rather than a literal: `registry/imageNodeAddable.test.ts` asserts its visibility facts against it, and `contentBrowserVirtual.test.ts`'s boot counts add one when it is unhidden (they were literals, and three tests failed the moment the node was unhidden). So ticking it back on keeps the suite green — checked by running the whole suite with the JSON served unhidden. Three things follow. `getEditorDefinitions()` is computed ONCE at module init, not `filter()`ed per call: every consumer memoizes on its identity, and a per-call array both breaks those memos and hands one render two different "the editor set" arrays (`editorVisibility.test.ts` asserts identity stability under the MOCKED file — the empty state can't see it, since there the filter never runs). The per-browser layer above it — `getEditorDefinitions(hidden)`, the optional categories the user has not switched on — keeps the same rule by memoizing per set (see the optional-categories convention). `nodeSearch.test.ts` ranks `getAllDefinitions()` through `nodeMatchRank` directly rather than through `searchNodes()`, because those pins are about ORDERING and hiding Perlin must not mean "noise" stops ranking Perlin first — one subsequence assertion bridges the two. And `nodeLabelRename.test.ts`'s findability sweep is scoped to the editor set (ranking still checks every def): a hidden node is *meant* to be unfindable.
- **The Texture (Image) node is OFFERED** (2026-09-18, owner: "Why there is no texture node, where I can select from existing?"). It shipped HIDDEN through `editorVisibility.json` while it was unfinished (GLB Phase 4), so the palette, the Add-node menu and the search box did not carry it and the only way to get one was to drop an image FILE — while its picker, the settings menu's **Texture** row, had been able to fill one from every image the project holds since Phase 4 Step 6. Unhiding it is the whole fix: the node was already complete. VERIFIED end to end in Chrome — Shift+A → "texture" lists it (through the `Also: texture, sampler, picture, bitmap` alias tail, second after UV, whose prose also mentions textures), adding it lands an EMPTY node whose slot reads "No image" with the title naming the Texture row (`IMAGE_EMPTY_HINT`), a PBR `.glb` import then builds four Image nodes, and that empty node's Texture row offers all four as thumbnails and fills from one in a single undo entry. The palette is 79 tiles instead of 78. `registry/textureNodeOffered.test.ts` pins this ONE node's visibility, and nothing else: `imageNodeAddable.test.ts` and `contentBrowserVirtual.test.ts` still derive from `isNodeHiddenFromEditor`, so the **In editor** checkbox stays usable for every other node without reddening CI. Re-hiding THIS node is now a deliberate decision that deletes an assertion first. The node's LABEL is still "Image" (renaming it is a registry-source change with search-collision rules of its own), so the palette tile and the menu row say Image while the control that fills it says Texture.
- **Adding a node — one placement path**: right-click canvas, **Shift+A** (opens AddNodeMenu at canvas centre; the search box autofocuses, so Arrow/Enter completes a keyboard-only add), dropping a wire on empty space, dragging a palette tile, or **clicking/Entering a palette tile**. Every tile route funnels through `tileDrag.ts` — `tileActivationProps` → `dropTileAtCanvasCenter` dispatches the SAME `fs-tile-drop` CustomEvent the touch-drag path uses, so `placeTilePayload` (including drop-on-edge splicing) stays the single implementation. Don't add a parallel placement path. **A click/Enter add is SCATTERED off the canvas centre** (`nextTileScatter`, 2026-09-09, owner request): it used to aim at the exact centre, so pressing a tile twice dropped the second node precisely on the first — indistinguishable from nothing having happened, which is how a repeated add reads as broken. Three things are load-bearing. The offset is in SCREEN px, applied before `screenToFlowPosition`, so the visible gap is the same at every zoom (a flow-space offset is invisible zoomed out and a shove zoomed in, and all that matters is that the cards do not sit on each other on screen). The angle steps by the GOLDEN angle plus bounded jitter rather than rolling free — a plain random angle repeats, and two of a handful of rolls landing a few degrees apart is exactly the stacked pair this prevents; the bounded step is always between a quarter and three quarters of a turn from the previous one, so `cos(Δ) <= 0` and two consecutive adds are at least `√2 · SCATTER_MIN_PX` (~34px) apart whatever the radii. And a DRAG is never scattered — the drop position is the user's aim, and nudging it would be the app arguing with a gesture (`tileScatter.test.ts` pins the single call site, since the vitest env is `node` and `dropTileAtCanvasCenter` reads a canvas rect). The AddNodeMenu is deliberately NOT covered: it places at the menu's own point, which for a right-click is where you pointed, and Shift+A's canvas-centre add costs a search and a keystroke per node rather than one repeatable press. **A SINGLETON type is not added twice — the gesture becomes "take me to it"** (`components/NodeEditor/singletonNodes.ts`; today the set is just `soundNode`, the Sound node — see its convention for why). Both add surfaces ask `findSingletonNode` and, on a hit, glide to the existing node through the same `focusNode` every other "take me there" gesture uses, instead of adding. In BOTH the check sits ABOVE the auto-connect that follows it, and for the same reason on each side: NodeEditor's tile path has already previewed a drag-connect plan against a PHANTOM id for a node that is now not going to exist (so it also clears the preview, or the highlight outlives the gesture), and AddNodeMenu may have been opened by dropping a WIRE, where connecting that wire into the pre-existing node would be a connection the user never aimed at. It is an ADD-SURFACE rule, not a graph invariant — a file from before the fold, or a hand-edited one, may still hold two, and nothing deletes one behind the user's back. **Several output nodes may coexist, and exactly ONE is ACTIVE** (2026-09-03; `utils/sdfPartition.ts` `activeSink` — see the several-outputs convention below). The Output's Add-menu row and palette tile simply ADD one (inactive unless it is the graph's first); paste/duplicate keeps Outputs in the clone set and strips the active flag off the clones; nothing redirects any more. (Until then the Output was a singleton and every add surface glided to the existing node; `outputFocus.ts` keeps only the framing helpers — `costFocusId` is the active sink — and `outputFocus.test.ts` now pins the OPPOSITE of the old redirects.) Related: the Output tile's preview socket reads `--node-cost-text` only under `.react-flow` — the token is published on `.node-editor`, which the asset bar also sits inside, so an unscoped read turned the TILE's socket white-on-white on a dark canvas (`outputTargetChip.test.ts`). **AddNodeMenu rows are ONE line — name + cost + category — and the description is the row's `title`**, picked up by the app-wide `TooltipLayer` (which delegates from `document` and borrows any `title`), so the menu is a list you scan and the explanation arrives on hover, in the same place the palette tiles put it. It used to render inline under each row: ~74 nodes each carrying a paragraph, and the widest description (Data Viz, 274 chars) is what forced `.context-menu--add-node`'s explicit width in the first place (now 280px, still explicit so the box doesn't resize itself around whatever the current search returns; `.fs-tooltip` wraps at its own 260px). The tail-stripping `displayDescription` is used here now — the inline line printed the `Also: …` search aliases. **Hover must NOT scroll the list**: `onMouseEnter` sets `focusedIndex` too, and the `scrollIntoView` that keyboard navigation needs fired a `scroll` event, which is one of TooltipLayer's dismiss triggers — it landed inside the 1 s dwell of the tooltip the same hover had just armed, and with the pointer then at rest no further `mouseover` re-armed it, so any row clipped by the list's bottom edge could never show its description at all (measured: scrollTop 47 → 66, tooltip never appeared). `scrollOnFocusRef` gates the scroll to keyboard moves and list swaps; `hoverFocus()` is the pointer path. Arrowing a row still shows no tooltip — arrow keys move `focusedIndex`, not DOM focus, so `focusin` never fires. `.context-menu__item--stacked` survives for DataRangeSettingsMenu's hint rows (the modifier exists because `.context-menu__item` is a shared single-row flex used by every submenu). **A press anywhere else CLOSES the menu** (`ContextMenu.tsx`, 2026-09-09, owner request). Until then the only closer was NodeEditor's `onPaneClick`, so the menu shut on bare canvas and stayed open for everything else — most obviously a click on a NODE, which is where a hand goes straight after opening that node's settings. It is **`pointerdown` in the CAPTURE phase**, for two independent reasons: React Flow's node handlers stop `click` propagation for their own gestures, so a bubble-phase click listener never hears the press that matters; and a press that becomes a DRAG produces no click at all while being unambiguously "I am doing something else now". It cannot eat its own opening press — the effect is armed only while `open`, and a right-click's pointerdown precedes the `contextmenu` that opens the menu. Two exemptions: the menu's own subtree (`ref.current.contains`), and `DISMISS_EXEMPT` — the popovers a settings menu itself opens (`.palette-pop`, `.mesh-picker__pop`), which portal to `document.body` and are therefore OUTSIDE the menu's subtree, so without the exemption picking a colour closed the menu the swatch belongs to. `onPaneClick` stays: closing twice is idempotent and it also clears the label peek, which this must not touch. Lives on the DISPATCHER beside the Escape handler, so every menu type gets both at once; `contextMenuDismiss.test.ts` pins the phase, the arming, both exemptions and that the two exempt classes are the ones those popovers really use.
- **One node, one look — the CANVAS is the reference.** A node is drawn on four surfaces: the graph canvas (`nodes/*`, the live implementation and the thing every other surface must match), the asset-browser tiles (`NodePreviewCard`), the node-editor.html overview (the same cards, plus a `GraphModal` that renders the REAL canvas components), and the Node Designer's stage (`NodeVisual` via `nodeDesigner/bridge`). Only the `shader` flow type gets there through the shared `NodeVisual` replica; `color`/`preview`/`mathPreview`/`clock`/`mic`/`output`/`raymarchOutput` each need a hand-written card, and **every hand-written card is a drift site**. The rules that keep them honest: (1) **a node's styling must never depend on which page draws it** — the `.react-flow .drag-num` opaque number plate was the live instance (identical widget, crisp on the canvas and washed-out on every tile; the designer only escaped by hand-mirroring the rule in `node-designer.html`), so the on-node look now sits on `.drag-num--compact` itself, which IS the on-node variant. Same shape as the `.mic-node`-scoped arm light. `.react-flow .node-base__cost-badge` is the one DELIBERATE exception (documented in NodeBase.css: the asset bar is outside the canvas and keeps the cost-gradient colour). (2) **Shared geometry/drawing beats a copied replica**: `soundGeometry.ts` (mic box + socket rows), `utils/clockFace.ts` (the Time face — the card used to hold a second copy that seeded its hand from `Date.now()`, so the tile pointed somewhere a freshly dropped node never does; it draws phase 0 now), `COLOR_NODE_SIZE` + `LABEL_MAX/MIN_PX` (the colour swatch, which had an 84/2/15px twin of the canvas's 56/1.5/10px, reverse-engineered against the strip's `zoom: 0.67` — the card now renders the REAL `.color-node` classes counter-scaled by `CARD_COLOR_SCALE`, which also got it the long-name wrap/clip/fit-text rules its twin silently lacked). (3) **A `<div>` replica cannot match `:disabled`** — the inert mic light needs the explicit `--inert` class or a tile shows enabled-green beside a grey unwired canvas node. (4) **Fake handles read the real tokens** (`--handle-size`/`--handle-border`): hardcoded 10px went wrong on touch, where tokens.css bumps the real socket to 12px. (5) **Every flow type needs a dispatch branch** — `output` had none and fell through to the generic shader card, which turned a 7-input def into seven editable number boxes; the overview showed that beside a `GraphModal` rendering the real Output node. Deleting a replica is the best fix available: the slider tile's hand-built track/thumb/labels are gone, and it renders the same `<input type="range">` the canvas and designer do. (6) **A node title is capped at TWO rows, it wraps at ONE balanced seam, and the node stays at its designed width.** Three rules do that together (2026-09-03, owner request with a mock: "wrap names to max 2 lines if necessary, making the nodes slimmer"). `.node-base__title` is `overflow-wrap: normal` (never split a word) plus a 2-line clamp; the authored designer width is applied as the PREFERRED width with `min-width: min-content` as the floor on EVERY surface (ShaderNode and NodeVisual alike — the cards' old floor-only branch and NodeVisual's `exactWidth` prop are gone); and `nodes/NodeTitle.tsx` is the ONE renderer of `.node-base__title`, giving the name exactly one break opportunity at the most balanced seam `titleSplit.ts` finds — a space, the character after `_`/`-`, or a camelCase boundary (Unicode-aware) — with every other space made non-breaking. The canvas header is the GENERATED VAR NAME, a single unbreakable run, so before this `cameraPosition1` forced a 47px design out to ~95px to sit on one line (and with break-word it had shattered into `camera`/`Position`/`1`); now it wraps as "camera / Position1" and the floor is the longer half. MEASURED: cameraPosition1 49px/2 rows, positionWorldDirection1 73px/2 rows ("positionWorld / Direction1"), mul1 47px/1 row; tiles agree (Camera Position 47px/2 rows, Cross Product 48px/2, Greater Than 45px/2). Digits stay on the fragment before them, so a lone `1` can never be a row. Every header keeps its `title` so a clamped name stays readable on hover; `titleSplit.test.ts` pins the seam rule and greps that no node component renders the title span by hand. TRAP: the pure module is `titleSplit.ts`, not `nodeTitle.ts` — on this case-insensitive filesystem a file differing from `NodeTitle.tsx` only by case fails `tsc` AND makes Vite resolve the extensionless `./NodeTitle` import to the `.ts` file, a 404 on every importer's cached transform after the rename until each importer is touched. (7) **The asset-card layer may SCALE a node, never RESTYLE one** — and this is the rule the hand-written cards actually broke. `NodePreviewCard.css` used to carry `.node-preview-card .node-base:not(--exact) { min-width: 100px }` plus header/badge/row overrides, to draw those cards "larger"; once `FitNodeHeading` normalized heading size that was pure distortion, and on a FIXED-geometry node it was a lie: the Sound node is exactly `MIC_W` wide (80px then, 88 since it grew the source picker), so a 100px card frame left its 77px body 20px short of the right edge — all four output sockets floated INSIDE the frame and the arm light sat off centre, beside a canvas node whose sockets are on the border (Time and Sine were widened past their own content the same way). Two mechanisms replace it. Each card passes the LIVE node's class to `CardShell` (`nodeClassName="clock-node"` …) so the node's own stylesheet reaches the card, and it renders the node's REAL structural classes (`.preview-node__canvas`, `.math-preview-node__port-row`, `.clock-node__canvas-wrap`) instead of the retired `__canvas--clock/--math/--noise` twins. And every card socket is `CardSocket` — NodeVisual's `StaticHandle` element, i.e. the live handle classes placed in the live wrapper — so `.clock-node__canvas-wrap > .react-flow__handle` centres the tile's socket on the clock FACE exactly as it does on the canvas; the old `--right-abs`/`--left-abs` twins hardcoded `top: 50%` of the whole card, which is why the Time tile's socket sat half a header-height below the node's. `assetCardGeometry.test.ts` pins all of it from source (no browser needed): no geometry declaration in the card stylesheet may reach a node-internal class, no `<CardShell>` may omit `nodeClassName`, the card-local handle classes may not come back, and every non-`shader` flow type must still have a dispatch branch. Measured after the fix (Chrome, card vs canvas node, frame-normalized): mic/Time/Perlin/Sine/Color all agree on aspect ratio and socket placement to within a rounding pixel. (8) **A surface may scale a card only from a WRAPPER around it** — node-editor.html's overview draws these same tiles at roughly canvas size with `zoom: var(--gp-preview-zoom)` on `.gp__preview`, which MULTIPLIES `.node-preview-card`'s own `zoom: 0.67` (1.5 × 0.67 ≈ 1) instead of replacing it. Overriding that 0.67 is what breaks, and it breaks one tile only: `CARD_COLOR_SCALE` is a RATIO against the card's zoom, not an absolute swatch size, so it rides an ancestor factor exactly like every other tile and is stranded compensating for a zoom that no longer applies — the Color / Property Color tiles end up the one pair in the set at the wrong size, with nothing in CSS to say why (re-deriving the constant per surface makes it worse: it would pin the swatch at 56px while its neighbours grew). `assetCardGeometry.test.ts` fails on a `zoom` declaration aimed at `.node-preview-card`/`.saved-group-card` from `GraphsPage.css`, and its node-internal geometry sweep now runs over that stylesheet too — SCALING a card is allowed from anywhere, reaching INTO one is the same defect wherever it is written.
- **Double-click (or double-tap) a node and EVERY one of its socket labels stays up** (`components/NodeEditor/labelPeek.ts`, pure + tested; the class goes on React Flow's node WRAPPER, the `fs-menu-active` idiom, and `.fs-labels-shown .typed-handle[data-tooltip]::after` in TypedHandle.css is the fourth trigger for the label the socket already has — same placement, never a second kind of bubble). A socket name is otherwise a one-at-a-time thing: you hover a dot and read one bubble, which is the wrong shape for the question people ask of an unfamiliar node — the whole port list — and unanswerable on touch, which has no hover. **It is built on CLICK, not `dblclick`**: a double TAP does not reliably synthesize a `dblclick`, which is exactly why the Color node carries a long-press beside its own `onDoubleClick`, so counting two clicks on the same id inside `DOUBLE_ACTIVATE_MS` (400) is what makes one implementation serve both pointers; clicks on two DIFFERENT nodes are never a pair however fast they arrive, and a backwards clock cannot pair an ancient click. Anything that already owns a double-click keeps it — `PEEK_EXEMPT_SELECTOR` (the number box's click-to-edit, a textarea's caret, `input`/`select`/`button`) and `PEEK_EXEMPT_NODE_TYPES` (the Color node, whose whole body IS its swatch and which has one socket to name). One node at a time, and three ways out: the same gesture again, Escape, or a click on empty canvas — the last two matter because touch has no Escape and on a dense graph the node you peeked may be under its own labels. **This is deliberately NOT the thing that was tried and rejected**: pinning every label while a node is SELECTED drowned the card in text (the note above `.typed-handle--tapped`), because selection happens constantly and as a side effect of dragging; a gesture that is asked for and put away is a different proposition. State is LOCAL to NodeEditor and applied imperatively — looking at a node's ports is not an edit, so it must never push through history, the autosave or a shared file.
- **A socket's label has ONE placement, whatever made it appear** (`handles/TypedHandle.css`, audited 2026-09-03). The name-and-type label (`data-tooltip`, the instant light bubble) shows on four triggers — pointer hover, a touch tap, the drag-reveal while a wire hunts nearby (`reveal`), and the drag-connect preview (`.fs-connect-socket`) — and used to land in two places depending on which: hover and tap ABOVE the dot, the two drag states BESIDE it, so the same label jumped as the gesture changed and read as two tooltips. Placement is now a property of the SOCKET: beside it on its outer side — left of a left-edge input, right of a right-edge output, above/below the Color node's perimeter output when it rides the top or bottom — centred on the dot, outside the card, keyed on React Flow's `.react-flow__handle-{left,right,top,bottom}` classes. The triggers decide only WHEN it shows and its emphasis (connect adds the arrow and border). Every label is in FRONT: the reveal's old `z-index: -1` bought nothing (a label 6px off the dot never covers it, and the dragged wire lives in React Flow's connection layer, whose order against the nodes layer no z-index inside a handle can change). MEASURED in Chrome over Float/mul/Mic/Colormap/Output/Color sockets and a collapsed-group pill: hover, reveal and connect all report the label beside the dot, 4px gap, dy 0, no clipping ancestor. Two other tooltip classes stay deliberately distinct from this one: PROSE (`title` → the app-wide TooltipLayer's dark bubble, 200 ms dwell — node headers carry the var name this way, chips their explanation), and asset-tile hover cards. A `title` on a `pointer-events: none` element is DEAD (TooltipLayer resolves its host from `elementFromPoint`), and two were: the collapsed group pill's socket label (removed — the socket beside it already carries the name) and the Colormap ramp (removed — the rows region overlays the strip, so even a hit-testable strip never receives the pointer; the name is one right-click away in the node's settings). Related: hovering a socket after a drag or wire-drop works — an earlier probe that said otherwise had dragged the node under the bottom chrome — and React Flow's own auto-pan near the pane edge slides a drag-connect target away from the held node, which is why a ring can vanish at rest only near the canvas edge.
- **F frames the selection** (`focusNodes` in `outputFocus.ts`, bound beside Shift+A in NodeEditor): the "view selected" key of every node editor, through the SAME animated, zoom-capped glide (`OUTPUT_FOCUS_FIT`) the Output tile, the Add-node row and the cost pill use — `focusNode` is now its single-node case — so every "take me there" gesture lands identically. A selected member hidden in a collapsed group frames the pill standing in for it (`focusTargets` maps each id through `outputFocusTarget`, dedupes, drops stale ids). With NOTHING selected it frames the whole graph through `glideFitAll` — the SAME call the canvas bar's ⤢ button makes, so the two cannot land differently — because a key that silently does nothing reads as broken. **Every control that moves the view glides the same way** (2026-09-16, owner: "keep zooming style consistent"): `VIEW_GLIDE_MS` (500) in outputFocus.ts is the one duration for the cost pill, F, and the canvas bar's −/+/fit, which used to SNAP because React Flow's `zoomIn`/`zoomOut`/`fitView` take no duration by default; all of them then share d3's cubic-in-out. The −/+ buttons go through `glideZoom` → `zoomTo`, NOT `zoomIn({ duration })`: React Flow scales the CURRENT transform, so a second press mid-glide interrupts the first at its half-finished zoom and two quick presses land well short of two steps (and an interrupted d3 transition never resolves its promise, so that cannot be used to know when a glide ended). `zoomStepTarget` steps from the pending glide's TARGET instead — MEASURED, three presses 80 ms apart land on exactly 1.2³ — and the pending target is dropped whenever the user drives the view: React Flow's own wheel/drag through `onMoveStart`'s non-null event, and by hand on the three paths that call `setViewport` directly (trackpad wheel pan, double-tap drag, two-finger touch), which `outputFocus.test.ts` counts. A bare key, so it must never fire while typing: INPUT/TEXTAREA (Monaco's input is a textarea), SELECT (the Audio node's picker lives on the canvas) and contentEditable are skipped, and any modifier bails so Cmd/Ctrl+F stays the browser's find. `outputFocus.test.ts` covers the helper and source-pins the binding and its guards; MEASURED: F on a selected Output lands it at the canvas centre at zoom 1.5, F on nothing fits every node inside the canvas, F in the name box types an "f" and moves nothing. **A selects every visible node** (`selectAll.ts`, bound in the same handler under the same guards): Blender's key beside Shift+A and F. "Visible" excludes members hidden inside a collapsed group — the pill stands in for them, and selecting invisible nodes would let Delete remove what nobody could see was selected — but includes frames and notes (React Flow skips a child whose parent is also dragged, so frame plus members drag once). Pressed with everything already selected it DESELECTS all, the only modifier-free way back out (Cmd/Ctrl+A stays the browser's). It dispatches React Flow `select` changes through the store's `onNodesChange` — the marquee's own path — so it is a selection change, not an edit: no history entry, no autosave, no resync, and Cmd+Z after it undoes the previous real edit. MEASURED: 0/3 → A → 3/3 → A → 0/3, a partial 1/3 → A → 3/3, A in the name box types an "a", Cmd+A and Shift+A untouched.
- **Surface parity is MEASURED, not assumed** (`nodes/nodeVisualParity.test.ts`; the sweep script drops every designable type on node-editor.html and selects it on node-designer.html, then diffs sockets, widgets, rows, sizes and glyph markup — 2026-09-03, owner: "in node-designer some nodes look different than in the node overview, for example Data Stripes"). 15 of 67 designable types differed, in four classes, each a way a replica came to depend on the page drawing it. (1) **The opt-in ramp filter was applied by the CARD, not by the replica** — the designer's bridge passed the raw definition, so Data Stripes / Data Viz showed `lowColor`/`highColor` there as NUMBER rows. `effectiveRampDef(def, [])` now runs INSIDE NodeVisual (a replica is a fresh node: nothing exposed) and NodePreviewCard's copy is gone. (2) **The CANVAS was wrong too, worse**: ShaderNode's rows layout filtered through `effDef` but its operator branch (`effectiveInputs(def…)`, `opSocketCount`) and the rows layout's DETACHED-socket loop (`def.inputs.map` for designer-moved sockets — Data Stripes' designer moved every socket, so this is the loop that drew it) read the raw `def`, so a freshly dropped Data Stripes carried all three input sockets with the two colours drawn as number boxes (NaN for a hex). Both read `effDef` now, and a COLOUR operand in either place gets the swatch the colour row gets (the interactive picker only on the designer stage, the inert span elsewhere — `colorPickerHistory.test.ts` pins three sites per file). Measured after: fresh canvas = card = designer at `signal` only; ticking Low Color in the settings menu mounts the socket with a real #1b2a4a swatch and no extra number box. (3) **The card inherited the PAGE's font size** — canvas 14px (app body), overview card 10px, designer 13px (its own body rule) — and the number widgets, sized from their container, came out 30px on a card and 32px on the stage for the same node; `.node-base` now sets `font-size: var(--font-size-md)` itself. (4) **The Slider's range input kept its UA 2px margin** on the designer page (no reset.css there), 4px bigger than the canvas; `.shader-node__slider` zeroes it. Also aligned: the designer previewed an input with no registry default as `0.1` where the canvas and card show `0` (2px wider Vec nodes) — `previewFor` seeds `0` now. What legitimately still differs: the designer wraps BUILT-IN art (nodes with no saved svg — sqrt, exp, log2, round, fract, clamp, greaterThan, lessThan, equal) in one extra empty `<g>`, which paints identically; and the canvas header is the var name (one row) where cards and the designer show the label (`Data Stripes`, two rows), so a card can be a header-row taller than its canvas node by design. PROBE TRAPS worth keeping: `getBoundingClientRect` on a card is scaled by the strip's `zoom` and FitNodeHeading's transform — compare `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`; and the overview keys rows by `.gp__type`, the designer's `#nodeSel` options by value.
- **Renaming a node in the Node Designer** (the inspector's **Name** / **Name (LV)** fields): a node's DISPLAY LABEL is editable; its `type` is not, and the Type row carries a `locked` chip saying why — `type` is the registry key, the `registryType` stored in every saved `.fastshader`, and what `codeToGraph` matches on, so renaming it would orphan every existing graph. The label is safe precisely because it is display-only: no engine module reads `def.label` (pinned by `nodeLabelRename.test.ts`, which greps the ten engine files), it reaches no generated code and no persisted payload, and the Latvian overlay is keyed by TYPE so an English rename can't orphan a translation. **Names are registry SOURCE, so they do NOT ride `customGlyphs.ts`** — English lives in `nodeRegistry.ts`'s `label`, Latvian in `node-i18n.json`'s `nodes` map, and they travel their own patch path: `locateRegistryLabels` (the `label` half of `descriptionSplice.ts`, sharing its two-form locator — property + the unary-math `[type, label, description]` tuple, label at index 1) plus `POST /__nd/labels`, which splices by BYTE RANGE so a rename is a one-line diff in a hand-formatted file. **Label and description slots are both keyed by node type and must never be merged into one `slots` array** — `spliceDescriptions` indexes them into a `Map`, so a merged list would collapse each pair and let a label patch rewrite a description's bytes (`nodeLabelRename.test.ts` asserts the ranges never overlap). Save tiers differ from the glyph file's: dev endpoint → linked folder (the same pure splice run in the browser behind a dynamic `import()`, so `@babel/parser` stays out of the designer's initial load) → **no download tier**, because a built page holds `nodeRegistry.ts` only as a compiled module and has no source text to splice; the fields disable themselves and say so. `assertValidNodeLabels` validates the map the registry would hold AFTER the patch, not the patch alone — uniqueness is a whole-file property (`nodeMatchRank` tiers an exact name hit above everything, so two nodes sharing a label make that tier ambiguous), compared case-insensitively because the ranker lowercases.
