- **Scalar→vec3 widening on Output channels**: a 1-channel source wired to a vec3-typed channel (`color`, `emissive`, `normal`, `position`) is emitted as `vec3(ref)`, NOT bare. three's `NodeMaterial.setupDiffuseColor` does `vec4(this.colorNode)` and TSL splats a 1-channel node across all FOUR components, so a bare `mx_noise_float(...)` on Color silently became the ALPHA too (`diffuseColor.a = noise`) — the surface flickered/vanished as if Opacity or Discard were wired when neither was. Scalar-ness comes from `getNodeOutputShape` (cpuEvaluator), not the registry port type, so an `any`-typed arithmetic chain is caught as well. codeToGraph MUST mirror this: `unwrapScalarWiden` collapses a single-argument `vec3(<identifier|member>)` in a channel position back to a direct edge — unambiguous because a real Vec3 node always emits three arguments — or the round trip grows a Vec3 node with y/z = 0 and re-emits `vec3(x, 0, 0)`, turning a grey ramp red. Both sides are pinned (`graphToCode.test.ts` + a `roundTrip.test.ts` case). Vec3-shaped sources emit byte-identically to before.
- **Unknown nodes**: unrecognized TSL functions in code→graph parsing create `unknown` nodes that store `functionName` and `rawExpression` in values. These round-trip through graphToCode (emitted verbatim), render as magenta `vec3(1,0,1)` fallback in live preview, and show as orange warnings (not errors) in the code editor. The `unknown` definition lives in `NODE_REGISTRY` but is excluded from `allDefinitions` (hidden from content browser/search)
- **Preview geometries**: five options — `sphere`, `cube`, `plane` (A-Frame primitives), `teapot` (tessellated IN the preview document from Bézier patches — see the runtime-teapot convention) and `bunny` (the one remaining OBJ model file, `public/models/`). Three predicates in `tslToPreviewHTML.ts` are the source of truth: `isObjGeometry()` (bunny), `isTeapotGeometry()`, and `isModelGeometry()` (obj + `custom` — the geometries that ride the model feed, rebuild the iframe on change and HIDE the subdivision slider; the teapot is deliberately NOT one). The inline `fit-bounds` A-Frame component recomputes vertex normals (after merging vertex duplicates so per-vertex displacement doesn't split the surface), auto-detects inverted winding (the bunny is CW), generates spherical UVs from each vertex's direction so `uv()` reads cleanly on OBJ meshes, and rescales so the longest axis = 1.6 to match primitive framing. **Those UVs are SEAM-SPLIT** (`splitUVSeam`, and see its own convention below). The source OBJs themselves are **pre-normalized** (bbox center at origin, longest axis exactly 1.6), so the runtime recenter/rescale is a no-op for them and `positionGeometry` ranges are comparable across all five geometries.
- **`fit-bounds` BAKES the normalization into the geometry attributes** (`FIT_BOUNDS_SCRIPT`, pinned by `previewFitBounds.test.ts`), and this is load-bearing rather than stylistic: `positionGeometry` is three's RAW position attribute, so scaling the Object3D — what this used to do — is completely invisible to the shader. A dropped model therefore fed its AUTHORED units to every position-driven preset, texture and noise-node default (all of which are tuned for the ±0.8 the pre-normalized built-in OBJs deliver), which is what shredded Vertex Wave on a 180-unit statue. The bake also has to be MEASURED in the entity's own frame — the old world-space `Box3.setFromObject` included `#preview-entity`'s resting tilt and `#spin-parent`'s live animation, so a model was scaled by its rotated hull (teapot ~19% undersized) and a dropped model's centring depended on the spin phase it happened to load at. Order matters: bake BEFORE `mergeByPosition` so its 1e-4 weld quantization is a fraction of the preview box rather than of authored units. Geometries are CLONED, never mutated (a glTF may reach one BufferGeometry from several nodes with different transforms), every local matrix in the subtree is then flattened to identity, mirrored transforms (negative determinant) get `flipWinding`, and skinned/instanced meshes deliberately fall back to the legacy Object3D path rather than desync their bind matrices. `public/podest.html` carries a hand-minified twin covered by NO sync plugin — `previewFitBounds.test.ts` runs both through the same assertions to catch drift. The bake DEQUANTIZES position/normal/tangent to Float32 first (`dequantize`, in both copies). `applyMatrix4` writes back into the attribute's own typed array, and three r184 truncated a KHR_mesh_quantization model's unnormalized Int16 positions to integers and wrapped normalized ones (0.5×3 came back as −0.5), measured. That is gltfpack's default output alongside meshopt. Float32 geometry, i.e. every built-in and every OBJ, is untouched, and every other attribute (uv, color) keeps its quantized form, through the seam split too.
- **The generated spherical UVs are SEAM-SPLIT, and the POLE is a documented limit** (`splitUVSeam` in `FIT_BOUNDS_SCRIPT`, mirrored in podest's twin, 2026-09-04). `atan2` wraps from +π to −π across the **−X half-plane**, so u steps 1 → 0 there; on an INDEXED mesh both sides of that step share ONE vertex, so every triangle straddling it interpolates u backwards across the whole range — the entire texture crushed, and mirrored, into a band one triangle wide. MEASURED on the shipped models: **245 of the teapot's 6320 triangles** (u-span up to 0.996) and 584 of the bunny's 69451, and on the teapot **the seam plane runs straight through the HANDLE** — which is exactly how it was reported from the canvas, as the handle-side UVs being "rotated and squashed". The fix is the textbook one: a triangle whose u-span exceeds half the range is straddling, so its LOW-u corners are duplicated and re-issued at **u + 1** (a mesh with no straddling triangle is left untouched, so this costs nothing on geometry that never had the defect). Three things are load-bearing. The duplicate is a COPY of its original, which is what keeps the mesh **welded for displacement** — the whole reason `mergeByPosition` exists: two vertices at one point with one normal, differing by exactly one texture period in u, move identically under any position- or normal-driven displacement and by one period under a uv-driven one (before this they differed by a FULL period, so a uv-driven displacement *tore the surface open* along the seam — the split repairs that too). The copy reads through **stride/offset** (`rawComponent`), because the preserve path synthesizes UVs for a GLB that ships none and a glTF primitive may interleave its attributes — a shape the regen path never produces, so a flat-array read is silently someone else's vertex; **morphAttributes are expanded too**, or every target reads past its end. And what this does NOT repair is the **POLE**, the projection's other and genuinely unfixable singularity: near ±Y the horizontal radius goes to zero, so neighbouring vertices a fraction of a millimetre apart sit at completely different azimuths and no per-vertex u can make their shared triangle small — MEASURED after the split, 4 of the teapot's triangles and 2 of the bunny's still span, every one with a corner within 8° of an axis (the lid-knob apex and the base centre), each a few pixels. Escaping that needs a different parameterization, not a better seam pass, so the test pins the honest invariant — **zero spanning triangles away from the poles**, plus a bound on the polar residue so a regression into it fails loudly. TRAP for anyone writing a test here: an un-rotated `SphereGeometry`'s own duplicated seam column sits EXACTLY where atan2 wraps, so on the preserve path nothing straddles and the split never runs — the interleaved test rotates by half a segment (`rotateY(π/24)`) precisely so it executes the code it exists to cover. **The projection is now only the FALLBACK: an OBJ that carries its own `vt`/`vn` KEEPS them on the regen path** (`splitByAuthored`, same day, mirrored in podest). Until then `mergeByPosition` threw both away and every OBJ was re-projected whatever the file said — invisible while the built-ins were bare `f a b c` files, visible the moment the regenerated teapot shipped an atlas. The position weld still runs first (it is what produces the smooth normals and drives the winding heuristic); the welded mesh is then re-split into one vertex per distinct (welded position, authored uv, authored normal), so a chart boundary or an authored crease stays split and everything else stays welded, and a source with no normals of its own takes the welded smooth ones. Corner j of the welded index is corner j of the source, EXCEPT that `flipWinding` swapped corners 1 and 2 of every triangle — the split mirrors that swap when reading the source, or a CW file with UVs gets its neighbour's texture coordinate on two corners of every face. The fit-bounds tests that exercise the PROJECTION therefore strip a three primitive of its authored uv/normal first (`bare()`), and a separate one proves the regenerated teapot's atlas survives the regen path with 99.5%+ of vertices carrying an authored (uv, normal) pair — keyed AFTER fit-bounds' own centre-and-scale, which is not quite the identity even on the pre-normalized file (the spout tip sits between samples at resolution 16).
- **The Utah teapot is TESSELLATED AT RUNTIME, and the subdivision slider is capped at 128 app-wide** (2026-09-05; `engine/teapotData.ts` + `engine/teapotGeometry.ts`, owner request: "replace the teapot with a UV'd one — the 1976 if it has UVs — offer the subdivision slider for it, cap the slider at 128 globally"). The old `teapot.obj` was a 6320-triangle mesh with no `vt` at all, so `uv()` on it was the spherical projection and the slider hid itself the moment it was selected. The official Utah Teapot page (graphics.cs.utah.edu/teapot, Cem Yuksel) publishes no control-point file — its teapots are generated client-side by a WASM module with an OBJ download and a 1–128 resolution slider (the origin of the 128) — so the data was RECOVERED: the 1976 mesh generated at resolution 8 under its "Patch Coordinates" layout exposes each patch's parameter grid, and a bicubic is determined by far fewer than 81 samples, so 16 control points per patch were solved by least squares (worst residual 4.35e-7, every coordinate one of Newell's own rationals — 0.784, 1.4375, 2.34375). The "Bijective Layout" turned out to be EXACTLY affine per patch (28 disjoint rectangles inside [0,1]², residual 4.5e-8), and its within-chart *t* is a per-column cubic Bézier whose control values are the normalized cumulative chord lengths of the column's control polygon measured in the UNSCALED 1975 frame (z × 4/3 before Blinn's 3/4) — verified to 7e-7 over all 252 sampled columns; arc length, chord length at any resolution and a bicubic texture patch were each 0.03–0.13 off. **One vacuous test almost sank this**: an early chord-length check accumulated with `cum.push(cum[j] + …)`, read an index that did not exist yet, produced NaN, and a `tot > 1e-9` guard then skipped every column — it reported an exact match of 0.00. `fixtures/utahTeapot1976Res4.json` is the official generator's own res-4 output, and `teapotGeometry.test.ts` proves every one of its face corners has a vertex of ours at the same position with the same texture coordinate (that includes the "triangulate tips" convention: each lid-apex triangle gets its own tip vertex with u at the midpoint of its two columns), plus the official triangle count 28·2N²−4N at every resolution. **Shape of the implementation**: `TEAPOT_SCRIPT` is one self-contained script string, the `FIT_BOUNDS_SCRIPT` precedent — the preview iframe evaluates it (registering the `teapot-mesh` component, which builds the BufferGeometry, hangs it on the entity and emits `model-loaded` so the shader, fit-bounds and the framing re-apply as for a swapped model), node tests and `scripts/gen-teapot-obj.mjs` evaluate the SAME text via `loadTeapot()` (the script binds to `window`, so node hands it a bare object under that name). It is in EVERY preview document so a primitive can hot-swap into the teapot — MEASURED 2026-09-05 at 19.2 KB, of which 16.1 KB is code and control points and 3.1 KB is comment and indent (the old "~16 KB, mostly control points" was the stripped figure). Two of these scripts are unconditional and they are most of the document: `FIT_BOUNDS_SCRIPT` is 22.7 KB of which nearly HALF is comment (11.9 KB stripped), `GLTF_ANIM_SCRIPT` — model geometries only — is 11.9 / 6.5 KB, and a default sphere document comes out ~31% comment bytes. Because these are TypeScript TEMPLATE LITERALS no minifier rewrites them, so those bytes also ship in the app bundle (~7.6 KB gzipped on the boot chunk, offline desktop build included) and are re-parsed by the iframe on every rebuild. Accepted, and to be fixed at BUILD time if ever: the scripts must stay unconditional (that is the hot-swap contract) and the comments must stay in the SOURCE, since they carry the measurement rationale the fit-bounds and teapot conventions rest on. The mesh is built IN the document rather than posted as OBJ text through the model feed on purpose: at the default 64 the teapot is 118k vertices / 229k triangles, ~15 MB of OBJ to serialize, post and re-parse per slider tick (60 MB at 128); the control points are 1.3 KB and the tessellation ~10 ms, so a slider tick is a tiny `fs:geometry {teapot: "resolution: N"}` and the XR popup, built from the same HTML, gets the teapot for free (`marchWindow.test.ts`'s rule: the popup renders what the pane renders). Positions come out in the preview frame directly (y up, centred, longest axis 1.6 — `TEAPOT_FRAME` from the res-64 bbox in the data module, so the normalization is resolution-independent), normals are analytic (cross(∂s, ∂t), the official generator's own orientation, with a step just inside the patch at the collapsed lid apex), and `fit-bounds` gets `regen: false` — it only bakes, since the weld+regen path had nothing to add and its projection would overwrite the atlas. Consequences to respect: `isTeapotGeometry` is its own predicate and the teapot is NOT `isModelGeometry` — no `fs:obj-model` feed, no mesh-inventory script, no `gltf-anim`, hot-swap instead of rebuild, slider visible; `__applyGeometry`'s key is `{o, m, t, g, r}` and `initialGeometryKey` must mirror it field for field; `buildTeapotAttr` is the ONE builder of the attribute (initial HTML and the hot-swap message); `SUBDIVISION_CAP` (= `TEAPOT_RES_MAX` = 128) is imported by ShaderPreview as `SUBDIVISION_MAX` and by `buildGeoAttr`'s segment clamp, and `validateSubdivision` CLAMPS a persisted/imported value rather than resetting it (256 under the old ceiling becomes 128, not 64); `subdivisionCap.test.ts` source-pins all of it. `public/models/teapot.obj` is regenerated from the same tessellator at `STATIC_TEAPOT_RESOLUTION` 16 (14,272 triangles — the Utah page's own count at 16 — with `vt` and `vn`, 1.26 MB) for podest and the XR bunny-style loaders, and the drift guard compares the committed bytes to the generator's output. The teapot is identified as a Utah Teapot in every OBJ header and the Model menu, per the page's terms ("freely available for any use, including commercial use … identify the model … as a Utah Teapot … acknowledge its origin as the historic Utah Teapot developed at the University of Utah"). Not done: podest keeps the static 16 (it has no subdivision control); the A-Frame tab still hangs the shader on a primitive.
- **Environment maps (`env` socket on the Output node) + `metalness`**: an image wired into Environment becomes real IBL (from its Color socket; Alpha/R/G/B are scalars and take the ordinary non-image path, or an Alpha wire would silently become full-colour IBL) — graphToCode emits the TEXTURE, never the sampled vec3 (`env: texture(_imageN_tex)` referencing the texture the image's share group declared, which for a sharer is ANOTHER node's var, always read through `imagePlanner.get`), tslCodeProcessor maps it to `envNode:` and the loader's `nodeProps` assigns `material.envNode` (added in 0.5, carried by the 0.8 every export references); three's `EnvironmentNode` auto-wraps a texture-valued envNode in `pmremTexture()` (per-renderer cached prefiltered radiance + irradiance, blur driven by the Roughness channel, reflectivity by the new Metalness channel — the loader always accepted `metalnessNode`, the graph just never emitted one). The image-source emission is GUARDED on the planner having placed that node, i.e. on a texture var that was actually declared: the invalid-payload fallback declares NO `_tex` var, and referencing it would be a module-killing ReferenceError — that case, and any non-image source, passes through as a plain node ref instead (legal envNode; a Color node = uniform ambient environment), vec3-widened when its shape isn't 3. Unlike image→normal, connecting image→env does NOT flip colorSpace — the map stays sRGB (PMREM decodes it). `OUTPUT_CHANNELS` appends `metalness` at the END so existing return-object key order stays byte-identical; the REGISTRY input order is the separate, user-facing arrangement (Color, Emissive, Roughness, Metalness, Opacity, Discard, Normal, Environment in the Pixel section, Displacement alone in Vertex) which `PIXEL_PORTS` and the ShaderSettingsMenu's `OPTIONAL_OUTPUT_PORTS` mirror — keep all three sequences aligned. Both new sockets are opt-in (not in `OUTPUT_DEFAULT_EXPOSED`), enabled via the Output node’s Material Settings menu or auto-exposed when an edge arrives. **The preview's Light dropdown grows an env entry** named after the attached image (`envMapName` string-selector in ShaderPreview — the cheap-string subscription pattern; falls back to the generic "Environment" label for non-image env sources AND for an image wired from its Alpha/R/G/B socket, which is a scalar ambient rather than IBL): selecting it applies `LIGHT_PRESETS.env`, an EMPTY rig on purpose, because envNode supplies radiance+irradiance itself and analytic lights on top would double-light; `effLighting` degrades a persisted `'env'` to studio the moment the map is unwired, so a stored pref can never boot a lightless void. Verified headless (Chrome, three r184 WebGL2 fallback — the Safari path): the real generated module lights an unlit sphere with correct angular mapping and roughness blur. Two caveats: PMREM needs a real-sized map (mip count is log2(height)−2, so a tiny test texture renders BLACK — an 8×4 did; real equirects are fine), and the canvas import pipeline is LDR, so no HDR sun intensities. Env cost is invisible to the point system (it is a channel, not a node) — pricing it needs a ShaderCarousel calibration entry.
- **Output node stored channel values (the on-node widgets)**: every exposed channel row carries a value cell — unwired `roughness`/`metalness`/`opacity`/`discard`/`position` render a compact DragNumberInput (shown defaults 1/0/1/0/0; 0-1-clamped except Displacement, which may go negative), unwired `color`/`emissive`/`normal`/`env` render a swatch showing the channel default (color WHITE, emissive/env BLACK, normal the flat tangent-space `#8080ff` — no unset state; every color popover leads with a "Default (none)" entry that CLEARS the stored key — the way a picked normal is re-applied back to default/none) opening `components/inputs/PaletteColorPicker` (the app-wide picker, see its own convention below). A wired channel shows the incoming value instead (`edgeValueLabel`, plain text, blue when live) via the two-step cheap-string selector — built on **`getTargetEdges`, never raw `s.edges`**, or a feeder inside a collapsed group degrades every row to a grey `…` (the group id has no registry def). Values live on `OutputNodeData.values` (**`getNodeValues()` still hard-returns `{}` for output — readers access the field directly**), and every cell shares ONE fixed width (`.output-node__val`, 20px — the compact box's step arrows are hidden at that width; drag-scrub and click-to-edit remain) so the label column stays perfectly vertical ("1" and "0.35" would otherwise render different box widths). The codegen contract, in order of precedence: an ABSENT key emits nothing (byte-stability — every pre-widget graph and builtin emits identically); a WIRED edge always wins; emission is EXPOSURE-GATED (what the node shows is what emits — a tampered file can't emit from a hidden channel, and ShaderSettingsMenu clears a channel's value when hiding it, incl. the auto-managed opacity path); values emit as inline `float(<num>)`/`color(0x<hex>)` (adversarial: Number()+num(), hexLiteral whitelist); a stored DISCARD emits the statement `Discard(float(v));`, a stored env color is a constant ambient envNode, and a stored NORMAL color emits `normalMap(color(0x…))` — DECODED as a normal-map texel ([0,1]→[-1,1] + TBN), never fed raw to normalNode — **zero discard/displacement and the default normal `#8080ff` (the identity texel) are literal no-ops and deliberately emit NOTHING**, so the widget default and an absent key are indistinguishable in code (a non-zero discard is an unconditional truthiness cull — the mesh vanishes, by design). codeToGraph's `matchStoredChannelValue` collapses exactly those inline shapes (and the bare `return color(0x…)` color-only form) back into stored values — identifiers stay real nodes — and `applyStoredOutputValues` seeds exposure for valued channels. Two resync rules in `mergeMatch` keep Apply honest: old exposedPorts union in only the VALUED channels (unioning the parse's whole seeded list resurrected defaults the user explicitly hid), and stored values SHADOWED by a wire are carried from the old node (they can't appear in the code text, so the parse could never legitimately clear them — without the carry, any Apply wiped the value a disconnect is supposed to restore). `scriptToTSL`'s `NODE_PROP_TO_CHANNEL` maps `metalnessNode`/`envNode` back to editor channels (missing entries made Load Script silently drop them). The asset-card mirror renders the real widgets inert (`.node-preview-card__inert` pointer-events wrapper, unset swatch span).
- **The colour picker is app-wide, and lives OUTSIDE the NodeEditor tree** (`components/inputs/PaletteColorPicker.tsx` + the pure `colorPickerModel.ts`; it has no React-Flow dependency, so CostBar / ShaderPreview / Toolbar can import it). It draws the shader's OWN palettes first, `BUILTIN_PALETTES` below them, and two bottom rows of globally-recent colours (`utils/recentColors`, which COLLAPSE when empty — placeholder cells read as a rendering bug). Seven rules are load-bearing. (1) **`history` is a REQUIRED prop with no default** (`'bracket' | 'none'`): `bracket()` opens `beginInteraction`, which snapshots the graph AND clears `future`, so bracketing a PREFERENCE site (canvas bg, draw ink, cost poles, preview bg, a live uniform) would push an undo entry that restores nothing and wipe the user's redo stack on every pick. The gate covers the CLEAR path too — `onClear` used to call `bracket()` directly. (2) **Live-apply and commit are different paths.** A native `<input type="color">` fires ~60 events/s while its wheel is dragged, so `applyLive` calls `onPick` per frame and records NOTHING; `createColorCommitStager` stages the value and one flush — on idle (600 ms, the `useHistoryBracket` window), blur, close, or unmount — records exactly ONE recent. The native `change` event is deliberately not the commit signal: browsers disagree about whether it fires during a drag, so a design keyed to it floods on one engine. A swatch click is neither: `pickSwatch` applies WITHOUT staging, so a palette or recent colour is never recorded. "Recent" is for colours the user MIXED — a palette colour is permanently one click away in the rows above, so recording it spends an MRU slot duplicating something already on screen and evicts a mixed colour that exists nowhere else, and re-recording a RECENT reorders the row under the pointer mid-click. It still flushes first, or a custom colour just dragged to would be dropped by the swatch click that follows it. (3) **Every effect is keyed on `open`** — the popover and its custom input only exist inside the portal, so an effect without it attaches to nothing; the custom input therefore uses React props (React treats `type="color"` as a text-like input, so `onChange` already covers native `input` AND `change`, deduped by its value tracker). (4) **A click inside an IFRAME closes it via window `blur`, not via the outside-pointerdown listener** — such a click never reaches this document, and the 3D preview is an iframe filling most of the right column, so "click elsewhere to dismiss" simply stopped working over the largest target on screen. Focus is the only signal that crosses: the browser focuses the `<iframe>` and blurs this window. Gated on `document.activeElement instanceof HTMLIFrameElement` and read on a macrotask (at blur time it is still the old element), because a bare close-on-blur also fires on an app/tab switch and, on macOS, when the "Custom" row opens the OS colour dialog — where it would kill the picker mid-pick. (5) **Escape `stopPropagation()`s** in its capture-phase listener: `ContextMenu.tsx` closes the whole settings menu on a bubbled Escape and skips only INPUT/TEXTAREA/SELECT/contentEditable — the swatches are `<button>`, so once a picker lives in a context menu a leaked Escape would take the menu with it. (6) **The portal host is RESOLVED, not `document.body`**: ShaderPreview fullscreens its own root and the top layer renders only that subtree, so a body portal would be invisible on exactly the controls kept usable in fullscreen — `pickPortalHost` adopts `fullscreenElement`/`webkitFullscreenElement` when it CONTAINS the anchor, and a `fullscreenchange` closes the popover so it can't be left in a torn-down host. (7) **Placement is measured** (`placePopover` + `useLayoutEffect` + `getBoundingClientRect`, the ContextMenu precedent) — the height is now variable, so the old `+130`/`-134` literals described a layout that no longer exists. Rows are a `grid-template-columns: repeat(5, 20px)`, NOT a flex row: every built-in is exactly 5 colours, which is why a bare `display: flex` never visibly failed, but user palettes run to 64 and spilled straight through the border; the 146px width is derived (5×20 + 4×4 = 116 content + 14 chrome + ~15 for a classic scrollbar) and the popover is `max-height`-bounded and scrolls. **`ColorPickerPopover` is exported separately** — anchor + `open` + `onClose`, no trigger — because the Color node IS the swatch (a 56px circle whose perimeter carries the output socket) and has nowhere to put one; it renders the popover anchored to itself, opened by the same double-click / long-press that used to click a hidden input. The canvas bar's background button does the same for the opposite reason: it is a `.fs-canvas-bar__btn` in a row of bar buttons with a small swatch inside, and a full-bleed colour chip there would break the bar's rhythm. **The rollout is complete and pinned**: `colorPickerHistory.test.ts` carries the reviewed `history` value for EVERY call site together with the writer that justifies it, fails on a picker in a file the table doesn't list, and fails on an `<input type="color">` rendered anywhere but the popover's own "Custom" escape hatch and `PalettesModal` (the palette EDITOR — it authors the very swatches the picker offers, so a picker there would be circular, and its rows run a bespoke draft-then-commit-on-blur model built around the native input's blur). TypeScript already catches a MISSING `history`; only a source pin catches a wrong one. Two consequences for the CSS: every site's size rule is written with TWO classes (`.shader-node__left .shader-node__input-color`, `.context-menu .context-menu__color`, …) because a single class ties with `.palette-swatch` and the winner would fall to whichever stylesheet the bundler emits last; and no site rule may set a `background` — the fill arrives as an inline `background` SHORTHAND, which also resets `background-clip`, so an inset (the preview's backdrop swatch) has to be drawn with an inset `box-shadow` instead. **`NodeVisual` is the one surface that does NOT get the picker**: it is the inert replica behind asset tiles and the node-editor overview, and the cards' `pointer-events: none` stops the pointer but not the keyboard — a real picker there would make every colour row a tab stop that opens a popover from a static replica. It renders `PaletteColorPicker` only under `interactive` (the Node Designer's stage, whose `onValueChange` writes a session-only preview model, hence `history="none"`) and otherwise an inert `<span>` carrying the SAME `.palette-swatch` classes — the OutputCardContent precedent, and the reason the old `<input readOnly>` had to go (`readOnly` is not honoured on a colour input, and a UA swatch and a `background` do not paint identically).
- **World normals: `normalWorld`, not `normalLocal`, whenever a normal meets a world-space direction.** The preview nests the mesh under `#preview-entity` (a resting tilt — 45/45/0 on sphere and cube) inside `#spin-parent` (a full 360° turntable animation while playing, restored from persisted `fs:previewRotation` even when paused), so the object→world rotation is essentially never identity. `dot(normalLocal, worldViewDir)` evaluates to `dot(N_world, R·V)`: a correctly-shaped fresnel measured about a view direction rotated by the model, so the rim slides off the silhouette and — past ~78° with edge-glow's `pow(inv,3)*2` — clips to solid glow across the whole model. All four fresnel presets (edge-glow, hologram, force-field, stylized-water) and top-cover use `normalWorld`. **Displacement keeps `normalLocal`** (vertex-wave): object-space position displaced along an object-space normal is correct, and a world normal there would shear the model as it spins. Caveat worth knowing: in the fragment stage `normalWorld` resolves through the material's SHADED normal, so it reflects anything wired into the Output node's Normal channel — desirable for a fresnel, but a coupling `normalLocal` doesn't have.

- **`hsl`/`toHsl` are NOT `three/tsl` exports — graphToCode emits its own branchless helper `Fn` at module scope**, and force-imports the `mul`/`add`/`sub`/`abs`/`mod`/`clamp`/`select`/… that only the helper BODY uses (`HSL_HELPER_LINES`/`TO_HSL_HELPER_LINES`; the import collector explicitly skips these two defs, since importing the node's own name would fail). `codeToGraph`'s visitors run over the WHOLE Program, so it `path.skip()`s a `const hsl|toHsl = Fn(…)` declarator BY NAME — without it the helper's own mul/sub/clamp statements parse back as standalone nodes and every Apply grows the graph. Emitted name and skip name are a DRIFT PAIR the guard test cannot catch: `codeToGraph.test.ts` feeds a synthetic `const hsl = Fn(…)`, so renaming the emitted helper keeps it green while real round-trips explode. The general rule: **anything graphToCode emits at module scope is graph content to codeToGraph until explicitly excluded** — the same class as the image node's ban on an async IIFE (its ReturnStatement visitor would hijack the output), and the next hand-emitted helper needs its own skip.
- **Module-scope helpers live in ONE table — `engine/moduleHelpers.ts`** (2026-09-02, keyed by helper NAME since 2026-09-03). The hsl/toHsl drift pair above is now structural: a helper is a `{ lines, imports }` entry whose name is either a def's `tslFunction` (with `tslImportModule` `''`) or a VARIANT of a def carrying `alias: { type, ports?, values? }` — the helper a MODE selects (`values.mode`, see the helper-variant convention) or one picked by the wired position's WIDTH (Box). graphToCode records every CALLEE it emits and then emits those helpers in table order, force-importing their `imports`; codeToGraph skips any `const <name> = Fn(` declarator whose name is in `MODULE_HELPER_NAMES` and resolves a call to a variant (or to a LEGACY name of a folded node — `smoothUnion`) through `HELPER_ALIASES` to its owning def, stamping the alias's `values` and mapping the arguments by its `ports`. **Every helper name is RESERVED from the variable namer** (`usedNames` is seeded with `MODULE_HELPER_NAMES`): a node variable is `<base><n>` and `sdBox2`/`sdBox3` are helpers, so a second Box node used to be named `sdBox2` — `const sdBox2 = sdBox2(…)` shadows the Fn it calls and throws a temporal-dead-zone ReferenceError at runtime (measured). `moduleHelpers.test.ts` Babel-parses each body and fails on a free-function call the `imports` list omits, checks every key resolves to a def (directly or through its alias), and round-trips every distance-field node byte-identically. Bodies use FREE functions only — the `.mix()`/`.smoothstep()`/`.step()` receiver-displacement rule.
- **Distance fields (`sdf` category, 2026-09-02, widened and consolidated 2026-09-03)** — Inigo Quilez's canonical forms as module helpers, with `cpuEvaluator` carrying the same maths for the on-node labels (value for every node; ranges only where an honest cheap interval exists — `sdCircle`, `sdCombine` union/intersect/subtract, `sdfMask`, `sdfModify` — the rest report `…` rather than a wrong bound). Negative inside, zero on the edge, positive outside; the 2D ones take a CENTRED position (wire `uv` through `sub(0.5)`), box extents are HALF-sizes, everything axial stands along Y (turn it with a Transform upstream). PRIMITIVES: `sdCircle` (a sphere for a vec3), `sdBox` (ONE def for 2D and 3D — the helper is chosen by the wired position's WIDTH at emission, `sdBox2`/`sdBox3`, both parsing back to the one def; `round` inflates the corners. **Half-extents are ONE vec3 socket** since 2026-09-09, so the dispatch now changes only WHICH COMPONENTS the helper reads and not the call's arity — both variants take (position, half size, rounding) and the flat case ignores `.z`, where before it carried a Half depth socket that meant nothing), `sdTorus`, `sdCylinder` (rounded, capped), `sdCapsule` (vertical), `sdCone` (capped, two radii), `sdPlane` (half-space, normal + offset; the normal is **one vec3 socket** since 2026-09-09, and it is the node whose default could NOT be broadcast from a scalar — a normal must point somewhere and (0,1,0) is not uniform — so the DEGENERATE case carries it: an unwired socket emits the bare 0 and the helper reads a zero-length normal as "up", which also stops a wired zero vector normalizing to NaN), `sdOctahedron` (exact), `sdStar` (n points, sharpness m — m = n is a regular polygon; uses TSL's two-argument `atan`). OPERATORS over the DOMAIN (p → p′): `sdfTransform` (**ONE vec3 socket per group** since 2026-09-09 — Move, Turn in degrees, Scale — where each was three floats; wire a Vec3 node, since an unwired group emits its scalar identity and there is no vec3 editor widget. **Scale is NON-UNIFORM** as of the same change: it divides p per axis, so the field is a BOUND rather than a true distance whenever the components differ — multiply back with Modify: scale using the SMALLEST component, and expect to lower the Raymarch node's Step scale on a strongly anisotropic shape), `sdfRepeat` (grid, ROUND form — the mod form disagrees between GLSL and WGSL for negative inputs; **vec3 Spacing and Limit**, same consolidation as Transform — spacing 0 on an axis still leaves that axis alone and limit 0 is still endless, per component), `sdfRepeatPolar` (n copies about Y, single evaluation — the sector seam on asymmetric shapes is documented), `sdfMirror` (**one vec3 socket** of 0..1 fold weights since 2026-09-09 — and its unwired DEFAULT changed with it, from (1,0,0) to (1,1,1). Unlike Plane there is no degenerate value to hang the old default on, because 0 is a meaningful weight (fold nothing) and so cannot double as a sentinel; folding all three axes beat folding none because a freshly dropped node that visibly does nothing reads as broken), `sdfDeform` (MODES twist / bend / elongate; `amount` stays a scalar — it drives twist and bend and is genuinely one number — while elongate's three stretch axes became **one vec3 socket**). Over the DISTANCE (d → d): `sdCombine` (MODES union / subtract [a minus b] / intersect / xor — IQ's k-NORMALISED quadratic smin, k the fillet thickness in distance units, floored at 1e-6 so k = 0 is the HARD op and never a division by zero; xor is exact and takes no k), `sdfModify` (MODES round / shell / scale). BRIDGES: `sdfExtrude` (2D distance + position → 3D, along Z), `sdfRevolve` (position → the 2D profile plane, about Y), `sdfMask` (distance → 0–1 coverage with a linear edge; deliberately not `smoothstep`, whose equal edges are a WGSL error at width 0). **Two folds carry a migration**: `sdBox2`/`sdBox3` → `sdBox` and `smoothUnion`/`sdSubtract` → `sdCombine` — `registry/legacyNodeTypes.ts` migrates the old REGISTRY TYPES on every restore path (values kept, ports were kept so every edge survives), while their old CALL NAMES in an already-exported `.js` parse back through `HELPER_ALIASES` (the 2013 mix-form `smoothUnion(a, b, k)` lands on Combine's union with the same k — a slightly different fillet, accepted). The whole family was rendered end to end in one shader on the real WebGL2 backend (2026-09-03) — expect bound-only-field cracks on deformed / scaled / smooth-combined surfaces, which is what the Raymarch Output's Step scale is for.
- **A vec3 INPUT SOCKET emits its unwired default as a BARE NUMBER, and the helper broadcasts it** (`sdfTransform`, `sdfRepeat`, `sdBox`, `sdfDeform`, `sdPlane`, `sdfMirror`, 2026-09-09). The obvious form — having codegen emit `vec3(0)` for an unwired vec3 port — is the trap: `codeToGraph` reads that as a real Vec3 CONSTRUCTOR and mints a node for it, so the graph grows by one node on every Apply (MEASURED: sdfExtrude's round trip went from 3 nodes to 4, with `errors: []` and a correct picture throughout — the `unwrapScalarWiden` class of defect). So emission is unchanged — an unwired port still emits its scalar default, and `unwiredDefaults.test.ts`'s whole-registry snapshot moved by exactly the two lines whose arity changed — and the broadcast happens inside the helper body, where `vec3(t)` fills from a float and is the identity on a vec3. That last fact is the one everything rests on and it is the kind that yields perfect-looking source and a blank preview, so `sdfVecSockets.test.ts` builds it against the REAL `three/tsl` rather than asserting on text. `cpuEvaluator` carries its own copy of these formulas, so it has a matching `vec3Input` that broadcasts an unwired scalar the same way — without it the card's live numbers disagree with the picture for every unwired group, reading Box's 0.5 into x alone as a flat sheet where the shader draws a 1x1x1 box. The UX consequence is deliberate: there is no vec3 editor widget, so a consolidated group has no inline number boxes and a translation costs a wired Vec3 node. **A group whose unwired default is NON-UNIFORM cannot use this shape as-is** — one scalar broadcasts uniformly, and emitting a `vec3(a, b, c)` literal is the graph-growing trap above. Two of the six hit that: Plane carries its (0,1,0) in the helper's degenerate branch, and Mirror simply changed default. A third case would want stored values that can hold a vector, which is a real feature (it is also what a vec3 editor widget needs) rather than a tweak.
- **Helper VARIANTS — one def, several emitted helpers, chosen by `values.mode`** (2026-09-03; `sdCombine`, `sdfModify`, `sdfDeform`). A def with `modes: { values, default, labels, symbols? }` keeps its DEFAULT mode's helper as its `tslFunction` and registers the others as table entries carrying `alias: { type, ports?, values: { mode } }` (a variant may take FEWER arguments — `sdXor` has no smoothness, the default `sdfTwist` has no stretch — so `helperCallPorts` is what both engines map arguments by). The mode lives in `values.mode` and NEVER in `defaultValues` (on a ShaderNode that map is the socket list, and `unwiredDefaults.test.ts` refuses a string there); `modeOf` reads it through the closed vocabulary with the default as the fallback, so junk from a `.fastshader` can only ever select a real helper and an ABSENT key is the default — which is what keeps a graph saved before a mode existed emitting byte-identically. `helperNameFor` is the ONE place the callee is chosen; the variable is named after the DEF (`sdCombine1` whatever the mode), never after the helper. The settings menu (`NodeSettingsMenu`) offers a Mode `<select>` for any def with `modes`, and ShaderNode draws the mode's symbol as a chip (bottom-left, the formula chip's mirror) so a Combine set to subtract is distinguishable from one set to union on the canvas — the header shows the var name and the rows the operands, so nothing else would tell them apart. Not mirrored into NodeVisual: `mode` is not a `defaultValues` key, so the replica can never be handed one (the formula-chip rule). `sdfModes.test.ts` round-trips every mode of every multi-mode def byte-identically and pins that helper names are reserved from the namer.
- **Several output nodes — the ACTIVE flag governs only the UNTARGETED ones** (2026-09-03; narrowed by the per-material Output split, 2026-09-18; `utils/sdfPartition.ts` `activeSink`, `utils/outputMaterials.ts` `defaultOutput` / `contributingOutputs`). Any mix of plain Outputs and Raymarch Outputs may coexist on the canvas. **A TARGETED plain Output ALWAYS contributes** — it shades the meshes it names and nothing else, so parking it behind another node's flag would silently drop a material the canvas still shows, and would make "which meshes does this shader paint" depend on a choice the user made about a DIFFERENT node. Among UNTARGETED plain Outputs exactly ONE contributes (the flagged one, else the lowest-ranked), which is what still lets a whole-model variant be PARKED beside the one in use; and a DRIVING Raymarch Output still suppresses every plain Output. So **"THE Output" is FOUR questions now**, each with its own function and each call site picking one deliberately — they were ONE function (`flagged ?? outputs[0]`), blind to whether the node names a mesh, which is safe only while one node holds every material. `defaultOutput` = who owns the module's TOP-LEVEL channels: the flagged-and-untargeted node, else the LOWEST-RANKED untargeted one, else **null** — a real answer rather than a gap, since a document whose every Output is targeted emits `parts` alone and loader 0.6/0.8 then leaves each unclaimed mesh on its authored material. `contributingOutputs` = the node SET whose materials reach the module, `defaultOutput` plus every targeted plain Output, returned in emit order — the ONE answer emission, both export plans, the code panel's tabs, the preview and its XR popup all read, with `costSeeds` the same shape for the cost total. `moduleSettingsOutput` = whose four `materialSettings` keys the module writes at top level (owner decision D1's fallback chain: `buildShaderModule` always writes them, so unlike the channels this must answer while ANY Output exists — `defaultOutput`, else the first Output of any kind; a Raymarch Output is not a candidate, its settings coming from `marchMaterialSettings`, which layers over this). And `findDefaultOutput` = "where do I point the USER" — the flagged Output else the first in array order, targeting deliberately NOT consulted — which the focus and glide helpers ask. **The failure they were split to prevent takes one more step than it looks, and the measurement is worth keeping.** A FRESHLY built GLB document does put its untargeted default first (measured: `gi_output`, then `gi_output_m0`, `gi_output_m1`), so array order happens to answer correctly there — but array order is NOT stable: `liftChildrenAfterParents` splices a node into a new slot on an ordinary drag-into-a-group and `useSyncEngine` reorders on every Apply. The moment a TARGETED index node reaches the front, a single blind predicate hands it the module's top-level channels — the same material painted twice, the module-level copy repainting every unclaimed mesh — while it also becomes the cost seed, the Uniforms scope, the preview window's owner and Preview mode's target. (`sdfPartition.ts`'s own comment still states the stronger "an import-built document's FIRST Output node is a targeted one", which the measurement above contradicts; the RULE it justifies is right either way.) `activeSink` narrowed the same way (flagged-and-eligible → first wired march → lowest-ranked untargeted), and `normalizeActiveOutput` STRIPS the flag from a targeted node, so the two rules cannot disagree about who is eligible. Both pick the lowest RANK with ties keeping array order, so a document that has never been split elects exactly what it always did. **The choice is node data**: clicking an untargeted node's preview SOCKET (`setActiveOutput`, one history entry, telemetry `output-activate`) writes `activeOutput: true` on it and deletes the key from every other sink — on a TARGETED node that socket is INERT, and why is D2 in the per-mesh convention. **An ABSENT key means "never chose"**, and then the historical rule decides — that absence is what keeps every saved graph, every built-in and every exported `.js` emitting byte-identically, the noise-`signed` precedent; a document acquires the key only when someone clicks. `normalizeActiveOutput` (first literal `true` wins, junk stripped, an ineligible holder stripped, same-array-when-clean) runs on every restore path beside `sanitizeOutputMaterials` and on the resync's final list; a saved group has its flag CLEARED at load (a fragment does not own the choice) and `instantiateSavedGroup` normalises the combined list with the live graph FIRST, so its choice wins; a paste strips the flag off the clones. Deleting the active node just removes its flag, so the fallback takes over — no re-election anywhere. **The resync is the subtle half** (`useSyncEngine` plus the pure `utils/resyncPairing.ts` and `utils/sinkCarry.ts`): a PARKED sink is absent from the code BY CONSTRUCTION (never deleted by the user — it was never in the text) and `carryInactiveSinks` brings it back whole together with its incoming edges whose source survived, while a TARGETED plain Output is NOT carried, because it CONTRIBUTES and the parse rebuilds it from the code — carrying it too would add a node BESIDE the parse's copy and grow a GLB document by one Output per mesh ON EVERY APPLY while emitting each part twice. `pairable` therefore excludes only PARKED sinks from the pairing buckets, every contributing Output being a candidate on its own, and `matchKey`'s BINDING tier does the rest (rule 3 of the per-mesh convention — without it an Apply moves one material's identity onto another mesh). `oldActive` is plain `activeSink` with NO last-resort term: the `contributingOutputs(oldNodes)[0]` fallback that stood there while an all-targeted document had no sink at all is gone, because both halves it patched are now done properly — every targeted Output pairs on its own binding, and `placeParsedOutputs` PLACES an unpaired plain Output below the lowest positioned one (`UNFOLD_DY` apart, in emit order, so a built column and a restored column are the same column) rather than handing it to the relayout, which is what keeps it clear of the `unpositioned.length === 0` gate that would otherwise take every group frame on the canvas with it. A parked march's feeder chain survives on its own because an inactive march is NOT partitioned (`drivingMarchOutput` gates the partition), so its position-dependent nodes emit in the flat body as ordinary consts. `activeOutput.test.ts` pins apply∘apply stability through the pure carry. Every sink's badge is its OWN price (`sinkCosts` — what the shader would cost with that node driving, muted on one that is not), while the meter is the UNION of every contributing seed; the Uniforms overlay lists only sliders that reach them. `foldExtraOutputs` is GONE — `unfoldOutputMaterials` replaced it, so a legacy one-Output-per-mesh document now loads natively as one node per mesh rather than being folded into one. Podest still knows nothing about any of this — it renders an exported module on its own picker. One RESIDUE, display-only and documented rather than fixed: `envMapName` (the preview's Light dropdown entry named after an attached environment image) reads `defaultOutput`, so on a document where EVERY Output is targeted that named entry is absent.
- **Imperative TSL (`Loop`, `If`, `.assign`) needs an ACTIVE STACK, and the module the loader runs has none** (measured 2026-09-02 against singularity's black-hole raymarcher). `buildShaderModule` strips the outer `Fn(() => {` and shaderloader calls the default export as a PLAIN FUNCTION, so a `Loop(...)` or `acc.assign(...)` at body level is a silent no-op — three warns "No stack defined for assign operation" only in Node; the preview just shows the un-looped constant. That is the same fact the `__pixel` Discard wrapper already encodes. An imperative block therefore has to be a **self-invoked `Fn(() => { …; return v; })()`** — one const in the flat chain — and that is the shape a future raymarch/loop node must emit. Two things were fixed to let that shape live: `parseBody` used to match `^return` on EVERY line and so hijacked the nested Fn's return as the colour channel (deleting it from the body and leaving the Fn void — a `getTypeFromLength` crash in the minified bundle); it now counts brace depth on the masked body (`nestedFnReturn.test.ts`). And `codeToGraph` used to drop a `Loop`/`If`/`Switch` statement in SILENCE while walking INTO its callback — the body's `const`s became flat top-level nodes, every accumulator aliased to its initial value, and the next Apply re-emitted a constant with `errors: []`; it now warns and `path.skip()`s the whole block (root callee resolved through an `If(...).Else(...)` chain) and warns on `.assign`-family statements naming the variable (`loopStatements.test.ts`). Still true and deliberate: the GRAPH cannot hold a loop (`isSafeExprNode` refuses arrow functions in an `unknown` node — unknown = pure data expression), so pasted imperative code survives in the preview only until the next graph→code sync overwrites the panel. Known side bug, unfixed: an `unknown` node's `rawExpression` keeps the ORIGINAL local names (`step(rLen, 0.13)`) while the re-emit renames `rLen` → `length1`, so the module throws `rLen is not defined`.
- **Raymarch Output (`raymarchOutput`, 2026-09-03) — surface AND volume marcher in ONE node** (it replaced the separate SDF Output and Volume Output the same day; nothing shipped in between). Category `output` (moved there from `sdf` on 2026-09-09 — see the registry counts above), its own flow type and component (`nodes/RaymarchOutputNode.tsx`, `MARCH_NODE_CONFIG`, the Output node's chrome via OutputNode.css, tile `MarchOutputCardContent`, footprint in `layoutEngine`). Sockets: **Surface** Field (sphere-traced to a hit), Color, Emissive; **Volume** Density (integrated front to back), Glow; **Sky** Background; **Settings** Steps 64, Step size 0.03, Epsilon 0.002, Bend 0 (gravity: the direction is pulled toward the origin by `bend·step / max(r², 0.01)` each step), Horizon 0 (a ray inside this radius is absorbed: alpha 1, black), **Window 1** (the radius of the sphere the preview renders through — LARGE puts the camera INSIDE it, the material is double-sided and the march starts at the camera on a back face, so the sky fills the view), **Field radius 1** (outside it a pure-surface march jumps straight to that bubble). It DRIVES when Field OR Density is wired (`drivingMarchOutput`, the ONE predicate: PreviewLink's wire, the preview's window `MARCH_WINDOW_GEOMETRY` = `'marchSphere'` sized by `marchWindowRadius` and never persisted, `marchMaterialSettings`' double side, the A-Frame tab's `<a-sphere radius>`, the cost seeds, useSyncEngine's badge). **Emission is ONE IIFE returning ONE vec4** (final RGB + coverage) with all shading INSIDE — measured on r184: a `struct` return recursed to a stack overflow, a `mat4` pack mis-typed, and outer `.toVar()` variables written from inside a Fn have no stack — so the surface is lit inside the march by a fixed key + ambient lambert over the gradient normal (four Field taps) and the result goes out as `{ color: color(0x000000), emissive: rm1Col, roughness: float(1) }`; the scene lights do not reach a marched surface (a known regression from the old SDF Output's normal channel, accepted for one node). Per channel `const rm1<Channel> = …`: a `Fn(([p]) => …)` (Background: `Fn(([dir]) => …)`, the FINAL bent direction in world space) when the chain depends on the scope's root, a captured identifier otherwise, `color(0x…)` for the Color swatch, a literal fallback when unwired; the numbers ride `const stepSize = float(…)` lines inside the IIFE (eps, bend, horizon, win, fieldR) so codeToGraph reads them back (`MARCH_PARAM_HANDLES`); a bend/horizon at 0 and unwired emits no steer/absorb line. Field-only advances `max(max(d, eps), gap)` (sphere tracing, plus the jump to the field bubble); any Density switches to fixed `stepSize`. `Discard(rm1.w < 0.01)` only when NO Background is wired. `raymarchOutput.test.ts` pins surface / volume / both emission and byte-identical round trips; `marchWindow.test.ts` the window, the `<a-sphere>`, double side and the camera start. **The VR popup renders what the PANE renders**: `handleOpenVR` passes `previewGeometry` + `marchWindow`, never the persisted Model choice — that choice is PARKED while a march drives, and passing it projected the black hole onto the teapot in VR (2026-09-03); `marchWindow.test.ts` pins the call and the xr document's window sphere. The cost pill aims at the driving node (`costFocusId`, above). Per-step partition: `utils/sdfPartition.ts` (`MARCH_SCOPES`, `marchPartition`). Cost: Field × (steps + 3), Density and Glow × (steps − 1), + 16 — plus Field × 5 while Occlusion is on and Field × 24 while a soft Shadow is on. **The march's own LIGHT section (2026-09-03)**: `lightX/Y/Z` (the key direction, default 0.6 0.8 0.5), `lightColor` and `ambient` (colour swatches or captured chains — declarators `rm1LightColor`/`rm1Ambient`; an untouched socket emits NO declarator and the literal 0.85 / 0.15 greys inline, so an untouched node shades exactly as the fixed light did), `ao` (IQ's 5-tap occlusion along the normal, unrolled, scaling the AMBIENT only; 0 = off and no extra taps), `shadow` (a second `Loop(int(24))` from the hit toward the light — penumbra = closest miss ÷ (travel × softness), scaling the KEY only; 0 = off), and `stepScale` (multiplies the sphere-trace advance, never the jump to the field bubble — lower it on bound-only fields: deformed, scaled, smooth-combined). All six ride `const <name> = float(…)` lines inside the IIFE (`MARCH_PARAM_HANDLES`), and the parser takes the FIRST `Loop(int(…))` as Steps — the shadow loop is a second one and must not overwrite it. The scene lights still never reach a marched surface (one vec4 return); this section is what the Light dropdown is to an ordinary shader. **The node shows its MAIN sockets only** (2026-09-04): Field, Color, Density and Background are exposed by default (`MARCH_DEFAULT_EXPOSED`, utils/exposedPorts.ts — the node is in `usesExposedPorts`); **Emissive and Glow left that set on 2026-09-09** (owner request), each being the second channel of a surface and of a volume — reached for once the shape is on screen — so they start hidden with the numbers and the light settings, and every number and light setting is hidden until ticked in its own right-click menu, `menus/RaymarchSettingsMenu.tsx` — the Output's `ShaderSettingsMenu` shape with VALUES: each row is a show-socket checkbox plus the setting's editor (a number field with the node's step and clamp, or a colour swatch), grouped as the node groups them. Hiding a socket drops its wires (`toggleExposedPort`) but KEEPS the value — a setting applies whether or not it is wired, and emission reads every value regardless of exposure — the one place this differs from the Output, which clears a hidden channel's value because its emission is exposure-gated. A wire landing on a hidden setting exposes it (`autoExposeConnectedParamPorts` + `exposeConnectedTarget`), the node re-measures its handles on the exposed set (`useUpdateNodeInternals`), a section with every socket hidden is skipped, and the palette tile shows the default set. `raymarchExposure.test.ts` pins all of it. Which Raymarch Output drives is the ACTIVE SINK (several outputs, one active — see that convention): a flagged Raymarch Output drives even with nothing wired (it then emits the "nothing wired" sentinel), a flagged plain Output silences every march, and a document with no flag keeps the historical rule (the first WIRED march, else the first Output). **Limits, deliberate**: no depth write; only `positionLocal`/`positionGeometry` are position roots and `rayDirection` the direction root; a node feeding two per-step sockets is emitted into both Fns and parses back as two nodes; `rayDirection` is a MODULE HELPER (`moduleHelpers.ts`) and the Image node's `dir` input (equirect sampling, LAST in its inputs so the splice order is unchanged) is how a sky image reaches Background. **Predecessors** (2026-09-02/03, both folded into this node within a day, neither ever shipped): an **SDF Output** (`sdfOutput` — surface marching only, rendered through a 2-unit `sdfBox` window, driven by Field alone) and a **Volume Output** (`volumeOutput` — density/emission/background). Nothing on disk carries either any more: no `SdfOutputNode.tsx`, no `sdfOut1`/`volOut1` emitted variable bases, no `sdfOutput.test.ts`/`volumeOutput.test.ts`, no `SDF_WINDOW_GEOMETRY`/`VOLUME_WINDOW_GEOMETRY` — so a comment, a memory note or a commit message naming one is describing text, not a file. The ONE survivor is codeToGraph's local `sdfOutputId`, which holds the Raymarch Output's id and is only named that way because renaming it is churn across nine call sites in the middle of a parser (it says so in a comment beside itself). Two of their rules read plausibly and are WRONG for this node: the window is a SPHERE (`MARCH_WINDOW_GEOMETRY` = `'marchSphere'`, and an `<a-sphere radius>` on the A-Frame tab), not a box; and it DRIVES on Field **or** Density (`drivingMarchOutput`), not on Field alone.
- **`setCode(code, 'code')` — the user typing in the TSL panel — writes `code` ONLY; `previewCode` advances on a graph→code sync or on Apply (`requestCodeSync`).** ShaderPreview subscribes to `previewCode`, so half-typed TSL never swaps `srcDoc`: without the split every keystroke would re-fetch and re-parse the ~1 MB A-Frame bundle under a fresh opaque origin — the same cost the 200 ms rebuild debounce exists to collapse. It is also the reason the preview looks frozen while you edit code until Apply; collapsing `setCode`'s two branches into one write is the accidental way to lose it.
- **A zero-input node's `defaultValues` KEY ORDER is a positional codegen contract in BOTH directions, and nothing in `nodeRegistry.ts` says so.** graphToCode's generic `inputs.length === 0 && def.defaultValues` branch emits `Object.keys(def.defaultValues)[0]` as the sole constructor argument (`graphToCode.ts`), and codeToGraph maps call argument `i` onto `Object.keys(def.defaultValues)[i]` — using that key as a real port id, so an identifier argument gets an EDGE wired to it. So the constructor value must stay FIRST: `slider`'s `{ value, min, max }` and `property_float`'s `{ value, name }` are the two multi-key defs riding on it today, and any future zero-input def joins them. Alphabetizing the literal or prepending a key is a tidy-up edit that silently changes what the node emits and what a parsed literal is stored under — `slider` would emit its `min`. The test pin covers float/int/slider/color only, so a reordered `property_float` passes CI. Order-INDEPENDENT and not to be confused with these: `property_color` is matched by name in a dedicated branch on both sides, the noise family's `{ pos, scale }` is wired by name in `processNoiseCall`, and `uv` declares real `def.inputs` so it never reaches `defaultKeys`.
- **Deleting a node SPLICES the chain, it does not cut it** (`utils/edgeUtils.ts` → `bridgeEdgesAcrossDeletedNodes`): each outgoing edge of a deleted node re-parents onto the live source reached by walking its first incoming edge upstream, crossing whole runs of deleted nodes — `X → A → B → C` with A+B selected becomes `X → C`. Single-input-per-port is preserved (first bridge to a `(target, targetHandle)` wins, later ones drop), an edge whose TARGET is deleted is dropped, and a chain that dead-ends upstream is dropped. React Flow's own delete is disabled (`deleteKeyCode={null}`) precisely so that every user-facing deletion path goes through that ONE helper — NodeEditor's keydown handler, `store.removeNode`, and the group-delete run. A fourth deletion path that skips it silently redefines what Delete means: the graph disconnects, the generated code changes, and nothing errors.
- **A baked lookup texture that is SAMPLED LINEARLY must be `HalfFloatType`, never `FloatType`**: WebGPU refuses to filter 32-bit float textures without the `float32-filterable` device feature, which the A-Frame bundle never requests — so a float32 LUT looks right on the WebGL2 preview and breaks only on WebGPU (exactly the split the WGSL/GLSL toggle exists to expose). `bakeHalfFloatTexture` (graphToCode) is the shared emitter for every linearly-sampled bake — the Stripes phase ramp, the Data-Viz value ramp, the 256-texel colormap LUT — and the phase ramp is normalized to [0,1] before encoding so float16's ~11-bit mantissa stays smooth. The Data node is the deliberate exception: it wants EXACT per-column values, so it keeps `FloatType` + Nearest, which needs no feature flag.
- **A node component that mounts or unmounts handles at runtime MUST call `useUpdateNodeInternals(id)` in an effect keyed on the handle SET** — `exposedPorts.join('|')`, the collapsed group's boundary-socket ids, the grown-operand count. React Flow never refreshes its handle-bounds map on its own, so an edge to a freshly mounted handle stays in the store and still emits correct code but simply never DRAWS, and existing edges snap to stale anchors when a socket count shifts. The symptom actively misleads: a page reload fixes it (every handle is measured on first mount), so it reads as a React Flow rendering glitch rather than a missing effect. All eight dynamic-handle components already do it — ShaderNode, OutputNode, RaymarchOutputNode, PreviewNode, GroupNode, SoundNode, ClockNode, ColorNode — so a NINTH node type is the only way to reintroduce the bug.
- **The A-Frame tab's `index.html` is DERIVED FROM THE MODULE, and it is DEFAULTS-ONLY ON PURPOSE** (`engine/tslToAFrameHTML.ts`). Two independent rules, both easy to erode:
  **(a) The uniform rows are parsed out of the `export const schema = { … }` block `buildShaderModule` emits** (tslCodeProcessor.ts), never re-derived from the node graph. That block IS the loader's uniform contract — declared properties, colours and live-audio channels all land in it — so a page built from it cannot list a uniform the shader lacks or miss one it has. Consequences: those emitted schema lines are a PARSED FORMAT now (change their shape and `parseShaderModuleSchema` must move with it — `tslToAFrameHTML.test.ts` pins both ends); every value is re-normalized on the way in (`Number()` for floats, `#rrggbb`-or-black for colours, a file-name whitelist for `src:`) because property defaults and the shader name arrive from a `.fastshader`; the attribute is emitted MULTI-LINE, one uniform per row, which is safe because A-Frame's style parser trims each `;`-separated chunk (`utils/styleParser.js`); and a uniform named **`src` is DROPPED from the rows** — that parser is last-wins and the `shader` component's own key is `src`, so emitting it would replace the module path with a number and the page would load no shader at all. That guard is `RESERVED_ATTRIBUTE_KEYS` in `tslToShaderModule.ts` and it is SHARED with the module's own usage header, which writes the same `<a-entity …>` example and had the identical collision; `buildHeader` also computes its unique-property list BEFORE branching on it, so a shader whose only property is named `src` prints the no-property example instead of a dangling `shader="src: shader.js; "`.
  **(b) The page sets NOTHING A-Frame would set itself.** No comments, no `<a-light>` rig, no `background`, no camera entity, no `orbit-controls`, no `vr-mode-ui`/`loading-screen` overrides, no geometry/material attributes — a bare `<a-scene>` with one primitive at `0 1.6 -3`. It is a page meant to be ENTERED IN VR, so the default camera, the default light rig and the default Enter-VR button are the point, not an omission; the editor preview's own lighting/background/spin/subdivision are deliberately NOT mirrored (only the primitive follows it). A test pins the ABSENCE of each of those attributes, because "helpfully" adding one back is the obvious wrong move. **The ONE exception to (b) is tessellation for a displacement shader, and it is a correctness fix rather than a taste one**: `<a-plane>` and `<a-box>` default to a SINGLE segment per axis (`aframe/src/geometries/plane.js`, `box.js`), so a plane is four vertices sharing one normal — a `positionNode` has nothing to move and the relief the editor shows is *absent*, not coarser (measured: a 0.2-amplitude wave becomes a bilinear quad off by 0.26), while a box's 24 per-face-normal vertices slide apart into six floating squares. So a module matching `/positionNode\s*:/` — this file's own predicate; the loader answers the same question structurally, off the BUILT material — gets explicit `segments-*` attributes at 64, which A-Frame maps onto `geometry.segments*` for every primitive (`extras/primitives/primitives/meshPrimitives.js`; the schema's `max: 20` is inspector metadata nothing enforces, and the app's own preview already passes 64-256). A displaced `<a-box>` used to split at its 12 shared edges at ANY segment count (BoxGeometry duplicates those positions with per-face normals) and the fix could not be inlined without costing the page its reason to exist — **the loader (0.6 and 0.8) welds them itself now**, so this page gets it free, and the same move fixed podest and every exported module. **The SECOND exception is the SINGLE-GLB export.** While the effective format is `.glb` the page hangs `shader="src: model"` on `<a-entity gltf-model="url(<name>.glb)">` (the `modelFile` option) instead of a primitive, because the export IS the model: no segments, no radius, and the same scene, backend and scripts. It needs loader 0.8 — the tab's label says so, and 0.6 reads `src: model` as a path, logs a `shader-error` and leaves the model on its own materials. The Three.js tab stays on the primitive and the `.js`. **The page carries NO script of its own**: the VR backend is forced declaratively by `<a-scene renderer="backend: webgl">` (the bundle's aframe#5847 carry — see the vendoring bullet), which is what makes three r184 take its WebGL2 backend, the only one that can enter a WebXR session (the WebGPU one hard-throws in `XRManager.setSession`). The SPELLING is load-bearing: only the raw key `backend:` reaches the patched branch and only the exact value `webgl` forces, so a typo silently does nothing and Enter VR throws on a headset much later (`aframeBackendProperty.test.ts` guards the patch; `tslToAFrameHTML.test.ts` pins the attribute AND the absence of any inline script). This replaced an inline `navigator.gpu`-hiding script on 2026-08-31; pages exported before then still carry it and keep working, since hiding gpu and forcing WebGL reach the same backend. **The two install facts live in the tab's `index.html` LABEL, not on the page** (`CodeEditor.tsx` + `lv.json`): put the `.js` beside it, and serve the folder over http(s). The second one is not pedantry — the loader `fetch`es the module, so a double-clicked `file://` page renders a plain white default-material primitive with a full working scene around it and says nothing. If the page ever stops being comment-free, that is the sentence to move onto it.
- **Plain Three.js is loader 0.8's FastShaders core, and two places say so**: README's "Using the shader module with plain Three.js" section and the usage header every export carries (`buildHeader` in `tslToShaderModule.ts`, which links to that section; it replaced the unqualified "Also usable directly with Three.js" line). The recipe is `FastShaders.use(THREE)` (the three/webgpu namespace; it installs `globalThis.THREE` when absent), `await FastShaders.load(url)`, then the SYNCHRONOUS `FastShaders.apply(mesh, shader, { values })`, which returns a binding (`set`, `uniforms`, `dispose`, `materialParts`). Those are the calls `loaderSwitch.test.ts` checks against the served loader's api object, so a header naming a call 0.8 lacks cannot ship (an early draft documented an async `apply(mesh, url, { THREE })` that never existed). `apply` does what the component does: uniforms from `schema`, the material and the emissive→colour fallback, the four material settings, `parts`, the position weld (`BufferGeometryUtils.mergeVertices` does not weld a BoxGeometry, measured), barycentric corners and the revision warning; tessellation stays the caller's. A glTF needs `loader.register(FastShaders.gltfPlugin)` before loading, and `FastShaders.decoders.configure({ DRACOLoader })` + `install(loader)` for Draco/meshopt: the bare namespace has no DRACOLoader, so the page imports it from `three/addons/loaders/DRACOLoader.js` (the README's import map carries a `three/addons/` entry), LoadingManager comes from the namespace `use()` bound, and the decoder files are found beside the loader's script. A `configure()` without `resolve` keeps that self-located resolver; it used to drop it, so the recipe as documented set NO decoder and an empty `lastError`. `shaderloaderDecoders.test.ts` runs the README's own call on a real GLTFLoader. Without the loader the module still imports standalone — it imports THREE from 'three/webgpu' when it bakes a texture and every TSL function it calls (see the A-Frame-pipeline bullet) — and the list above is the caller's to redo. **The code panel's Three.js tab (`tslToThreeHTML.ts`) runs that same sequence**: the loader's classic script from `CDN_BASE`/`LOADER_FILE` between the import map and the page's module, then `use(THREE)`, `load('./<file>')` and `apply(mesh, shader, { values: params })`, where `params` restates the schema's defaults so the reader can see and change every property. That retired its hand wiring and five defects that came with it: plain values passed as params, so a colour arrived as a string and nothing updated live; a static import that threw on a texture shader because no `globalThis.THREE` existed yet; `mergeVertices`, a no-op on a box; a MeshStandardNodeMaterial with no emissive fallback; and `barycentric` assigned as a stray material property. The page keeps only what the loader cannot know, the segment counts a displacing shader needs and the camera inside a march window. The window's double side arrives as the module's own `side: 2` (CodeEditor feeds the tab the march-aware material settings), so the page has no `DoubleSide` line. It registers no glTF plugin and no decoders, because it never loads a model. `threeEmbed.test.ts` EXECUTES the page's own script in a vm sandbox against the served loader, real three and real generated modules: colour and float properties, a displaced 64-segment box that must come out welded, a Colormap texture shader, and a march window. A test-side stand-in replaces only the Blob-URL import, which node cannot do. `loaderSwitch.test.ts` checks every `FastShaders.<call>` the page spells against the loader's api. The page has not been opened in a real browser yet, and it needs the 0.8 push and purge like every export. The three version is a drift set (`THREE_REVISION` in `engine/threeRevision.ts` — `THREE_VERSION` derives from it — the README snippet's `three@` URLs, package.json, and the submodule's `super-three` pin), pinned by `threeRevision.test.ts`, `threeEmbed.test.ts` (which also refuses any `@latest` in the README) and `loaderSwitch.test.ts`. The header's wording is constrained: the loaders' `autoInjectTSLImports` (0.6 and 0.8 alike) matches an UNANCHORED `import\s*{` on the raw source, comments included, and `autoDetectSchema` scans for `params.<name>`, so the header may never contain `import {`, `params.<ident>` or `const X = uniform(` (pinned in `tslToShaderModule.test.ts`, and run against the real 0.5, 0.6 and 0.8 loaders in `shaderloaderTransform.test.ts`, which also checks the header comes out of `autoInjectTSLImports` byte-identical). Call syntax such as `FastShaders.apply(` is safe there only because `autoInjectTSLImports` strips `//` comments before it scans for calls, and the plain-three block names no loader FILE, so a loader bump never has to edit it. Editing the header changes the bytes of every new export, comments only, the study package's shader included.
- **Push the `a-frame-shaderloader` submodule BEFORE the release tag, and PURGE jsdelivr when the pushed file is new.** Exported `.js` reference the loader at `cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/Alvis1/a-frame-shaderloader@master` (`CDN_BASE`, `tslToShaderModule.ts`) — as does the A-Frame tab's generated `index.html` (bundle + loader, the same two files the header names), while the app's own preview reads the same-origin `public/js/` copies (`resolveAssetUrl`) — so a loader pushed after the app deploys 404s for **every recipient of an exported shader while working perfectly for the author**, and jsdelivr caches branch refs (~12 h) AND caches the 404, so it does not self-heal on the next request. Force it: `https://purge.jsdelivr.net/gh/Alvis1/a-frame-shaderloader@master/js/<file>` — for the loader switch that is `js/a-frame-shaderloader-0.8.js` plus the five `js/decoders/` files (the Draco pair, meshopt, and the basis transcoder pair, whose sha256s must start `8478b5b6d6b74e7d` / `6cf17dc889352c42`), each checked with `curl -sL <cdn url> | shasum -a 256` against the local file. The full order: push the submodule → commit the parent's pointer bump and run `npm test` → purge → `npm version patch && git push --follow-tags` → `npm run deploy:alvismisjuns` → `npm run deploy:sferas` (held while a study cohort runs — the participant's preview changes loader). Push from the MAIN checkout's `a-frame-shaderloader/`, fast-forwarded from wherever the work was committed: a worktree's `--shared` submodule clone has the main checkout's submodule as its `origin`, whose checked-out `master` refuses the push. Order matters for a second, independent reason: `release.yml` checks the submodule out at the pointer RECORDED here (`submodules: recursive`) and `vendorSync.test.ts` compares it against the TRACKED `public/js/` copies, so an un-bumped pointer fails the release's own test gate before a single binary is built.

## TypeScript Notes

- `@babel/traverse` CJS/ESM interop: `const traverse = (typeof _traverse.default === 'function' ? _traverse.default : _traverse)`
- React Flow v12: import from `@xyflow/react`, use `Node<DataType, TypeName>` generics
- `applyNodeChanges`/`applyEdgeChanges` return base types — cast with `as AppNode[]`
- `@types/three` uses `^0.184.0` (caret) to match the installed three `^0.184.0`
- `ParseError.severity` is optional — omitted (or `'error'`) blocks code→graph sync; `'warning'` allows sync to proceed
- `tsconfig.node.json` must have `"composite": true` and `"noEmit": false` when referenced from main tsconfig
